## Part I The Coming / 第一部 来临

### I / 一

===Original===
Magister Nicholas Udal, the Lady Mary’s pedagogue, was very hungry and very cold. He stood undecided in the mud of a lane in the Austin Friars. The quickset hedges on either side were only waist high and did not shelter him. The little houses all round him of white daub with grey corner beams had been part of the old friars’ stables and offices. All that neighbourhood was a maze of dwellings and gardens, with the hedges dry, the orchard trees bare with frost, the arbours wintry and deserted. This congregation of small cottages was like a patch of common that squatters had taken; the great house of the Lord Privy Seal, who had pulled down the monastery to make room for it, was a central mass. Its gilded vanes were in the shape of men at arms, and tore the ragged clouds with the banners on their lances. Nicholas Udal looked at the roof and cursed the porter of it.

“He could have given me a cup of hypocras,” he said, and muttered, as a man to whom Latin is more familiar than the vulgar tongue, a hexameter about “pocula plena.”

He had reached London before nine in one of the King’s barges that came from Greenwich to take musicians back that night at four. He had breakfasted with the Lady Mary’s women at six off warm small beer and fresh meat, but it was eleven already, and he had spent all his money upon good letters.

===Chinese===
尼古拉斯·尤达尔先生，玛丽公主的业师，此刻又饥又冷。他站在奥古斯丁修士区一条小巷的烂泥里，去留未决。两旁的绿篱只齐腰高，遮蔽不了他。四下里那些白灰垩墙、灰色角柱的小屋，原是旧日修士院的马厩和杂用偏房。那一带整个儿是住家与园圃的迷宫，篱笆枯干，果园里的树木被霜剥得精光，凉亭一派冬日萧索，人迹全无。这一片攒聚的小屋，倒像荒地上被人擅自占垦的一角；居中一团巍然的，是御玺大臣的大宅——他为了给它腾地，把整座修士院都拆了。宅顶镀金的风向标铸成披甲武士的模样，用长矛上的旌旗撕扯着破絮般的云。尼古拉斯·尤达尔望着那屋顶，咒骂起它的门房来。

“他满可以赏我一杯希波克拉斯（hypocras）酒，”他说着，又像个拉丁文比俗语更熟的人那样，喃喃念出一句咏‘pocula plena’〔满斟之杯〕的六音步诗。

他搭国王的一艘驳船在九点前到了伦敦——那船从格林尼治来，当夜四点还要载乐师回去。六点钟他同玛丽公主的侍女们一起用过早餐，温热的淡啤酒佐鲜肉；可如今已经十一点，他的钱全都花在了正经学问上。


===Original===
He muttered: “*Pauper sum, pateor, fateor, quod Di dant fero*,” but it did not warm him.

The magister had been put in the Lady Mary’s household by the Lord Privy Seal, and he had a piece of news as to the Lady’s means of treasonable correspondence with the Emperor her uncle. He had imagined that the news﻿—which would hurt no one because it was imaginary﻿—might be worth some crowns to him. But the Lord Privy Seal and all his secretaries had gone to Greenwich before it was light, and there was nothing there for the magister.

“You might have known as much, a learned man,” the porter had snarled at him. “Isn’t the new Queen at Rochester? Would our lord bide here? Didn’t your magistership pass his barge on the river?”

“Nay, it was still dark,” the magister answered. The porter sniffed and slammed to the grating in the wicket. Being of the Old Faith he hated those Lutherans﻿—or those men of the New Learning﻿—that it pleased his master to employ.

===Chinese===
他喃喃道：“*Pauper sum, pateor, fateor, quod Di dant fero*〔我贫穷，我显露，我承认；神之所赐，我皆承受〕”，但这话暖不了他。

这位业师是御玺大臣安插进玛丽公主府中的，他手里捏着一条消息，说的是郡主同她舅父皇帝私通书信的门路。他原想着这条消息——既是凭空虚构，便伤不着任何人——或许能给他换来几个克朗。可御玺大臣带着他全体书记，天不亮就去了格林尼治，那里什么也没给业师留下。

“你本该想到这一层才是，还是个有学问的人哩，”门房曾对他咆哮。“新王后不是到罗切斯特了么？咱们大人还会待在这儿？您先生就没在河上同他的驳船交臂而过？”

“不曾，那时天还黑着呢，”业师答道。门房嗤了一声，把便门上的格栅砰地关死。他是旧教中人，恨那些路德派——或者那些新学派——只因为主人家高兴雇用他们。


===Original===
Udal hesitated before the closed door; he hesitated in the lane beyond the corner of the house. Perhaps there would be no barges at the steps﻿—no King’s barges. The men of the Earl Marshal’s service, being Papists, would pelt him with mud if he asked for a passage; even the Protestant lords’ men would jeer at him if he had no pence for them﻿—and he had none. He would do best to wait for the musicians’ barge at four.

Then he must eat and shelter﻿—and find a wench. He stood in the mud: long, thin, brown in his doctor’s gown of fur, with his black flapped cap that buttoned well under his chin and let out his brown, lean, shaven and humorous face like a woodpecker’s peering out of a hole in a tree.

The volumes beneath his arms were heavy: they poked out his gown on each side, and the bitter cold pinched his finger ends as if they had been caught in a door. The weight of the books pleased him for there was much good letters there﻿—a book of Tully’s epistles for himself and two volumes of Plautus’ comedies for the Lady Mary. But what among his day’s purchases pleased him most was a medallion in silver he had bought in Cheapside. It showed on the one side Cupid in his sleep and on the other Venus fondling a peacock. It was a heart-compelling gift to any wench or lady of degree.

===Chinese===
尤达尔在紧闭的门前踌躇；他又在宅子拐角外的小巷里踌躇。码头台阶边兴许一条驳船也没有——国王的驳船是没有的了。王室典礼官府里的人都是教皇派，他去讨个同舟，人家要拿烂泥砸他；便是新教贵族家的仆从，他若没有便士赏钱，也要受嗤笑——而他一个子儿也没有。他顶好还是等四点钟载乐师的驳船。

那么他总得吃上一点、寻个遮蔽——还得找个娘们。他站在烂泥里：瘦长条个子，裹着毛皮衬里的博士袍，黑帽的护耳严严实实扣到下巴底下，露出那张棕色、精瘦、剃得干净而带诙谐的脸，活像一只从树洞里探头张望的啄木鸟。

他腋下的书卷沉甸甸的，把袍子两边顶得鼓起来；酷寒夹得他指尖生疼，仿佛叫门夹了似的。书的分量却叫他欢喜，因为这里面正经学问多着呢——一卷图利（Tully）的书信集给他自己，两册普劳图斯（Plautus）的喜剧给玛丽公主。但他这一天买的东西里，最得意的还是在奇普赛德（Cheapside）买下的一枚银质小徽章：一面刻着酣睡的丘比特，另一面刻着爱抚孔雀的维纳斯。无论送哪个娘们或有身份的贵妇，这都是一件夺人心魄的赠礼。


===Original===
He puckered up his deprecatory and comical lips as he imagined that that medal would purchase him the right to sigh dolorously in front of whatever stomacher it finally adorned. He could pour out odes in the learned tongue, for the space of a week, a day, or an afternoon according to the rank, the kindness or the patience of the recipient.

Something invisible and harsh touched his cheek. It might have been snow or hail. He turned his thin cunning face to the clouds, and they threatened a downpour. They raced along, like scarves of vapour, so low that you might have thought of touching them if you stood on tiptoe.

If he went to Westminster Hall to find Judge Combers, he would get his belly well filled, but his back wet to the bone. At the corner of the next hedge was the wicket gate of old Master Grocer Badge. There the magister would find at least a piece of bread, some salt and warmed mead. Judge Combers’ wife was easy and bounteous: but old John Badge’s daughter was a fair and dainty morsel.

He licked his full lips, leered to one side, muttered, “A curse on all lords’ porters,” and made for John Badge’s wicket. Badge’s dwelling had been part of the monastery’s curing house. It had some good rooms and two low storeys﻿—but the tall garden wall of the Lord Privy Seal had been built against its side windows. It had been done without word or warning. Suddenly workmen had pulled down old Badge’s pigeon house, set it up twenty yards further in, marked out a line and set up this high wall that pressed so hard against the house end that there was barely room for a man to squeeze between. The wall ran for half a mile, and had swallowed the ground of twenty small householders. But never a word of complaint had reached the ears of the Privy Seal other than through his spies. It was, however, old Badge’s ceaseless grief. He had talked of it without interlude for two years.

===Chinese===
他抿起那副赔笑讨好的滑稽嘴唇，想象这枚徽章终将缀上谁的胸衣，便替自己挣得了在它面前凄然长叹的权利。他还能用雅言倾出一篇篇颂歌，或一星期，或一天，或一个下午，全看领受的人身份高低、心地厚薄、耐性长短。

一样看不见的粗砺东西拂过他的面颊。许是雪，许是雹子。他转过那张精瘦狡黠的脸去看云，云正摆出一场倾盆大雨的架势。它们像一条条雾纱疾驰而过，压得那么低，人若踮起脚尖，简直要想着能触到它们。

他若去威斯敏斯特厅投孔勃斯法官（Judge Combers），肚子管保填得满满，脊背却要湿透到骨。下一道篱笆的拐角，便是老杂货商巴杰师傅家的便门。在那儿，业师至少能讨到一块面包、一点盐、一盏温热的蜂蜜酒。孔勃斯法官的太太随和又出手大方；可老约翰·巴杰家的闺女，才是一口细嫩可人的甜点心。

他舔了舔丰厚的嘴唇，斜眼溜了一遭，咕哝道：“愿灾殃降落到大大小小的门房头上，”便朝约翰·巴杰的便门走去。巴杰的住屋原是修士院腌腊坊的一部分，有几间像样的屋子，上下两层低矮楼层——可御玺大臣那道高高的园墙，偏偏贴着他侧面的窗户砌了起来。这事做得不声不响，连个招呼也不打。忽一日，工匠们拆了老巴杰的鸽舍，往里挪了二十码重新立起，划了一条线，便砌起这道高墙，墙贴着宅尾贴得那么紧，只容一个人侧身挤过。这道墙绵延半英里，吞掉了二十户小户人家的地皮。可是除了奸细的耳报，从无一句怨言送到御玺大臣耳中。然而这是老巴杰心头永不干涸的创痛。他为这事絮絮叨叨，两年没有间歇过。


---

===Original===
The Badges’ room﻿—their houseplace﻿—was fair sized, but so low ceiled that it appeared long, dark and mysterious in the winter light There was a tall press of dark wood with a face minutely carved and fretted to represent the portal of Amiens Cathedral, and a long black table, littered with large sheets of printed matter in heavy black type, that diffused into the cold room a faint smell of ink. The old man sat quavering in the ingle. The light of the low fire glimmered on his silver hair, on his black square cap two generations old; and, in his old eyes that had seen three generations of changes, it twinkled starrily as if they were spinning round. In the cock forward of his shaven chin, and the settling down of his head into his shoulders, there was a suggestion of sinister and sardonic malice. He was muttering at his son:

“A stiff neck that knows no bending, God shall break one day.”

His son, square, dark, with his sleeves rolled up showing immense muscles developed at the levers of his presses, bent his black beard and frowned his heavy brows above his printings.

“Doubtless God shall break His engine when its work is done,” he muttered.

===Chinese===
巴杰家的堂屋——他们一家人起居的正屋——地方还算宽绰，只是顶棚太低，在冬日天光里显得狭长、幽暗而神秘。有一座深色木料的高柜，柜面细雕精镂，做出亚眠大教堂（Amiens Cathedral）门扉的样式；一张长长的黑桌，摊满一叠叠粗黑字体的印张，向寒冷的屋子里散发着一缕淡淡的油墨气味。老人坐在炉火角落里瑟缩着。低低的火光照亮他银白的头发，照亮他那顶传了两代的黑色方帽；在他那双阅尽三世变迁的老眼里，火光星星点点地闪烁，仿佛眼珠正在打转。他剃光的下巴向前一探，脑袋往肩膀里一缩，那模样隐隐透出一股阴鸷讥诮的恶意。他正冲着他儿子嘀咕：

“一副硬脖子，只知强撑不知弯，总有一日上帝要把它折断。”

他的儿子生得方正黝黑，卷着袖子，露出在印刷机压杆上千锤百炼出的一身虬结筋肉，垂着黑须，浓眉紧锁，俯在他的印件上。

“等这器械的工做完了，上帝自然拆毁它，”他低声道。


===Original===
“You call Privy Seal God’s engine?” the old man quavered ironically. “Thomas Cromwell is a brewer’s drunken son. I know them that have seen him in the stocks at Putney not thirty years ago.”

The printer set two proofs side by side on the table and frowningly compared them, shaking his head.

“He is the flail of the monks,” he said abstractedly. “They would have burned me and thousands more but for him.”

“Aye, and he has put up a fine wall where my arbour stood.”

===Chinese===
“你倒管御玺大臣叫上帝的器械？”老人颤巍巍地讥诮道。“托马斯·克伦威尔不过是酒坊老板的醉鬼儿子。我认得亲眼见过不到三十年前他在帕特尼（Putney）戴枷示众的人。”

印刷匠把两张校样并排摆上桌，皱着眉比对，摇着头。

“他是笞打修士的连枷，”他心不在焉地说。“若不是有他，他们早把我连同几千号人烧成灰了。”

“是了，他还在我凉亭的地界上砌起一道好墙哩。”


===Original===
The printer took a chalk from behind his ear and made a score down his page.

“A wall,” he muttered; “my Lord Privy Seal hath set up a wall against priestcraft all round these kingdoms﻿—”

“Therefore you would have him welcome to forty feet of my garden?” the old man drawled. “He pulls down other folks’ crucifixes and sets up his own walls with other folks’ blood for mortar.”

The printer said darkly:

===Chinese===
印刷匠从耳后取下一截粉笔，顺着页面划了一道线。

“一道墙，”他咕哝道；“咱们的御玺大臣在大大小小这些王国四境，砌下了抵御教士权术的墙——”

“所以就请他来受用我园子里那四十尺地了？”老人慢悠悠地说。“他拆人家的十字架，却拿人家的血当灰浆，砌他自家的墙。”

印刷匠阴沉沉地说：


===Original===
“Papists’ blood.”

The old man pulled his nose and glanced down.

“We were all Papists in my day. I have made the pilgrimage to Compostella, for all you mock me now.”

He turned his head to see Magister Udal entering the door furtively and with eyes that leered round the room. Both the Badges fell into sudden, and as if guilty, silence.

===Chinese===
“教皇派的血。”

老人捻了捻鼻子，垂下眼去。

“我年轻那会儿，人人都是教皇派。我还去孔波斯特拉（Compostella）朝过圣哩，任凭你们如今怎么笑话我。”

他转过头，看见尤达尔先生蹑手蹑脚跨进门来，一双眼睛滴溜溜扫视全屋。巴杰爷俩霎时陷入沉默，仿佛做贼心虚一般。


===Original===
“*Domus parva, quies magna*,” the magister tittered, and swept across the rushes in his furs to rub his hands before the fire. “When shall I teach your Margot the learned tongues?”

“When the sun sets in the East,” the printer muttered.

Udal sent to him over his shoulder, as words of consolation:

“The new Queen is come to Rochester.”

===Chinese===
“*Domus parva, quies magna*〔屋宇虽小，安宁实大〕，”业师吃吃笑道，一身裘皮扫过地上的灯芯草，凑到火前搓起手来。“我几时才能教教你们的玛戈雅言啊？”

“等太阳打东边落下去的时候吧，”印刷匠咕哝道。

尤达尔把话从肩膀上方抛过去，权当宽慰：

“新王后已经到罗切斯特了。”


===Original===
The printer heaved an immense sigh:

“God be praised!”

Udal snickered, still over his shoulder:

“You see, neither have the men of the Old Faith put venom in her food, nor have the Emperor’s galleys taken her between Calais and Sandwich.”

===Chinese===
印刷匠长叹一声：

“赞美上帝！”

尤达尔仍旧肩头一偏，嗤笑着抛过话来：

“你瞧，旧教的人既没往她的吃食里下毒，皇帝的战船也没能在加来（Calais）与桑威奇（Sandwich）之间把她劫走。”


===Original===
“Yet she comes ten days late.”

“Oh moody and suspicious artificer. *Afflavit deus!* The wind hath blown dead against Calais shore this ten days.”

The old man pulled his long white nose:

“In my day we could pray to St. Leonard for a fair wind.”

===Chinese===
“可她还是迟到了十天。”

“唉，你这阴郁多疑的匠人。*Afflavit deus*〔上帝吹了一口气〕！这十天来，风一直对着加来的海岸死吹。”

老人捻了捻他那长白鼻子：

“在我那时候，还能向圣伦纳德（St. Leonard）祷告求顺风哩。”


===Original===
He was too old to care whether the magister reported his words to Thomas Cromwell, the terrible Lord Privy Seal, and too sardonic to keep silence for long about the inferiority of his present day.

“When shall I teach the fair Margot the learned tongue?” Udal asked again.

“When wolves teach conies how to play on pipes,” the master printer snarled from his chest.

“The Lord Privy Seal never stood higher,” Udal said. “The match with the Cleves Lady hath gained him great honour.”

===Chinese===
他老得不在乎业师会不会把他的话报给可怖的御玺大臣托马斯·克伦威尔了，也刻薄得憋不住长久不说今世如何今不如昔。

“我几时才能教教美丽的玛戈雅言呢？”尤达尔又问。

“等狼教兔子吹笛子的时候吧，”印刷师傅从胸腔里咆哮出这一句。

“御玺大臣的权势从来没有像今日这般炽盛，”尤达尔说。“同克利维斯家小姐的联姻替他挣来了大大的体面。”


===Original===
“God cement it!” the printer said fervently.

The old man pulled at his nose and gazed at nothing.

“I am tired with this chatter of the woman from Cleves,” he croaked, like a malevolent raven. “An Anne she is, and a Lutheran. I mind we had an Anne and a Lutheran for Queen before. She played the whore and lost her head.”

“Where’s your niece Margot?” Udal asked the printer.

===Chinese===
“愿上帝把这桩婚事粘得牢牢的！”印刷师傅热诚地说。

老人捻着鼻子，望着虚空出神。

“克利维斯那个女人的闲话，我听也听腻了，”他哑着嗓子说，像一只怀着歹意的乌鸦。“她是个安妮，又是个路德派。我记得先前咱们也立过一个安妮、一个路德派当王后。她做了婊子，丢了脑袋。”

“你侄女玛戈呢？”尤达尔问印刷匠。


===Original===
“You owe me nine crowns,” the old man said.

“I will give your Margot ten crowns’ worth of lessons in Latin.”

“Hold and enough,” the printer muttered heavily. “Tags from Seneca in a wench’s mouth are rose garlands on a cow’s horns.”

“The best ladies in the land learn of me,” Udal answered.

===Chinese===
“你欠我九个克朗，”老人说。

“我给你家玛戈十个克朗的拉丁文课。”

“够了，休再说了，”印刷匠沉甸甸地咕哝。“叫娘们满嘴塞内加（Seneca）的文句，好比往牛角上挂玫瑰花环。”

“全国最好的贵妇都在从我求学，”尤达尔回答。


===Original===
“Aye, but my niece shall keep her virtue intact.”

“You defame the Lady Mary of England,” Udal snickered.

The old man said vigorously, “God save her highness, and send us her for Queen. Have you begged her to get me redress in the matter of that wall?”

“Why, Providence was kind to her when it sent her me for her master,” Udal said. “I never had apter pupil saving only one.”

===Chinese===
“是么，可我侄女得守身如玉。”

“你这是诽谤英格兰的玛丽公主了，”尤达尔嗤笑道。

老人斩钉截铁地说：“上帝保佑她殿下，愿上帝叫她做我们的王后。你可求过她为我那道墙的事讨个公道？”

“嘿，天意待她不薄，把我派去当她的业师，”尤达尔说。“比她更聪敏的学生，我生平只教过一个。”


===Original===
“Shall Thomas Cromwell redress?” the old man asked.

“If good learning can make a good queen, trust me to render her one,” Udal avoided the question. “But alas! being declared bastard﻿—for very excellent reasons﻿—she may not﻿—”

“You owe me nine crowns,” old Badge threatened him. He picked irritably at the fur on his gown and gazed at the carved leg of the table. “If you will not induce Privy Seal to pull down his wall I will set the tipstaves on you.”

Master Udal laughed. “I will give thy daughter ten crowns’ worth of lessons in the learned tongues.”

===Chinese===
“托马斯·克伦威尔肯讨这个公道么？”老人问。

“若说好学问能造出一位好王后，那便信我，我保管调教得出，”尤达尔避而不答。“只可惜！她既被裁定为私生——理由是再好不过的——她便不能——”

“你欠我九个克朗，”老巴杰威胁他。他烦躁地揪着袍子上的毛皮，眼睛盯着桌子的雕花腿。“你要是劝不动御玺大臣拆他的墙，我就叫法警上门拿你。”

尤达尔先生笑了。“我给你家闺女十个克朗的雅言课。”


===Original===
“You will receive another broken crown, magister,” the younger John said moodily. “Have you not scars enow by your wenching?”

Udal pushed back the furs at his collar. “Master Printer John Badge the Younger,” he flickered, “if you break my crown I will break your chapel. You shall never have license to print another libel. Give me your niece in wedlock?”

The old man said querulously, “Here’s a wantipole without ten crowns would marry a wench with three beds and seven hundred florins!”

Udal laughed. “Call her to bring me meat and drink,” he said. “Large words ill fill an empty stomach.”

===Chinese===
“那你领到的就该是又一个开花脑袋了，先生，”小约翰阴沉沉地说。“你寻花问柳，头上的疤还不够多么？”

尤达尔翻开领口的毛皮。“小约翰·巴杰印刷师傅，”他眉梢一挑，“你若打破我的头，我就端了你的印所。你休想再领到执照印一本诽谤小册子。把你侄女嫁给我如何？”

老人哼哼唧唧地说：“瞧这穷措大，十个克朗都拿不出，倒想聘一个带三张床、七百佛洛林（florins）的闺女！”

尤达尔笑了。“叫她来给我拿酒拿肉吧，”他说。“大话填不饱空肚子。”


===Original===
The younger John went negligently to the great Flemish press. He opened the face and revealed on its dark shelves a patty of cold fish and a black jack. With heavy movements and a solemn face he moved these things, with a knife and napkins, on to the broad black table.

The old man pulled his nose again and grinned.

“Margot’s in her chamber,” he chuckled. “As you came up the wicket way I sent my John to turn the key upon her. It’s there at his girdle.” It clinked indeed among rules, T-squares and callipers at each footstep of the heavy printer between press and table.

Magister Udal stretched his thin hands towards it. “I will give you the printing of the Lady Mary’s commentary of Plautus for that key,” he said.

===Chinese===
小约翰慢条斯理地踱到那台佛兰德大印刷机前。他打开机身，在幽暗的架格里露出一块冷鱼肉馅饼和一只黑皮酒壶。他动作滞重、面色肃穆，把这些东西连同刀和餐巾，一并摆上宽大的黑桌。

老人又捻了捻鼻子，咧嘴一笑。

“玛戈在她屋里呢，”他呵呵笑道。“你打便门道上来的时候，我就叫我约翰拿钥匙把她反锁了。钥匙就在他腰上。”那钥匙当真在直尺、丁字尺和卡钳中间叮当作响，应和着壮实的印刷匠在印机与长桌之间的每一步。

尤达尔先生朝那把钥匙伸出瘦手。“拿这把钥匙换，我让你们印玛丽公主的普劳图斯评注本，”他说。


===Original===
The printer murmured “Eat,” and set a great pewter saltcellar, carved like a Flemish pikeman, a foot high, heavily upon the cloth.

Udal had the appetite of a wolf. He pulled off his cap the better to let his jaws work.

“Here’s a letter from the Doctor Wernken of Augsburg,” he said. “You may see how the Lutherans fare in Germany.”

The printer took the letter and read it, standing, frowning and heavy. Magister Udal ate; the old man fingered his furs and, leaning far back in his mended chair, gazed at nothing.

===Chinese===
印刷匠低声道了句“吃吧”，把一座大锡盐盒沉甸甸放上桌布——盐盒雕成佛兰德长矛手的模样，足有一尺来高。

尤达尔生着狼一样的胃口。他摘掉帽子，好让两颚运转自如。

“这儿有封奥格斯堡（Augsburg）的韦恩肯博士（Doctor Wernken）写来的信，”他说。“你可以看看路德派在德意志的境况。”

印刷匠接过信，站着读，眉头紧锁，神色沉重。尤达尔先生吃着；老人抚弄他的毛皮，往后深深靠进那把修补过的椅子里，望着虚空出神。


===Original===
“Let me have the maid in wedlock,” Udal grunted between two bites. “Better women have looked favourably upon me. I had a pupil in the North﻿—”

“She was a Howard, and the Howards are all whores,” the printer said, over the letter. “Your Doctor Wernken writes like an Anabaptist.”

“They are even as the rest of womenkind,” Udal laughed, “but far quicker with their learning.”

A boy rising twenty, in a grey cloak that showed only his bright red stockings and broad-toed red shoes, rattled the back door and slammed it to. He pulled off his cap and shook it.

===Chinese===
“把这丫头嫁了我吧，”尤达尔在两口吃食之间咕哝。“比她体面的女人还青眼相待过我哩。我在北边曾有过一个学生——”

“她是个霍华德家的，霍华德家的女人全是婊子，”印刷匠眼睛不离信纸，说道。“你那位韦恩肯博士写信的做派，倒像个再洗礼派（Anabaptist）。”

“她们同旁的女人家一个样，”尤达尔笑道，“只是学东西利索得多。”

一个年将二十的小伙子，灰斗篷底下只露出鲜红的长袜和宽头红鞋，把后门摇得哗啦响，又砰地甩上。他摘下帽子抖了抖。


===Original===
“It snows,” he said buoyantly, and then knelt before his grandfather. The old man touched his grandson’s cropped fair head.

“*Benedicite*, grandson Hal Poins,” he muttered, and relapsed into his gaze at the fire.

The young man bent his knee to his uncle and bowed low to the magister. Being about the court, he had for Udal’s learning and office a reverence that neither the printer nor his grandfather could share. He unfastened his grey cloak at the neck and cast it into a corner after his hat. His figure flashed out, lithe, young, a blaze of scarlet with a crowned rose embroidered upon a chest rendered enormous by much wadding. He was serving his apprenticeship as ensign in the gentlemen of the King’s guard, and because his dead father had been beloved by the Duke of Norfolk it was said that his full ensigncy was near. He begged his grandfather’s leave to come near the fire, and stood with his legs apart.

“The new Queen’s come to Rochester,” he said; “I am here with the guard to take the heralds to Greenwich Palace.”

===Chinese===
“下雪了，”他喜洋洋地说，随即在祖父面前跪下。老人抚了抚孙子剪短的浅色头发。

“*Benedicite*〔请降福〕，孙子哈尔·普因斯（Hal Poins），”他喃喃道，又回过神去凝望炉火。

年轻人向他舅舅屈膝，又向业师深深一躬。他在宫廷当差，对尤达尔的学问和职分怀着敬意，那是印刷匠和他祖父都无法分享的。他解开颈间的灰斗篷，跟帽子一起甩进墙角。他的身形倏地亮出来：矫健，年轻，一身炫目的猩红，衬着厚垫而显得格外阔大的前胸上，绣着一朵加冠的玫瑰。他正在国王卫队的绅士卫兵里见习掌旗；他亡父生前深得诺福克公爵喜爱，人人都说他补上整份掌旗官缺的日子近了。他求得祖父许他凑近炉火，便叉开腿站定了。

“新王后到罗切斯特了，”他说；“我随卫队来，要护送纹章官们去格林尼治王宫。”


===Original===
The printer looked at him unfavourably from the corner of his dark and gloomy eyes.

“You come to suck up more money,” he said moodily. “There is none in this house.”

“As Mary is my protectress!” the boy laughed, “there is!” He stuck his hands into his breeches pocket and pulled out a big fistful of crowns that he had won overnight at dice, and a long and thin Flemish chain of gold. “I have enow to last me till the thaw,” he said. “I came to beg my grandfather’s blessing on the first day of the year.”

“Dicing﻿ ﻿… Wenching﻿ ﻿…” the printer muttered.

===Chinese===
印刷匠从阴郁的黑眼睛眼角里，不以为然地打量他。

“你是来吮吸更多钱的，”他闷闷地说。“这家里一个子儿也没有。”

“玛丽就是我的主保圣人！”小伙子笑道，“有的是！”他把手插进马裤口袋，掏出一大把隔夜掷骰子赢来的克朗，还有一条细长的佛兰德金链。“我的钱够花到开春化冻，”他说。“我来是求祖父在新年头一天赐福给我。”

“掷骰子……逛娘们……”印刷匠咕哝道。


===Original===
“If I ask thee for no blessing,” the young man said, “it’s because, uncle, thou’rt a Lutheran that can convey none. Where’s Margot? This chain’s for her.”

“The fair Margot’s locked in her chamber,” Udal snickered.

“Why-som-ever then? Hath she stolen a tart?”

“Nay, but I would have her in wedlock.”

===Chinese===
“我若不向您求福，”年轻人说，“舅舅，那是因为您是个路德派，传达不了福佑。玛戈呢？这条链子是给她的。”

“美丽的玛戈锁在她屋里呢，”尤达尔嗤笑道。

“这却是为什么？她偷果馅饼吃了？”

“不是，是我要娶她为妻。”


===Original===
“Thou﻿—you﻿—your magistership?” the boy laughed incredulously. The printer caught in his tone his courtier’s contempt for the artificer’s home, and his courtier’s reverence for the magister’s learning.

“Keep thy sister from beneath this fox’s tooth,” he said. “The likes of him mate not with the like of us.”

“The like of thee, uncle?” the boy retorted, with a good-humoured insolence. “My father was a gentleman.”

“Who married my sister for her small money, and died leaving thee and thy sister to starve.”

===Chinese===
“您——您——先生您？”小伙子难以置信地笑起来。印刷匠从他的语气里听出了两样东西：朝臣对手艺人家庭的轻蔑，和朝臣对业师学问的敬重。

“别让你妹妹落进这只狐狸的牙口，”他说。“他那样的人，跟咱们这样的人家结不了亲。”

“咱们这样的人家，舅舅？”小伙子回嘴，放肆里带着好脾气。“我父亲可是位绅士。”

“一位图我妹妹那点微薄嫁妆成婚的绅士，死了撇下你和你妹妹挨饿。”


===Original===
“Nay, I starve not,” the boy said. “And Margot’s a plump faggot.”

“A very Cynthia among willow-trees,” the magister said.

“Why, your magistership shall have her,” the boy said. “I am her lawful guardian.”

His grandfather laughed as men laugh to see a colt kick up its heels in a meadow.

===Chinese===
“哼，我可没挨饿，”小伙子说。“玛戈是根壮实的柴火妞。”

“真是柳树丛里的一位辛西娅（Cynthia），”业师说。

“嘿，先生您要娶就娶吧，”小伙子说。“我是她的合法监护人。”

他祖父笑起来，像人看见小马驹在草场上撒欢踢蹄那样笑。


===Original===
But the printer waved his bare arm furiously at the magister.

“Get thee gone out of this decent house.” His eyes rolled, and his clenched fist was as large as a ham. “Here you come not a-wenching.”

“Moody man,” the magister said, “your brains are addled with suspicions.”

The young man swelled his scarlet breast still more consequentially. “This is no house of thine, uncle, but my grandfar’s.”

===Chinese===
可印刷匠朝业师愤怒地挥动起赤裸的胳膊。

“滚出这个体面人家。”他眼珠乱转，攥紧的拳头有火腿那么大。“这地方不许你来沾腥。”

“阴郁的人啊，”业师说，“你的脑子叫疑心搅成一锅粥了。”

年轻人愈发煞有介事地挺起他猩红的胸脯。“这可不是您的宅子，舅舅，是我祖父的。”


===Original===
“Young ass’s colt!” the printer fulminated. “Would’st have thy sister undone by this Latin mouth-mincer?”

Udal grinned at him, and licked his lips. The printer snarled:

“Know’st thou not, young ass, that this man was thrown out of his mastership at Eton for his foul living?”

Udal was suddenly on his feet with the long pasty-knife held back among the furs of his gown.

===Chinese===
“小驴驹子！”印刷匠雷霆大怒。“你要让你妹妹毁在这张耍拉丁嘴皮子的嘴里？”

尤达尔朝他咧嘴一笑，舔了舔嘴唇。印刷匠咆哮道：

“你不知道么，小蠢驴，这人当年就是因为在伊顿（Eton）品行肮脏，才被撵出教席的？”

尤达尔霍地站起身，长长的馅饼刀藏进他袍子的毛皮后面。


===Original===
“Ignoble﻿ ﻿…” he began, but he lost his words in his trembling rage. The printer snatched at his long measuring stick.

“Down knife,” he grunted, for his fury, too, made his throat catch.

“Have a care, nunkey,” the young man laughed at the pair of them. “They teach knife-thrusts in his Italian books.”

“I will have thy printer’s licence revoked, ignoble man,” the magister said, grinning hideously. “Thou, a Lutheran, to turn upon me who was undone by Papist lies! They said I lived foully; they said I stole the silver cellars.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“卑贱的……”他刚开口，狂怒得声音发颤，话都说不成句。印刷匠一把抓过他的长量尺。

“放下刀，”他闷喝一声，他的怒火也噎住了喉咙。

“留神呐，好舅舅，”年轻人冲着他俩笑道。“他那意大利文书里，可是教人使刀子的。”

“我要叫人吊销你的印刷执照，卑贱的人，”业师说，笑容狰狞。“你，一个路德派，竟敢反咬我这被教皇派谎言毁掉的人！他们说我活得肮脏；说我偷了银窖。……”


===Original===
He turned upon the old man, stretching out the hand that held the knife in a passionate gesture:

“Your Papists said that,” he appealed. “But not a one of them believed it, though you dub me Lutheran.﻿ ﻿… See you, do I not govern now the chief Papist of you all? Would that be if they believed me filthy in my living. Have I not governed in the house of the Howards, the lord of it being absent? Would that have been if they had believed it of me?﻿ ﻿… And then.﻿ ﻿…” He turned again upon the printer. “For the sake of your men﻿ ﻿… for the sake of the New Learning, which God prosper, I was cast down.”

The printer grunted surlily:

“ ’Tis known no wench is safe from thy amorousness. How many husbands have broken thy pate?”

===Chinese===
他转向老人，握刀的手激动地直伸出去：

“是你们教皇派说的那些话，”他申诉道。“可他们中间没有一个人信，尽管你们管我叫路德派。……你们瞧，我如今不正在管教着你们大伙儿里最铁杆的教皇派么？他们若真信我生活污秽，还能这样么？霍华德府上老爷不在的时候，不也是我在当家么？他们若信了那些话，还能这样么？……再说。……”他又转向印刷匠。“为了你们这些人……为了新学——愿上帝使它昌隆——我才栽了这跟头。”

印刷匠粗声粗气地哼道：

“谁人不知，哪个娘们也逃不过你的淫性。有多少做丈夫的打破过你的脑袋？”


===Original===
The magister threw the knife on to the table and rose, frostily rustling in his gown.

“I shall bring thee down, ignoble man,” he said.

“If thou hast the power to do that,” the old man asked suddenly, “wherefore canst not get me redress in the matter of my wall?”

The magister answered angrily:

===Chinese===
业师把刀摔在桌上，站起身，袍子窸窣作响，寒气凛凛。

“我要把你扳倒，卑贱的人，”他说。

“你若真有那本事，”老人突然发问，“怎么就不能为我那道墙的事讨个公道？”

业师愤然答道：


===Original===
“Privy Seal hath swallowed thy land: he shall not disgorge. But this man he shall swallow. Know you not that you may make a jack swallow, but no man shall make him give back; I, nor thou, nor the devil’s self?”

“Oh, a God’s name bring not Flail Crummock into this household,” the young man cut in. “Would you undo us all?”

“Ignoble, ignoble, to twit a man with that Eton villainy,” the magister answered.

“A God’s name bring not Privy Seal into the quarrel,” the young man repeated. “None of us of the Old Faith believe that lie.”

===Chinese===
“御玺大臣吞了你的地：他不会吐出来。可这个人，他吞得下。你岂不知，皮酒壶只管灌它吞，谁也没法叫它吐出来？我不成，你不成，魔鬼他亲自来也不成。”

“哦，千万看在上帝份上，别把连枷克鲁默克（Flail Crummock）招进这个家门来，”年轻人插嘴道。“你们要害死我们全家么？”

“卑贱，卑贱，拿伊顿那桩无赖行径戳人的心窝，”业师答道。

“看在上帝份上，别把御玺大臣卷进这场口角，”年轻人又说了一遍。“我们旧教的人，没一个信那个谎。”


===Original===
“Keep thy tongue off Cromwell’s name, young fool,” his grandfather said. “We know not what walls have ears.”

The young man went pale: the printer himself went pale, remembering suddenly that the magister was a spy of Cromwell’s; all three of them had their eyes upon Udal; only the old man, with his carelessness of his great age, grinned with curiosity as if the matter were a play that did not concern him. The magister was making for the door with the books beneath his arm and a torturing smile round his lips. The boy, with a deep oath, ran out after him, a scarlet flash in the darkening room.

Old Badge pulled at his nose and grinned maliciously at the fire beside him.

“That is thy deliverer: that is thy flail of the monks,” he croaked at his son. The printer gazed moodily at the fire.

===Chinese===
“嘴巴放干净些，别碰克伦威尔的名讳，小蠢货，”他祖父说。“咱们可不知道哪堵墙长着耳朵。”

年轻人脸白了：印刷匠自己也白了脸，猛然想起这位业师是克伦威尔的密探；三个人六只眼睛都钉在尤达尔身上；只有老人，仗着高寿万事不惧，好奇地咧着嘴笑，仿佛这事是一出与他无干的戏。业师正朝门口走去，腋下夹着书，唇边挂着一丝折磨人的浅笑。小伙子狠狠咒骂了一声，追出门去，像一道猩红的闪电掠过渐暗的屋子。

老巴杰捻着鼻子，对着身旁的炉火恶狠狠地咧嘴而笑。

“这就是你的救主：这就是你那把笞打修士的连枷，”他哑着嗓子对儿子说。印刷匠郁郁地凝视炉火。


===Original===
“Nay, it is but one of his servants,” he answered mechanically.

“And such servants go up and down this realm of England and ride us with iron bridles.” The old man laughed dryly and bitterly. “His servant? See how we are held﻿—we dare not shut our doors upon him since he is Cromwell’s servant, yet if he come in he shall ruin us, take our money that we dare not refuse, deflower our virgins.﻿ ﻿… What then is left to us between this setter up of walls and his servants?”

The printer, fingering the T-square in his belt, said, slowly, “I think this man loves too well that books should be printed in the Latin tongue to ruin any printer of them upon a private quarrel. Else I would get me across the seas.”

“He loves any wench much better,” the old man answered maliciously. “Hearken!”

===Chinese===
“不，这不过是他的一个仆役，”他机械地答道。

“这样的仆役，正在这英格兰国里四处横行，拿铁辔头骑在我们头上。”老人干涩而苦涩地笑了。“他的仆役？瞧咱们被拿捏成了什么样——他是克伦威尔的仆役，咱们不敢对他闭门不纳；可他一旦进来，就要毁了我们，拿走我们不敢不给的钱，糟蹋我们的闺女。……在这砌墙的和他的仆役们之间，还给我们剩下了什么？”

印刷匠摩挲着腰间的丁字尺，缓缓说道：“我想这个人太巴望有用拉丁文印的书，不至于为一场私怨，去毁掉哪个印这种书的匠人。不然我早渡海走了。”

“他巴望得更欢的是随便哪个娘们，”老人恶意地应道。“且听！”


===Original===
Through the wall there came a scuffling sound, thumps, and the noise of things falling. The wall there touched on the one that Cromwell had set up, so that there was bare room for a man to creep between.

“Body of God,” the printer said, “is he eavesdropping now?”

“Nay, this is courtship,” the old man answered. His head leaned forward with a birdlike intentness; he listened with one hand held out as if to still any sound in the room. They heard footsteps from the floor above, a laugh and voices. “Now Margot talks to him from her window.”

The printer had a motion of convulsed rage:

===Chinese===
墙那边传来一阵厮扭声、捶击声和东西倒落的声响。那边的墙正贴着克伦威尔砌起的这道墙，中间只留下容一个人爬过的空当。

“上帝的身体啊，”印刷匠说，“他这会儿是在偷听么？”

“不，这是求欢呢，”老人答道。他的头带着鸟儿般的专注向前探出；他伸出一只手听着，仿佛要按住屋里的一切声息。他们听见头顶那层楼传来脚步声、一声笑和人语。“这会儿玛戈在窗口同他说话呢。”

印刷匠做出一个怒得抽搐的动作：


===Original===
“I will break that knave’s spine across my knee.”

“Nay, let be,” the old man said. “I command thee, who am thy father, to let the matter be.”

“Would you have him﻿ ﻿…” the printer began with a snarl.

“I would not have my house burnt down because this Cromwell’s spy’s body should be found upon our hands.﻿ ﻿… Tomorrow the wench shall be sent to her aunt Wardle in Bedfordshire﻿—aye, and she shall be soundly beaten to teach her to love virtue.”

===Chinese===
“我要在那恶棍的脊梁上，拿膝盖把它折断。”

“别，由他去，”老人说。“我以你父亲的身分命令你，这事由它去。”

“你要让他……”印刷匠咆哮着开了腔。

“我不愿为了克伦威尔这个奸细的尸首落在我们手上，叫我这座房子给人烧了。……明天就送那丫头去贝德福德郡（Bedfordshire）她沃德尔（Wardle）姨母家——是了，还要结结实实打她一顿，教她学会爱惜贞节。”


===Original===
The young man opened the house door and came in, shivering in his scarlet because he had run out without his cloak.

“A pretty medley you have made,” he said to his uncle, “but I have calmed him. Wherefore should not this magister marry Margot?” He made again for the fire. “Are we to smell always of ink?” He looked disdainfully at his uncle’s proofs, and began to speak with a boy’s seriousness and ingenuous confidence. They would tell his uncle at Court that if good print be the body of a book, good learning is even the soul of it. At Court he would learn that it is thought this magister shall rise high. There good learning is much prized. Their Lord the King had been seen to talk and laugh with this magister. “For our gracious lord loveth good letters. He is in such matters skilled beyond all others in the realm.”

The old man listened to his grandson, smiling maliciously and with pride; the printer shrugged his shoulders bitterly; the muffled sounds and the voices through the house-end continued, and the boy talked on, laying down the law valiantly and with a cheerful voice.﻿ ﻿… He would gain advancement at Court through his sister’s marriage with the magister.

Going back to the palace at Greenwich along with the magister, in the barge that was taking the heralds to the King’s marriage with Anne of Cleves, the young Poins was importunate with Udal to advance him in his knowledge of the Italian tongue. He thought that in the books of the Sieur Macchiavelli upon armies and the bearing of arms there were unfolded many secret passes with the rapier and the stiletto. But Udal laughed good-humouredly. He had, he said, little skill in the Italian tongue, for it was but a bastard of classical begettings. And for instruction in the books of the Sieur Macchiavelli, let young Poins go to a man who had studied them word by word﻿—to the Lord Privy Seal, Thomas Cromwell.

===Chinese===
年轻人推开屋门进来，一身猩红冻得发抖，因为他跑出去时没披斗篷。

“你们闹得可真热闹，”他对舅舅说，“不过我把他劝平静了。这位先生为什么就不能娶玛戈？”他又朝炉火凑过去。“难道我们要一辈子闻油墨味么？”他轻蔑地扫了一眼舅舅的校样，随即带着少年人的郑重和坦然的自信开了腔。宫廷里的人会告诉他舅舅：如果说精良的印刷是一本书的身体，精深的学问便是它的灵魂。到了宫廷他就会知道，人人都说这位先生前程远大。在那里，学问是极受看重的。他们的主上国王，就有人瞧见同这位先生谈笑过。“因为我们仁慈的主上雅好文墨。在这类事情上，他的造诣举国无人能出其右。”

老人听着孙子说话，又是得意又是恶意地微笑；印刷匠苦涩地耸耸肩；宅墙那头闷闷的声响和人语仍在继续，小伙子滔滔不绝，气壮声朗地发着宏论。……他要借妹妹嫁给这位先生，在宫廷里谋个进身之阶。

小普因斯同业师一道，乘着送纹章官们去国王与克利维斯的安妮婚典的驳船回格林尼治王宫；一路上他缠着尤达尔，要他传授意大利文的进益。他以为马基雅维利（Macchiavelli）先生论兵阵与武艺的书里，铺陈着长剑与短匕的诸多秘传招数。可尤达尔好脾气地笑了。他说，意大利文他懂得有限，那不过是古典正脉生出的杂种。至于讲授马基雅维利先生那些书，就让小普因斯去找一位逐字研读过的行家——御玺大臣托马斯·克伦威尔。


===Original===
They both dropped their voices at the name, and, another gentleman of the guard beginning to talk of rich men who had fallen low by the block, the stake, and gaming, Udal mentioned that that day he had seen a strange sight.

“There was in the Northern parts, where I governed in his absence the Lord Edmund Howard’s children, a certain Thomas Culpepper. Main rich he was, with many pastures and many thousands of sheep. A cousin of my lady’s he was, forever roaring about the house. A swaggerer he was, that down there went more richly dressed than earls here.”

That day Udal had seen this Culpepper alone, without any servants, dressed in uncostly green, and dragging at the bridle of a mule, on which sat a doxy dressed in ancient and ragged furs. So did men fall in these difficult days.

“How came he in London town?” the Norroy King-at-Arms asked.

===Chinese===
听到那个名字，两人都放低了声音；这时另一位卫队绅士谈起那些因断头台、火刑柱和赌局而败落的富豪，尤达尔便说起，他那天见到过一桩怪事。

“在北边，我替埃德蒙·霍华德勋爵（Lord Edmund Howard）照管儿女的那地方，有个托马斯·卡尔佩珀。他阔得很，有许多牧场、成千上万只羊。他是夫人的表亲，成天在宅子里咋咋呼呼。是个好摆阔的家伙，在那边穿得比这儿的伯爵们还体面。”

那天尤达尔瞧见这个卡尔佩珀孤零零一个人，不带仆从，穿着不值钱的绿衣裳，拽着一头骡子的缰绳；骡背上骑着个穿陈旧破烂皮裘的粉头。这艰难时世，人就是这样栽下来的。

“他到伦敦城来做什么？”诺罗伊纹章院长（Norroy King-at-Arms）问。


===Original===
“Nay, I stayed not to ask him,” Udal answered. He sighed a little. “Yet then, in my Lord Edmund’s house I had my best pupil of all, and fain was I to have news of her.﻿ ﻿… But he was a braggart; I liked him not, and would not stay to speak with him.”

“I’ll warrant you had dealings with some wench he favoured, and you feared a drubbing, magister,” Norroy accused him.

The long cabin of the state barge was ablaze with the scarlet and black of the guards, and with the gold and scarlet of the heralds. Magister Udal sighed.

“You had good, easy days in Lord Edmund’s house?” Norroy asked.

===Chinese===
“这个，我没停下来问他，”尤达尔答道。他微微叹了口气。“可那时候，在埃德蒙勋爵府上，我有过平生最好的学生，我也乐意打听她的消息。……但他是个吹牛匠；我不喜欢他，便没停步同他说话。”

“我敢保你是招惹了他相好的哪个娘们，怕挨一顿好打，先生，”诺罗伊打趣着数落他。

御舫长长的船舱里，卫队的猩红与墨黑、纹章官的金色与猩红交相辉映，亮得晃眼。尤达尔先生叹了口气。

“你在埃德蒙勋爵府上，日子过得又舒坦又自在吧？”诺罗伊问。

### II / 二

===Original===
The Lord Privy Seal was beneath a tall cresset in the stern of his barge, looking across the night and the winter river. They were rowing from Rochester to the palace at Greenwich, where the Court was awaiting Anne of Cleves. The flare of the King’s barge a quarter of a mile ahead moved in a glowing patch of lights and their reflections, as though it were some portent creeping in a blaze across the sky. There was nothing else visible in the world but the darkness and a dusky tinge of red where a wave caught the flare of light further out.

He stood invisible behind the lights of his cabin; and the thud of oars, the voluble noises of the water, and the crackling of the cresset overhead had, too, the quality of impersonal and supernatural phenomena. His voice said harshly:

“It is very cold; bring me my greatest cloak.”

Throckmorton, the one of Cromwell’s seven hundred spies who at that time was his most constant companion, was hidden in the deep shadow beside the cabin-door. His bearded and heavy form obscured the light for a moment as he hurried to fetch the cloak. But merely to be the Lord Cromwell’s gown-bearer was in those days a thing you would run after; and an old man in a flat cap﻿—the Chancellor of the Augmentations, who had been listening intently at the door﻿—was already hurrying out with a heavy cloak of fur. Cromwell let it be hung about his shoulders.

The Chancellor shivered and said, “We should be within a quarter-hour of Greenwich.”

===Chinese===
御玺大臣立在他驳船船尾一座高高的火烛架下，望着这黑夜与冬日的河。船正从罗切斯特划往格林尼治的王宫，宫廷上下都在那里等候克利维斯的安妮。前头四分之一英里外，国王驳船的火光在一团辉耀的灯影与倒影中移动，仿佛某种拖着烈焰爬过天穹的预兆。世上此外再无一物可见，只有黑暗，还有更远处一道浪头接住火光时泛起的一抹暗红。

他立在舱室灯火之后，隐而不见；桨的顿挫、水的絮聒、头顶火烛架的噼啪，也都有一种超然人事、近乎神异的气象。他的声音严苛地响起：

“天很冷；把我那件最厚的斗篷取来。”

斯罗克莫顿——克伦威尔七百名密探中彼时最常随侍左右的那一个——正藏在舱门旁深深的暗影里。他赶去取斗篷，那蓄须的魁伟身影把火光遮暗了一霎。可在那些日子里，哪怕当一当克伦威尔大人捧袍子的差，也是人人争逐的事；一位戴扁帽的老人——增益院大法官（Chancellor of the Augmentations），方才一直在门边凝神偷听——已经抱着一件厚重的裘皮斗篷抢着出来了。克伦威尔听任它被披上肩头。

大法官打了个寒噤，说：“我们该不出一刻钟就到格林尼治了。”

===Original===
“Get you in if you be cold,” Cromwell answered. But the Chancellor was quivering with the desire to talk to his master. He had seen the heavy King rush stumbling down the stairs of the Cleves woman’s lodging at Rochester, and the sight had been for him terrible and prodigious. It was Cromwell who had made him Chancellor of the Augmentations﻿—who had even invented the office to deal with the land taken from the Abbeys﻿—and he was so much the creature of this Lord Privy Seal that it seemed as if the earth was shivering all the while for the fall of this minister, and that he himself was within an inch of the ruin, execration, and death that would come for them all once Cromwell were down.

Throckmorton, a giant man with an immense golden beard, issued again from the cabin, and the Privy Seal’s voice came leisurely and cold:

“What said Lord Cassilis of this? And the fellow Knighton? I saw them at the stairs.”

Privy Seal had such eyes that it was delicate work lying to him. But Throckmorton brought out heavily:

“Cassilis, that this Lady Anne should never be Queen.”

===Chinese===
“冷就进舱去，”克伦威尔答道。可大法官浑身发颤，一心想同主人攀谈。他亲眼看见沉重的国王跌跌撞撞冲下罗切斯特那位克利维斯女人下榻处的楼梯，那一幕在他眼里可怖而惊天动地。正是克伦威尔立他做了增益院大法官——这官职本身就是克伦威尔创设出来、专办各修道院没收入官的地产的——他依附这位御玺大臣已深到这般地步，仿佛大地始终都在为这位大臣的倾覆而战栗，而克伦威尔一旦倒台，那要落到他们众人头上的覆灭、咒骂与死亡，他自己也不过一指之遥。

斯罗克莫顿——一个长着一大把金色胡须的彪形大汉——又从舱里出来了，御玺大臣的声音悠悠地、冷冷地响起：

“这件事卡西利斯勋爵（Lord Cassilis）怎么说？还有奈顿（Knighton）那家伙？我在楼梯口看见他们了。”

御玺大臣那双眼睛，叫人对他撒谎成了桩细活。可斯罗克莫顿还是沉甸甸地吐出口：

“卡西利斯说，这位安妮小姐（Lady Anne）断然当不成王后。”

===Original===
“Aye, but she must,” the Chancellor bleated. He had been bribed by two of the Cleves lords to get them lands in Kent when the Queen should be in power. Cromwell’s silence made Throckmorton continue against his will:

“Knighton, that the Queen’s breath should turn the King’s stomach against you! Dr. Miley, the Lutheran preacher, that by this evening’s work the Kingdom of God on earth was set trembling, the King having the nature of a lecher.﻿ ﻿…”

He tried to hold back. After all, it came into his mind, this man was nearly down. Any one of the men upon whom he now spied might come to be his master very soon. But Cromwell’s voice said, “And then?” and he made up his mind to implicate none but the Scotch lord, who was at once harmless and unliable to be harmed.

“Lord Cassilis,” he brought out, “said again that your lordship’s head should fall ere January goes out.”

He seemed to feel the great man’s sneer through the darkness, and was coldly angry with himself for having invented no better lie. For if this invisible and threatening phantom that hid itself among these shadows outlasted January he might yet outlast some of them. He wondered which of Cromwell’s innumerable ill-wishers it might best serve him to serve. But for the Chancellor of the Augmentations the heavy silence of calamity, like the waiting at a bedside for death to come, seemed to fall upon them. He imagined that the Privy Seal hid himself in that shadow in order to conceal a pale face and shaking knees. But Cromwell’s voice came harsh and peremptory to Throckmorton:

===Chinese===
“是，可她非当上王后不可，”大法官咩声细气地说。他受过克利维斯家两位贵族的贿，许下诺言，等王后得势便替他们弄到肯特的田地。克伦威尔的沉默逼得斯罗克莫顿违着本意往下说：

“奈顿说，王后的一口气，就能叫国王的胃口反过来冲着您！路德派宣道师米利博士（Dr. Miley）说，经今晚这一遭，神的国在地上就震颤了，因为国王生就的是个淫徒的性子……”

他想收住话头。毕竟，他心里转念，这个人已经快倒了。他如今刺探的这些人，随便哪一个，不久都可能变成他的主人。可克伦威尔的声音说：“还有呢？”他便拿定主意，只牵连那位苏格兰勋爵——此人既伤不了人，也不怕被人伤。

“卡西利斯勋爵，”他吐出来，“又说了一遍：大人您的脑袋，不等一月过完就要落地。”

隔着黑暗，他仿佛觉出那位大人物的讥诮，冷冷地恼恨自己没编出个更好些的谎来。因为，倘若这个藏在暗影里的、隐而不见的威胁之影熬过了一月，兴许也能熬倒他们中间的某几个。他盘算着，克伦威尔数不清的仇家当中，投效哪一个于他最为合算。可对增益院大法官来说，灾殃那沉重的死寂——像守在病榻边等死——仿佛已经罩了下来。他想象御玺大臣之所以藏进那片暗影里，是为了掩住一张惨白的脸和两条打颤的膝盖。但克伦威尔的声音已严厉专断地传向斯罗克莫顿：

===Original===
“What men be abroad at this night season? Ask my helmsmen.”

Two torchlights, far away to the right, wavered shaking trails in the water that, thus revealed, shewed agitated and chopped by small waves. The Chancellor’s white beard shook with the cold, with fear of Cromwell, and with curiosity to know how the man looked and felt. He ventured at last in a faint and bleating voice:

“What did his lordship think of this matter? Surely the King should espouse this lady and the Lutheran cause.”

Cromwell answered with inscrutable arrogance:

“Why, your cause is valuable. But this is a great matter. Get you in if you be cold.”

===Chinese===
“这般夜时，还有何人在外行走？去问问我的舵手们。”

右方远处两点火把光摇曳着，在水上抖出颤动的光痕；水面被这样照见，显出被小浪搅碎的动荡。大法官的白胡须抖个不住——为着冷，为着怕克伦威尔，也为着想看看此人的脸色心绪究竟怎样的好奇。他终于放胆，用微弱的、咩咩般的声音说：

“大人您对这事怎么想？国王总该既娶这位小姐、也娶路德派的大业吧。”

克伦威尔带着莫测高深的倨傲答道：

“嗐，你们那桩大业值钱得很。可这是件大事。冷就进舱去。”

===Original===
Throckmorton appeared noiselessly at his elbow, whilst the Chancellor was mumbling: “God forbid I should be called Lutheran.”

The torches, Throckmorton said, were those of fishers who caught eels off the mud with worms upon needles.

“Such night work favours treason,” Cromwell muttered. “Write in my notebook, ‘The Council to prohibit the fishing of eels by night.’ ”

“What a nose he hath for treasons,” the Chancellor whispered to Throckmorton as they rustled together into the cabin. Throckmorton’s face was gloomy and pensive. The Privy Seal had chosen none of his informations for noting down. Assuredly the time was near for him to find another master.

===Chinese===
斯罗克莫顿悄没声地出现在他肘边，那时大法官还在喃喃：“求上帝别叫人把我唤作路德派。”

斯罗克莫顿说，那火把是渔人的：他们在泥滩外，用穿了虫饵的针钓鳗鱼。

“这样的夜间营生给叛乱行方便，”克伦威尔咕哝道。“记到我的记事本上：‘枢密院应禁止夜间捕鳗。’”

“他闻叛乱的鼻子多灵啊，”两人窸窸窣窣一同进舱时，大法官低声对斯罗克莫顿说。斯罗克莫顿脸色阴沉，若有所思。御玺大臣没有挑中他任何一条密报来记录。无疑，他另寻主子的时辰近了。

===Original===
The barge swung round a reach, and the lights of the palace of Greenwich were like a flight of dim or bright squares in mid air, far ahead. The King’s barge was already illuminating the crenellated arch at the top of the river steps. A burst of torches flared out to meet it and disappeared. The Court was then at Greenwich, nearly all the lords, the bishops and the several councils lying in the Palace to await the coming of Anne of Cleves on the morrow. She had reached Rochester that evening after some days’ delay at Calais, for the winter seas. The King had gone that night to inspect her, having been given to believe that she was soberly fair and of bountiful charms. His courteous visit had been in secret and in disguise; therefore there were no torchmen in the gardens, and darkness lay between the river steps and the great central gateway. But a bonfire, erected by the guards to warm themselves in the courtyard, as it leapt up or subsided before the wind, shewed that tall tower pale and high or vanishing into the night with its carved stone garlands, its stone men at arms, its lions, roses, leopards, and naked boys. The living houses ran away from the foot of the tower, till the wings, coming towards the river, vanished continually into shadows. They were low by comparison, gabled with false fronts over each set of rooms and, in the glass of their small-paned windows, the reflection of the fire gleamed capriciously from unexpected shadows. This palace was called Placentia by the King because it was pleasant to live in.

Cromwell mounted the steps with a slow gait and an arrogant figure. Under the river arch eight of his gentlemen waited upon him, and in the garden the torches of his men shewed black yew trees cut like peacocks, clipped hedges like walls with archways above the broad and tiled paths, and fountains that gleamed and trickled as if secretly in the heavy and bitter night.

A corridor ran from under the great tower right round the palace. It was full of hurrying people and of grooms who stood in knots beside doorways. They flattened themselves against the walls before the Lord Privy Seal’s procession of gentlemen in black with white staves, and the ceilings seemed to send down moulded and gilded stalactites to touch his head. The beefeater before the door of the Lady Mary’s lodgings spat upon the ground when he had passed. His hard glance travelled along the wall like a palpable ray, about the height of a man’s head. It passed over faces and slipped back to the gilded wainscoting; tiring-women upon whom it fell shivered, and the serving men felt their bowels turn within them. His round face was hard and alert, and his lips moved ceaselessly one upon another. All those serving people wondered to see his head so high, for already it was known that the King had turned sick at the sight of his bedfellow that should be. And indeed the palace was only awake at that late hour because of that astounding news, dignitaries lingering in each other’s quarters to talk of it, whilst in the passages their waiting men supplied gross commentaries.

===Chinese===
驳船绕过一道河湾，极远处，格林尼治王宫的灯火像悬在半空中的一行方格，或明或暗。国王的驳船已在照亮河阶顶上那座带雉堞的拱门。一蓬火炬轰然迸出迎它，随即隐没。其时宫廷驻跸格林尼治，几乎所有的贵族、主教和大小枢密机构的成员都宿在宫中，等候克利维斯的安妮明日驾到。她因冬季海况在加来耽搁了几日，当晚方抵罗切斯特。国王当夜前去相看——人家叫他相信，她生得端凝秀丽、丰姿饶人。他这番谦和的造访是秘密的、微服的；因此园苑里没有执炬的仆役，河阶与中央大门之间横陈着黑暗。只有卫兵们在庭院里生起取暖的旺火，火苗迎着风忽而腾起忽而低伏，时而照出那座高塔苍白而高耸的身影，时而又让它连同石雕的花环、石雕的甲士、它的狮子、玫瑰、豹和无衣的童子一齐没入夜色。起居的屋舍从塔脚下延展开去，待两翼渐渐趋向河边，便不断没入重重暗影。相形之下它们低矮，每一套居室上面都装着假山墙的门面，小块玻璃窗上映着火光，从出其不意的暗影里倏然闪亮。国王管这座宫殿叫普莱森希亚（Placentia），因为住在里面惬意。

克伦威尔缓步登上台阶，身姿倨傲。河拱之下，他的八名绅士侍立迎候；园苑里，他仆从的火把照出剪成孔雀模样的黑紫杉、修得墙一般齐整的树篱——宽阔的砖铺甬道上方开着拱门——还有喷泉，在这沉滞苦寒的夜里，仿佛偷偷地闪亮、滴沥。

一条走廊从大塔底下穿出，绕王宫整整一周。廊上满是匆匆往来的人，还有一堆堆站在门口的马夫。御玺大臣的仪仗过处——他的绅士们穿黑衣、执白杖——他们纷纷贴墙屏息；天花板仿佛垂下一根根雕花镀金的钟乳，要触到他的头。玛丽公主寝所门前的卫士在他走过之后，朝地上啐了一口。他那严苛的目光沿着墙壁扫过去，像一道几乎触手可及的光线，约在人头的高度。它掠过一张张脸，又滑回镀金的壁板；被它照到的侍女们打个寒噤，男仆们只觉肚肠在腹里翻搅。他的圆脸严峻而机警，两片嘴唇一刻不停地互相磨蹭。这些当差的个个纳闷，怎么他头还抬得这样高——国王见了那未来的同床人竟恶心欲呕，这消息早已尽人皆知。宫里到这般深夜还醒着，也确乎正是为着这桩骇人听闻的新闻：显贵们流连在彼此的居处谈论不休，廊道里他们的随从则供应着粗鄙的注疏。

===Original===
He entered his door. In the anteroom two men in his livery removed his outer furs deftly so as not to hinder his walk. Before the fire of his large room a fair boy knelt to pull off his jewelled gloves, and Hanson, one of his secretaries, unclasped from his girdle the corded bag that held the Privy Seal. He laid it on a high stand between two tall candles of wax upon the long table.

The boy went with the gloves and Hanson disappeared silently behind the dark tapestry in the further corner. Cromwell was meditating above a fragment of flaming wood that the fire had spat out far into the tiled fore-hearth. He pressed it with his foot gently towards the blaze of wood in the chimney.

His plump hands were behind his back, his long upper lip ceaselessly caressed its fellow, moving as one line of a snake’s coil glides above another. The January wind crept round the shadowy room behind the tapestry, and as it quivered stags seemed to leap over bushes, hounds to spring in pursuit, and a crowned Diana to move her arms, taking an arrow from a quiver behind her shoulder. The tall candles guarded the bag of the Privy Seal, they fluttered and made the gilded heads on the rafters have sudden grins on their faces that represented kings with flowered crowns, queens with their hair combed back on to pillows, and pages with scolloped hats. Cromwell stepped to an aumbry, where there were a glass of wine, a manchet of bread, and a little salt. He began to eat, dipping pieces of bread into the golden saltcellar. The face of a queen looked down just above his head with her eyes wide open as if she were amazed, thrusting her head from a cloud.

===Chinese===
他进了自己的门。前厅里，两名穿他家号衣的仆人利落地除下他的外裘，免得碍他的脚步。大房间的炉火前，一个俊秀的男孩跪着替他褪下缀宝的手套；他的书记官之一汉森（Hanson）从他腰带上解下那只系着绳的袋子，里头装着御玺。他把袋子放到长桌上、两支高高的蜡烛之间的一座高架上。

男孩携着手套去了，汉森无声地隐入远处角落那幅暗色挂毯后面。克伦威尔正对着一小片燃烧的木头出神——那是炉火吐出来、远远溅到砖砌炉沿上的。他用脚把它轻轻拨向烟囱里那一堆柴焰。

他丰腴的双手背在身后，长长的上唇不住地摩挲着它的同伴，像蛇身上一圈的纹路从另一圈上滑过。一月的风贴着挂毯背后，在这幽暗的房间里逡巡；挂毯颤动时，仿佛有牡鹿跃过灌木，猎犬腾身追逐，一位加冕的狄安娜（Diana）移动手臂，要从肩后的箭囊里抽一支箭。高高的蜡烛守着那袋御玺；烛焰摇曳，映得椽子上那些镀金的头像忽然咧嘴而笑——那是些戴着花冠的国王、发髻后拢、枕在枕上的王后、戴扇贝边帽子的侍童。克伦威尔踱到一座壁橱前，那里有一杯酒、一个面包卷、一点盐。他吃了起来，把面包掰成小块，蘸着那金盐盒里的盐。一张王后的脸恰在他头顶上方俯视，双眼圆睁，仿佛不胜惊愕，从一朵云里探出头来。

===Original===
“Why, I have outlived three queens,” he said to himself, and his round face resignedly despised his world and his times. He had forgotten what anxiety felt like because the world was so peopled with blunderers and timid fools full of hatred.

The marriage with Cleves was the deathblow to the power of the Empire. With the Protestant Princes armed behind his back, the imbecile called Charles would never dare to set his troops on board ship in Flanders to aid the continual rebellions, conspiracies and risings in England. He had done it too often, and he had repented as often, at the last moment. It was true that the marriage had thrown Charles into the arms of France: the French King and he were at that very minute supping together in Paris. They would be making treaties that were meant to be broken, and their statesmen were hatching plots that any scullion would reveal. Francis and his men were too mean, too silly, too despicable, and too easily bribed to hold to any union or to carry out any policy.﻿ ﻿…

He sipped his wine slowly. It was a little cold, so he set it down beside the fire. He wanted to go to bed, but the Archbishop was coming to hear how Henry had received his Queen, and to pour out his fears. Fears! Because the King had been sick at sight of the Cleves woman! He had this King very absolutely in his power; the grey, failing but vindictive and obstinate mass known as Henry was afraid of his contempt, afraid really of a shrug of the shoulder or a small sniff.

===Chinese===
“嗐，我已经送走了三个王后，”他自语道，那张圆脸听天由命地鄙夷着他的世界和他的时代。他早已忘却焦虑是什么滋味，因为这世上挤满了满腹怨毒的莽汉和怯懦的蠢货。

与克利维斯的联姻，是对帝国势力的一记致命打击。有新教诸侯在他背后持剑而立，那个唤作查理的蠢材，便再也不敢从法兰德斯发兵装船，来接应英格兰层出不穷的叛乱、阴谋与起事了。这样的事他做得太多，也太多回在最后关头悔了。不错，这桩婚事把查理抛进了法兰西的怀抱：此时此刻，法兰西国王正同他在巴黎共进晚餐哩。他们会缔结一些存心要撕毁的条约，他们的谋臣在孵化一些随便哪个厨役都能戳穿的阴谋。弗朗索瓦和他的党羽太卑劣、太愚蠢、太下作、又太容易收买，守不住任何同盟，也推行不了任何方略……

他缓缓啜着酒。酒有些凉了，他便把它搁到炉边。他想上床睡觉，可大主教就要来了，来听亨利怎样迎接他的王后，来倾倒他的恐惧。恐惧！就因为国王见了克利维斯那个女人恶心！这个国王已经全然攥在他掌中：那团唤作亨利的、灰色的、衰朽却睚眦必报而顽梗的庞然之物，怕的是他的轻蔑——说实在的，怕的是一耸肩、一嗤鼻。

===Original===
With the generosity of his wine and the warmth of his fire, his thoughts went many years ahead. He imagined the King either married to or having repudiated the Lady from Cleves, and then dead. Edward, the Seymour child, was his creature, and would be king or dead. Cleves children would be his creations too. Or if he married the Lady Mary he would still be next the throne.

His mind rested luxuriously and tranquilly on that prospect. He would be perpetually beside the throne, there would be no distraction to maintain a foothold. He would be there by right; he would be able to give all his mind to the directing of this world that he despised for its baseness, its jealousies, its insane brawls, its aimless selfishness, and its blind furies. Then there should be no more war, as there should be no more revolts. There should be no more jealousies; for kingcraft, solid, austere, practical and inspired, should keep down all the peoples, all the priests, and all the nobles of the world. “Ah,” he thought, “there would be in France no power to shelter traitors like Brancetor.” His eyes became softer in the contemplation of this Utopia, and he moved his upper lip more slowly.

Now the Archbishop was there. Pale, worn with fears and agitation, he came to say that the King had called to him Bishop Gardiner and the more Catholic lords of the Council. Cranmer’s own spy Lascelles had made this new report.

===Chinese===
带着酒的宽和与炉火的暖意，他的思绪驰往许多年后。他想象国王或已另娶、或已废去克利维斯来的那位贵妇，然后死了。爱德华，西摩家（Seymour）的那个孩子，是他的造物，将来讲不定为王，不然便死。克利维斯生的孩子也将出自他的造就。再不然，国王娶了玛丽公主，他也照样紧挨着王座。

他的心思奢逸而安恬地栖息在这幅远景上。他将永居王座之侧，无需再分心去保立足之地。他将名正言顺地待在那里；他可以把全副心智，用来驾驭这个他因其卑污、因其妒恨、因其癫狂的内讧、因其漫无目的的自私、因其盲目的狂怒而鄙夷的世界。到那时便不再有战争，一如不再有叛乱。到那时便不再有妒恨；因为王道之术——坚实、冷峻、切实而得天启——将镇抚世上万民、万僧、万贵。“啊，”他想，“到那时，法兰西便再没有能庇护布兰塞托（Brancetor）之流叛贼的权势了。”悬想这乌托邦之际，他的眼神柔和下来，上唇也动得更慢了。

这时大主教到了。他脸色苍白，被恐惧和激荡折磨得憔悴，来报告说，国王已把温彻斯特主教加德纳和枢密院里旧教色彩更重的几位勋贵召了去。克兰默自己的密探拉塞尔斯（Lascelles）递上了这份新的报闻。

===Original===
His white sleeves made a shivering sound, the fur that fell round his neck was displaced on one shoulder. His large mouth was open with panic, his lips trembled, and his good-natured and narrow eyes seemed about to drop tears.

“Your Grace knoweth well what passed tonight at Rochester,” Cromwell said. He clapped his hands for a man to snuff the candles. “You have the common report.”

“Ah, is it even true?” The Archbishop felt a last hope die, and he choked in his throat. Cromwell watched the man at the candles and said:

“Your Grace hath a new riding mule. I pray it may cease to affright you.”

“Why?” he said, as the man went. “The King’s Highness went even to Rochester, disguised, since it was his good pleasure, as a French lord. You have seen the lady. So his Highness was seized with a make of palsy. He cursed to his barge. I know no more than that.”

===Chinese===
他那白色的衣袖发出一阵颤颤的窸窣，颈间垂落的裘皮歪到了一边肩上。他因惊惶而张着大嘴，嘴唇哆嗦，一双善良的细眼睛看去就要落泪。

“大人并非不知今晚罗切斯特出了甚么事，”克伦威尔说。他拍拍手，叫人来剪烛花。“您听到的，不过是人人都有的传闻。”

“啊，竟是真的么？”大主教觉出最后一线希望死了，喉咙里哽了一声。克伦威尔望着那个剪烛的人，说：

“大人新得了一头骑骡。我祈祷它别再惊了您。”

“这又何必呢？”那人退下后，他说。“国王陛下御驾亲临罗切斯特，微服——既然圣心乐于此道——扮作一位法兰西贵人。您已经见过那位小姐。于是陛下便发了一阵瘫症。他咒骂着回到他的驳船上。我知道的，不比这些更多。”

===Original===
“And now they sit in the council.”

“It seems,” Cromwell said.

“Ah, dear God have mercy.”

The Archbishop’s thin hands wavered before the crucifix on his breast, and made the sign of the cross.

The very faces of his enemies seemed visible to him. He saw Gardiner, of Winchester, with his snake’s eyes under the flat cap, and the Duke of Norfolk with his eyes malignant in a long, yellow face. He had a vision of the King, a huge red lump beneath the high dais at the head of the Council table, his face suffused with blood, his cheeks quivering.

===Chinese===
“而此刻他们正坐在枢密院里。”

“像是这样，”克伦威尔说。

“啊，亲爱的上帝，发发慈悲。”

大主教瘦削的双手在胸前的十字架前摇曳，画了个十字。

仇敌们的面目仿佛就显在他眼前。他看见温彻斯特的加德纳，扁帽底下一双蛇眼；看见诺福克公爵，长长的黄脸上目光歹毒。他还有一个幻象：国王，枢密院长桌尽头高台底下的一大团红肉，满脸充血，两颊打颤。

===Original===
He wrung his hands and wondered if at Smithfield the Lutherans would pray for him, or curse him for having been lukewarm.

“Why, goodman gossip,” Cromwell said compassionately, “we have been nearer death ten times.” He uttered his inmost thoughts out of pity:﻿—All this he had awaited. The King’s Highness by the report of his painters, his ambassadors, his spies﻿—they were all in the pay of Cromwell﻿—had awaited a lady of modest demeanour, a coy habit, and a great and placid fairness. “I had warned the Almains at Rochester to attire her against our coming. But she slobbered with ecstasy and slipped sideways, aiming at a courtesy. Therefore the King was hot with new anger and disgust.”

“You and I are undone.” Cranmer was passive with despair.

“He is very seldom an hour of one mind,” Cromwell answered. “Unless in that hour those you wot of shall work upon him, it will go well with us.”

“They shall. They shall.”

===Chinese===
他绞着双手，不知道到了史密斯菲尔德（Smithfield），路德派们肯为他祈祷呢，还是要咒骂他曾不冷不热。

“嗐，老伙计，”克伦威尔怜悯地说，“咱们曾有十回比这更贴近死亡。”他出于怜悯，把心底最深的话都说了出来：——这一切他早已等着了。国王陛下，凭他的画师们、他的使节们、他的密探们——他们全都领着克伦威尔的银子——的报告，等着的是一位举止端凝、情态娇羞、容貌丰丽而恬静的小姐。“我早告诫过罗切斯特的德意志人，叫他们趁我们到来之前替她装束停当。可她欢喜得涎水直淌，侧身一滑，想行个屈膝礼。于是国王便新添了一腔怒火与憎厌。”

“您和我都完了。”克兰默因绝望而一任摆布。

“他难得有一个钟头心思不移，”克伦威尔答道。“除非在那一个钟头里，教您知道的那班人打动了他的心，我们的事都会顺遂。”

“他们会打动他的。他们会打动他的。”

===Original===
“I wait to see.”

There seemed to Cranmer something horrible in this impassivity. He wished his leader to go to the King, and he had a frantic moment of imagining himself running to a great distance, hiding his head in darkness.

Cromwell’s lips went up in scorn. “Do you imagine the yellow duke speaking his mind to the King? He is too craven.”

A heavy silence fell between them. The fire rustled, the candles again needed snuffing.

“Best get to bed,” Cromwell said at last.

===Chinese===
“我等着瞧。”

在克兰默看来，这无动于衷里有一种可怖的东西。他盼着他的首领到国王那里去，有一阵子竟狂乱地想象自己奔到极远的地方，把头藏进黑暗里。

克伦威尔的嘴唇轻蔑地向上一撇。“您当那黄脸公爵敢向国王吐露心曲么？他太怯了。”

两人之间落下一阵沉重的静默。炉火窸窣，蜡烛又该剪花了。

“还是上床的好，”克伦威尔终于说。

===Original===
“Could I sleep?” Cranmer had the irritation of extreme fear. His master seemed to him to have no bowels. But the waiting told at last upon Cromwell himself.

“I could sleep an you would let me,” he said sharply. “I tell you the King shall be another man in the morning.”

“Ay, but now. But now.﻿ ﻿…” He imagined the pens in that distant room creaking over the paper with their committals, and he wished to upbraid Cromwell. It was his policy of combining with Lutherans that had brought them to this.

Heavy thundering came on the outer door.

“The King comes,” Cromwell cried victoriously. He went swiftly from the room. The Archbishop closed his eyes and suddenly remembered the time when he had been a child.

===Chinese===
“我睡得着么？”克兰默带着极度恐惧的躁怒。在他看来，他的首领简直没有心肝。可这等待，到头来也磨动了克伦威尔自己。

“您若肯放我，我本睡得着，”他厉声说。“我告诉您，到了早晨，国王就是另一个人了。”

“是，可眼下呢。眼下呢。……”他想象那间远处的屋子里，笔尖正咯吱咯吱在纸上写着拘押令；他想责备克伦威尔。正是克伦威尔同路德派结盟的一贯方略，把他们带到了这步田地。

外门上传来沉重的擂打。

“国王来了，”克伦威尔得胜般喊道。他疾步出房。大主教闭上眼睛，忽然记起自己还是孩子的时光。

===Original===
Privy Seal had an angry and contemptuous frown at his return. “They have kept him from me.” He threw a little scroll on to the table. Its white silence made Cranmer shudder; it seemed to have something of the heavy threatening of the King’s self.

“We may go to bed,” Cromwell said. “They have devised their shift.”

“You say?”

“They have temporised, they have delayed. I know them.” He quoted contemptuously from the letter: “We would have you send presently to ask of the Almain Lords with the Lady Anne the papers concerning her pre-contract to the Duke of Lorraine.”

Cranmer was upon the point of going away in the joy of this respite. But his desire to talk delayed him, and he began to talk about the canon law and pre-contracts of marriage. It was a very valid cause of nullity all the doctors held.

===Chinese===
回来时，御玺大臣拧着一脸怒气与轻蔑。“他们把我同他隔开了。”他把一个小纸卷掷到桌上。那卷白纸的沉默叫克兰默发抖；它似乎带着几分国王本人的沉重威吓。

“我们可以睡了，”克伦威尔说。“他们已想出他们的招数了。”

“您是说？”

“他们敷衍，他们拖延。我了解他们。”他轻蔑地引用信上的话：“望卿即刻遣人，向随侍安妮小姐的德意志诸贵查询，索取有关她与洛林公爵（Duke of Lorraine）先期婚约的文书。”

克兰默因这缓刑之喜，几乎就要抽身告退了。可他攀谈的欲望又拖住了他，他便谈起了教会法和婚姻的先期婚约来。所有的博士都认定，那是婚姻无效的十足理由。

===Original===
“Think you I have not made very certain the pre-contract was nullified? This is no shift,” and Cromwell spoke wearily and angrily. “Goodman Archbishop, dry your tears. Tonight the King is hot with disgust, but I tell you he will not cast away his kingdom upon whether her teeth be white or yellow. This is no woman’s man.”

Cranmer came nearer the fire and stretched out his lean hands.

“He hath dandled of late with the Lady Cassilis.”

“Well, he hath been pleasant with her.”

===Chinese===
“您以为我没有查明又查明，那份先期婚约早已注销作废？这算不得甚么招数，”克伦威尔说，声气又倦又恼。“大主教老哥，擦干您的眼泪。今晚国王是恼厌攻心，可我告诉您，他不至于为了她的牙齿是白是黄，便把王国抛了。他可不是拜倒在女人脚下的人。”

克兰默凑近炉火，伸出瘦削的双手。

“他近来正同卡西利斯夫人（Lady Cassilis）调笑逗弄哩。”

“嗯，他对她一向殷勤和悦。”

===Original===
Cranmer urged: “A full-blown man towards his failing years is more prone to women than before.”

“Then he may go a-wenching.” He began to speak with a weary passion. To cast away the Lady Anne now were a madness. It would be to stand without a friend before all nations armed to their downfall. This King would do no jot to lose a patch upon his sovereignty.

Cranmer sought to speak.

“His Highness is always hot o’nights,” Cromwell kept on. “It is in his nature so to be. But by morning the German princes shall make him afraid again and the Lutherans of this goodly realm. Those mad swine our friends!”

===Chinese===
克兰默进言道：“人到了盛年将尽的时候，比从前更容易恋着女色。”

“那他便去寻花问柳好了。”他开始带着一种厌倦的激愤说下去。此刻抛弃安妮小姐，是丧心病狂。那就等于在磨刀霍霍、等着他们倒台的列国面前，孤零零没有半个朋友。这位国王，绝不肯为任何事折损他主权的一星半点。

克兰默想开口。

“陛下一到夜里总欲火炽盛，”克伦威尔只顾说下去。“他的天性如此。可到了早晨，德意志的诸侯们，还有这个昌盛国度的路德派们，就又要叫他害怕了。那些疯猪，还是咱们的盟友哩！”

===Original===
“He will burn seven of them on tomorrow sennight,” Cranmer said.

“Nay! I shall enlarge them on Wednesday.”

Cranmer shivered. “They grow very insolent. I am afraid.”

Cromwell answered with a studied nonchalance:

===Chinese===
“再过一星期的明天，他就要把他们中间的七个烧死，”克兰默说。

“不！我星期三便放他们出来。”

克兰默打个寒噤。“他们闹得越发肆无忌惮了。我怕。”

克伦威尔拿出一副刻意的不经意回答：

===Original===
“My bones tell me it shall be an eastward wind. It shall not rain on the new Queen’s bridals.” He drank up his warm wine and brushed the crumbs from the furs round his neck.

“You are a very certain man,” the Archbishop said.

Going along the now dark corridors he was afraid that some ruffling boy might spring upon him from the shadows. Norfolk, as the Earl Marshal, had placed his lodgings in a very distant part of the palace to give him long journeys that, telling upon his asthma, made him arrive breathless and convulsed at the King’s rooms when he was sent for.

===Chinese===
“我的骨头告诉我，要起东风了。新王后大婚之日，下不了雨。”他喝干那杯温酒，掸去颈间裘皮上的面包屑。

“您真是个十拿九稳的人，”大主教说。

走在此时已一片漆黑的走廊里，他害怕会有哪个寻衅的少年从暗影里朝他扑上来。诺福克身为王室典礼官，把他的住所安置在宫中极偏远处，叫他每回奉召都要长途跋涉，哮喘被诱发，到得国王的房间时已气急败坏、浑身抽搐。

### III / 三

===Original===
The shadow of the King kept hands from throats in the palace, but grooms were breaking each others’ heads in the stables till towards morning. They fought about whether it were lawful to eat fish on a Friday, and just after daybreak a gentleman’s oarsman from Sittingbourne had all his teeth to swallow for asserting that the sacrament should be administered in the two kinds. The horses were watered by ostlers who hummed the opprobrious song about Privy Seal, called “Crummock.”

In the hillocks and lawns of the park round the palace Lutherans waited all night to welcome their Queen. They lit small fires on the turf and, standing round them, sang triumphal hymns. A Princess was coming from Cleves, a Lutheran; the dayspring from on high was visiting them; soon, soon now, the axe and the flail should be given into their hands.

In the dawn their boats could be seen pulling like water-beetles all across the pallid river from the Essex shores. They clustered in grey masses round the common steps.

A German horse merchant from the City pulled a putrid cat out of the river mud and held it over his head. He shrieked: “Hic hocus pocus,” parodying the “*Hoc corpus meum*” of the Mass. The soldiers of the Duke of Norfolk were unable to reach him for the crowd. There were but ten of them, under a captain, set to guard the little postern in the side wall of the garden. Towards ten o’clock the Mayor of London came by land. He had with him all his brotherhood with their horses and armed guards in a long train. The mayor and his aldermen had entrance into the palace, but the Duke had given orders that men and horses must bide in the park. There were forty battles of them, each of one hundred men.

The great body came in sight, white, shining even in the grey among the trees along the long garden wall.

===Chinese===
国王的阴影在宫里镇着，使人不得向彼此的喉咙下手；马厩里，马夫们却互相打破脑袋，直闹到将近天明。他们为星期五食鱼是否合法而斗殴；破晓刚过，西廷伯恩（Sittingbourne）来的一名绅士的桨手，只因坚称圣餐应当饼酒两式兼领，便被打得满口牙齿咽下肚去。饮马的马夫们一面哼着讥刺御玺大臣的骂歌，那歌名叫“克鲁默克”（“Crummock”）。

宫殿四周园苑的丘阜与草茵上，路德派们彻夜守候，迎接他们的王后。他们在草皮上燃起一堆堆小火，围着火站成一圈圈，唱着得胜的圣诗。一位公主正从克利维斯而来，一位路德派的公主；高天的晨光已照临他们；很快，很快，斧钺与连枷就要交到他们手中了。

晨光里，看得见他们的小船从埃塞克斯（Essex）岸边出发，像一队队水甲虫，划过整片灰白的河面。他们灰压压地攒聚成团，围在公共码头台阶四周。

城里来的一名德意志马贩从河泥里拽出一具腐烂的猫尸，高举过头顶。他尖声怪叫：“Hic hocus pocus”〔戏法师的胡乱咒语〕，戏仿的是弥撒中的“*Hoc corpus meum*”〔这是我的身体〕。诺福克公爵的兵丁被人群阻着，够不到他。他们统共十人，归一名队长统带，奉命守卫花园侧墙上的小后门。将近十点钟，伦敦市长从旱路到了。他带着他公会的全体人马，骑者与武装卫队拖成一条长阵。市长与其参事们获准进宫，公爵却已下令：人马一概留在苑中。他们共有四十个战团，每团一百人。

大队人马进入了视野，白皑皑一片，在灰色天光里沿着长长的花园围墙、在树木之间发亮。


===Original===
“Body of God,” the captain said, “there shall be broken crowns.” He bade his men hold their pikes across, and paced unconcernedly up and down before the door.

The City men came down in a solid body, and at sight of the red crosses on their white shoulders the Lutherans set up a cry of “Rome, Rome.” Their stones began to fly at once, and, because they pressed so closely in, the City bowmen had no room to string their bows. The citizens struck out with their silvered staves, but the heavy armour under their white surcoats hindered them. The Lutherans cried out that the Kingdom of God was come on earth because a Queen from Cleves was at hand.

An alderman’s charger was struck by a stone. It broke loose and crashed all foaming and furious through a tripe stall on which a preacher was perched to hold forth. The riot began then. All in among the winter trees the City men in their white and silver were fighting with the Lutherans in their grey frieze. The citizens’ hearts were enraged because their famous Dominican preacher had been seized by the Archbishop and spirited into Kent. They cried to each other to avenge Dr. Latter on these lowsels.

Men struck out at all and sundry. A woman, covered to the face in a fur hood and riding a grey mule, was hit on the arm by the quarterstaff of a Protestant butcher from the Crays, because she wore a crucifix round her neck. She covered her face and shrieked lamentably. A man in green at the mule’s head, on the other side, sprang like a wild cat under the beast’s neck. His face blazed white, his teeth shone like a dog’s, he screamed and struck his dagger through the butcher’s throat.

===Chinese===
“上帝的身体！”队长说，“免不了要打破几颗脑袋。”他吩咐部下把长矛横持，自己若无其事地在门前踱来踱去。

城里人结成整队压了下来，路德派一见他们白袍肩上的红十字，便喊起“罗马，罗马”来。石块立时乱飞；由于人群逼得太近，城里的弓手竟没有余地张弓上弦。市民们抡起包银的官杖还击，但白色罩袍底下的沉重铠甲碍着他们的手脚。路德派高喊：神的国已临到尘世，因为克利维斯的王后近在眼前了。

一名市参事的战马被石块击中，挣脱了缰，口吐白沫、狂怒地冲垮一个卖牛肚的摊子，摊上正踞着一位宣道师讲道。骚乱就此开场。冬日的树木之间，穿白着银的城里人与穿灰色粗呢的路德派厮打成一团。市民们心头火起，因为他们那位有名的多明我会（Dominican）宣道师被大主教捉去，密移到了肯特（Kent）。他们彼此呼喊，要在这帮贱胚身上为拉特博士（Dr. Latter）报仇。

人们见人就打。一个妇人，皮兜帽遮到脸上，骑一头灰骡，只因颈间挂着一具十字苦像，臂上便挨了克雷（the Crays）来的一个新教屠户一记长棍。她掩住脸，哀哀地尖叫。骡头另一侧的一个绿衣汉子，野猫般蹿到牲口颈下。他的脸白得像火光一闪，牙齿像狗的一般发亮，他嘶叫着，把匕首捅穿了屠户的喉咙。


===Original===
His motions were those of a mad beast; he stabbed the mule in the shoulder to force it to plunge in the direction of the soldiers who kept the little gate, before in the throng the butcher had reached the ground. The woman was flogging at the mule with her reins. “I have killed ’un,” he shrieked.

He dived under the pikes of the soldiers and gripped the captain by both shoulders. “We be the cousins of the Duke of Norfolk,” he cried. His square red beard trembled beneath his pallid face, and suddenly he became speechless with rage.

Hands were already pulling the woman from her saddle, but the guards held their pikes transversely against the faces of the nearest, crushing in noses and sending sudden streaks of blood from jaws. The uproar was like a hurricane and the woman’s body, on high, swayed into the little space that the soldiers held. She was crying with the pain of her arm that she held with her other hand. Her cousin ran to her and mumbled words of inarticulate tenderness, ending again in “I have killed ’un.”

The mob raged round them, but the soldiers stood firm enough. A continual cry of “Harlot, harlot,” went up. Stones were scarce on the sward of the park, but a case bottle aimed at the woman alighted on the ear of one of the guards. It burst in a foam of red, and he fell beneath the belly of the mule with a dry grunt and the clang of iron. The soldiers put down the points of their pikes and cleared more ground. Men lay wallowing there when they retreated.

===Chinese===
他的一举一动像头疯兽；不等那屠户的尸身在人群里倒地，他已把匕首戳进骡子的肩头，逼它朝守小门的兵丁那边冲去。那妇人正用缰绳抽打骡子。“我杀了那家伙！”他尖声叫道。

他从兵丁们的矛杆底下钻过去，双手抓住队长的双肩。“我们是诺福克公爵的亲戚！”他喊道。他方正的红胡须在苍白的脸底下颤抖，忽然间他怒得说不出话来。

许多只手已经在把妇人往下拖离鞍座，卫兵们却把长矛横过来对着最近那些人的脸，压塌鼻梁，叫颚间迸出一道道血来。喧嚣如飓风，那妇人的身子高高在上，摇摇晃晃进了兵丁们守住的那一小块空地。她一只手托着另一只受伤的胳膊，疼得直哭。她的表哥奔到她身边，喃喃说着不成语句的温存话，末了又是那句“我杀了那家伙”。

暴民围着他们咆哮，兵丁们却站得纹丝不动。“荡妇，荡妇”的喊声此起彼伏。园苑草皮上石块稀少，可有一只掷向那妇人的扁酒瓶，落在了其中一名卫兵的耳朵上。瓶子迸出一团血红的泡沫，他闷哼一声，带着铁器的铿响倒在骡腹底下。兵丁们放平矛尖，又清出一圈空地。他们退后时，人们还在那里打滚。


===Original===
The man shouted at the captain: “Can you clear us a way to yon stairs?” and, at a shake of the head, “Then let us enter this gate.”

The captain shook his head again.

“I am Thomas Culpepper. This is the Duke’s niece, Kat,” the other shouted.

The captain observed him stoically from over his thick and black beard.

===Chinese===
那汉子朝队长喊道：“你能替我们清出一条路，到那边的码头台阶去么？”见对方摇头，又说：“那就让我们进这道门。”

队长又摇了摇头。

“我是托马斯·卡尔佩珀。这位是公爵大人的侄孙女凯特（Kat），”那汉子喊道。

队长从他那部浓黑的胡须上方恬然打量着他。


===Original===
“The King’s Highness is within this garden,” he said. He spoke to the porter through the little niche at the wicket. A company of the City soldiers, their wands beating like flails, cleared for a moment the space in front of the guards.

Culpepper with the hilt of his sword was hammering at the studded door. The captain caught him by the shoulder and sent him to stagger against the mule’s side. He was gasping and snatched at his hilt. His bonnet had fallen off, his yellow hair was like a shock of wheat, and his red beard flecked with foam that spattered from his mouth.

“I have killed one. I will kill thee,” he stuttered at the captain. The woman caught him round the neck.

“Oh, be still,” she shrieked. “Still. Calm. Y’kill me.” She clutched him so closely that he was half throttled. The captain paced stoically up and down before the gate.

===Chinese===
“国王陛下就在这园子里，”他说。他隔着便门上的小壁龛同门房说话。一队城里兵丁挥杖如连枷，一时清出了卫兵门前的空地。

卡尔佩珀用剑柄捶打那扇钉头门。队长一把抓住他的肩，把他推得踉跄着撞在骡子身上。他喘着气，又伸手去抓剑柄。他的软帽掉落了，黄头发像一束麦秸，红胡子上溅着口中喷出的点点飞沫。

“我已经杀了一个。我还要杀了汝，”他冲着队长结结巴巴地说。那妇人搂住了他的脖子。

“嗳，别动了，”她尖叫道。“别动。定一定。你要我的命了。”她把他搂得那么紧，勒得他半晌透不过气来。队长在门前恬然地踱来踱去。


===Original===
“Madam,” he said, “I have sent one hastening to his duke-ship. Doubtless you shall enter.” He bent to pull the soldier from beneath the mule’s belly by one foot, and picking up his pike, leaned it against the wall.

With his face pressed against his cousin’s furred side, Thomas Culpepper swore he would cut the man’s throat.

“Aye, come back again,” he answered. “They call me Sir Christopher Aske.”

The red jerkins of the King’s own guards came in a heavy mass round the end of the wall amid shrieks and curses. Their pikestaves rose rhythmically and fell with dull thuds; with their clumsy gloved hands they caught at throats, and they threw dazed men and women into the space that they had cleared before the wall. There armourers were ready, with handcuffs and leg-chains hanging like necklaces round their shoulders.

===Chinese===
“夫人，”他说，“我已差人飞跑报他家公爵大人去了。您必定进得去。”他弯下腰，拽着一只脚把那卫兵从骡腹底下拖出来，又拾起长矛，靠在墙上。

托马斯·卡尔佩珀把脸贴着表妹的皮裘侧面，赌咒说要割那人的喉咙。

“好，回头再来，”他答道。“人家叫我克里斯托弗·阿斯克爵士。”

国王贴身卫队的红色号衣，在尖叫与咒骂声中，沉甸甸地从墙那头围了过来。他们的矛杆起落有节，落下时闷响一声声；他们戴着笨拙的手套去掐喉咙，把打得晕头转向的男男女女扔到墙前清出的空地上。那里军械士早已候着，手铐脚镣像项链似的挂在他们肩头。


===Original===
The door in the wall opened silently, the porter called through his niche: “These have leave to enter.” Thomas Culpepper shouted “Coneycatcher” at the captain before he pulled the mule’s head round. The beast hung back on his hand, and he struck it on its closed eyes in a tumult of violent rage. It stumbled heavily on the threshold, and then darted forward so swiftly that he did not hear the direction of the porter that they should turn only at the third alley.

Tall and frosted trees reached up into the dim skies, the deserted avenues were shrouded in mist, and there was a dead and dripping silence.

“Seven brawls y’have brought me into,” the woman’s voice came from under her hood, “this weary journey.”

He ran to her stirrup and clutched her glove to his forehead. “Y’ave calmed me,” he said. “Your voice shall ever calm me.”

===Chinese===
墙上的门无声地开了，门房隔着壁龛喊道：“这几位获准进内。”托马斯·卡尔佩珀掉转骡头之前，冲队长骂了一声“拐子”。那牲口坠着他的手不肯走，他在暴怒如狂之中，一拳打在它闭着的眼睛上。骡子在门槛上重重绊了一下，随即窜得那么快，门房吩咐他们须到第三条岔道才得转弯，他竟没有听清。

高大结霜的树木探入朦胧的天空，杳无人迹的长径笼在雾里，一片死寂，唯有滴水之声。

“这趟乏人的旅程，你害我卷进了七场殴斗，”妇人的声音从兜帽底下传来。

他奔到她的马镫旁，把她的手套按在自己的额头上。“你使我平静下来了，”他说。“你的声音永远能使我平静。”


===Original===
She uttered a hopeless “Oh, aye,” and then, “Where be we?”

They had entered a desolate region of clipped yews, frozen fountains, and high, trimmed hedges. He dragged the mule after him. Suddenly there opened up a very broad path, tiled for a width of many feet. On the left it ran to a high tower’s gaping arch. On the right it sloped nobly into a grey stretch of water.

“The river is even there,” he muttered. “We shall find the stairs.”

“I would find my uncle in this palace,” she said. But he muttered, “Nay, nay,” and began to beat the mule with his fist. It swerved, and she became sick and dizzy with the sudden jar on her hurt arm. She swayed in her saddle and, in a sudden flaw of wind, her old and torn furs ruffled jaggedly all over her body.

===Chinese===
她发出一声绝望的“哦，是了”，接着又问：“咱们这是到了哪儿？”

他们已走进一片荒凉境地：修剪过的紫杉、冻结的喷泉、又高又齐整的树篱。他拖着骡子跟在后面。忽然眼前展开一条极宽的路径，铺砖的宽度足有数英尺。左边通向一座高塔张口的拱门，右边气派地斜下一片灰蒙蒙的水面。

“河就在那边，”他喃喃道。“我们会找到码头台阶的。”

“我要在这座宫里找到我的叔祖，”她说。他却喃喃着“不，不”，开始用拳头捶打骡子。骡子一侧身，她伤臂上猛然一震，顿时恶心头晕。她在鞍上摇晃，一阵疾风里，她那身破旧的皮裘参差错乱地翻卷起来，裹满全身。

### IV / 四

===Original===
The King was pacing the long terrace on the river front. He had been there since very early, for he could not sleep at nights, and had no appetite for his breakfast. When a gentleman from the postern gate asked permission for Culpepper and the mule to pass to the private stairs, he said heavily:

“Let me not be elbowed by cripples,” and then: “A’ God’s name let them come,” changing his mind, as was his custom after a bad night, before his first words had left his thick, heavy lips. His great brow was furrowed, his enormous bulk of scarlet, with the great double dog-rose embroidered across the broad chest, limped a little over his right knee and the foot dragged. His eyes were bloodshot and heavy, his head hung forward as though he were about to charge the world with his forehead. From time to time his eyebrows lifted painfully, and he swallowed with an effort as if he were choking.

Behind him the three hundred windows of the palace Placentia seemed to peer at him like eyes, curious, hostile, lugubrious or amazed. He tore violently at his collar and muttered: “I stifle.” His great hand was swollen by its glove, sewn with pearls, to an immense size.

The gentleman told him of the riot in the park, and narrated the blasphemy of the German Lutheran, who had held up a putrid dog in parody of the Holy Mass.

The face of the King grew suffused with purple blood.

“Let those men be cut down,” he said, and he conceived a sorting out of all heresy, a cleansing of his land with blood. He looked swiftly at the low sky as if a thunderbolt or a leprosy must descend upon his head. He commanded swiftly, “Let them be taken in scores. Bid the gentlemen of my guard go, and armourers with shackles.”

===Chinese===
国王正在临河的长露台上踱来踱去。他天没亮就已到了那里，因为夜里睡不安稳，早餐也毫无胃口。后门一位侍从请示可否放卡尔佩珀和那头骡子过去到私用阶梯，他沉甸甸地说：

“别叫瘸子们挤撞我。”随即又道：“以上帝之名，放他们过来。”一夜睡得糟糕之后，他惯常如此：头一句话还没离开那两片肥厚的嘴唇，主意已经变了。他宽大的额头上沟壑纵横，那具腥红的庞大身躯——宽阔的胸前横绣着一朵硕大的重瓣犬蔷薇——右膝微微一个踉跄，那只脚便拖曳起来。他双眼布满血丝、滞重难睁，头颅前倾，仿佛正要用额头去冲撞整个世界。他不时痛苦地扬起眉毛，费力地吞咽，像噎住了似的。

他身后，普拉森舍宫（Placentia）的三百扇窗子仿佛一只只眼睛窥伺着他，好奇的、怨毒的、凄怆的、骇异的。他狠狠扯开衣领，喃喃道：“我闷得慌。”他那只大手叫缀满珍珠的手套绷得肿胀，胀到了吓人的尺寸。

侍从禀报了园囿中的骚乱，又讲起那个德意志路德派信徒的渎神行径：他高高举起一条腐烂的死狗，戏仿弥撒圣祭。

国王的脸上涌满了紫黑的血。

“把那些人砍了，”他说，心中已然起了一个念头：要将一切异端甄别清理，用血洗净他的国土。他倏地瞥了一眼低垂的天色，仿佛霹雳或癞疫眼看就要降到他头上。他疾速传令：“成批地拿办。叫我的卫队侍从们去，再叫军械匠们带上镣铐。”


===Original===
The sharp pain of the ulcer in his leg gnawed up to his thigh, and he stood, dejected, like a hunted man, with his head hanging on his chest, so that his great bonnet pointed at the ground. He commanded that both Privy Seal and the Duke of Norfolk should come to him there upon the instant.

This grey and heavy King, who had been a great scholar, dreaded to read in Latin now, for it brought the language of the Mass into his mind; he had been a composer of music and a skilful player on the luquotation markste, but no music and no voices could any more tickle his ears.

Women he had loved well in his day. Now, when he desired rest, music, good converse and the love of women, he was forced to wed with a creature whose face resembled that of a pig stuck with cloves. He had raged overnight, but, with the morning, he had seen himself growing old, on a tottering throne, assailed by all the forces of the Old Faith in Christendom. Rebellions burst out like fires every day in all the corners of his land. He had no men whom he could trust: if he granted a boon to one party it held them only for a day, and the other side rose up. Now he rested upon the Lutherans, whom he hated, and, standing on that terrace, he had watched gloomily the great State barges of the Ambassadors from the Empire and from France come with majestic ostentation downstream abreast, to moor side by side against the steps of his water-gate.

It was a parade of their new friendship. Six months ago their trains could not have mingled without bloodshed.

At last there stood before him Thomas Cromwell, un-bonneted, smiling, humorous, supple and confident for himself and for his master’s cause, a man whom his Prince might trust. And the long melancholy and sinister figure of the Duke of Norfolk stalked stiffly down among the yew trees powdered with frost. The furs from round his neck fluttered about his knees like the wings of a crow, and he dug his Earl Marshal’s golden staff viciously into the ground. He waved his jewelled cap and stood still at a little distance. Cromwell regarded him with a sinister and watchful amusement; he looked back at Privy Seal with a black malignancy that hardened his yellow features, his hooked nose and pursed lips into the likeness of a mask representing hatred.

This Norfolk was that Earl of Surrey who had won Flodden Field. They all then esteemed him the greatest captain of his day﻿—in the field a commander sleepless, cunning, cautious, and, in striking, a Hotspur.

===Chinese===
腿上溃疡的锐痛一路啃噬上大腿，他颓然伫立，像个被追猎的人，头垂在胸前，那顶大软帽直指地面。他传谕御玺大臣与诺福克公爵即刻到那里见他。

这位灰色而沉重的国王，当年曾是了不起的学者，如今却怕读拉丁文，因为拉丁文会把弥撒的语言带进他的心里；他曾是作曲的家、高明的鲁特琴手，可如今再没有任何音乐与人声能撩动他的耳朵。

当年他也曾深爱过女色。如今，当他渴望安宁、音乐、清谈与女子的爱恋之时，却被迫娶了一门亲——那活物的脸，活像一只插遍丁香的猪。他夜间暴怒了一场，可到了清晨，他看见自己正在老去，端坐在摇摇欲坠的王座上，遭着基督教世界全部旧教势力的围攻。叛乱如火，日日在他国土的各个角落里迸发。他没有可以信托的人：施恩于一党，那一党只消停一日，另一党便又起来作乱。如今他倚为支柱的，是他所憎恨的路德派；方才站在露台上，他曾阴郁地望着皇帝与法兰西两国大使的盛大御舫，威严煊赫地并排顺流而下，并排系泊在他水门的台阶旁。

这是两国新交好的一场炫耀。六个月前，他们的随从队伍若是相遇，断不能不见血。

终于，托马斯·克伦威尔站到了他面前：免冠、含笑、诙谐、柔顺，对自己、对主上的大业都满怀自信，是一位君王尽可托付之人。跟着，诺福克公爵那瘦长、阴郁而险恶的身影，迈着僵直的步子，从覆着白霜的紫杉树间走下来。他颈间垂落的裘皮飘到膝边，像乌鸦的双翼，他把王室典礼官的金杖狠狠捅进地里。他挥了挥缀宝的帽子，在稍远处站定。克伦威尔用一种阴险而警惕的兴味打量他；他也回瞪御玺大臣，那一腔黑毒把他蜡黄的面孔、鹰钩鼻子和紧抿的嘴，硬生生凝成一具仇恨的假面。

这位诺福克，便是当年赢得弗洛登原野（Flodden Field）的那位萨里伯爵。那时众人都推他为当世第一名将——临阵不眠的统帅，诡诈、谨慎，而一旦挥兵突进，又是一员霍茨珀（Hotspur）式的猛将。


===Original===
A dour and silent man, he was the head of all the Catholics, of all the reaction of that day. But, in the long duel between himself and Cromwell he had seemed fated to be driven from post to post, never daring to proclaim himself openly the foe of the man he dreaded and hated. Cranmer, with his tolerant spirit, he despised. Here was an archbishop who might rack and burn for discipline’s sake, and he did nothing.﻿ ﻿… And all these New Learning men with their powers of language, these dark bearded men with twinkling and sagacious eyes, he detested. He went clean shaved, lean and yellow-faced, with a hooked nose that seemed about to dig into his chin. It was he who said first: “It was merry in England before this New Learning came in.”

The night before, the King had sworn that he would have Privy Seal’s head because Anne of Cleves resembled a pig stuck with cloves. And, shaking and shivering with cold that penetrated his very inwards, with a black pain on his brow and sparks dancing before his jaundiced eyes, the Duke cursed himself for not having urged then the immediate arrest of the Privy Seal. For here stood Cromwell, arrogantly by the King’s side with the King graciously commanding him to cover his head because it was very cold and Cromwell was known to suffer with the earache.

“You are Earl Marshal,” the King’s voice drowned Norfolk’s morning greeting. He veered upon the Duke with such violence that his enormous red bulk seemed about to totter over upon the tall and bent figure. A searing pain had shot up his side, and, as he gripped it, he appeared to be furiously plucking at his dagger. He had imagined Chapuys and Marillac, the Ambassadors, coming upon guards with broken heads and sending to Paris letters over which Francis and his nephew should snigger and chuckle.
