“You are Earl Marshal. You have the ordering of these ceremonies, and you let rebels and knaves break heads within my very park for all the world to see!”

In his rage Norfolk blurted out:

“Privy Seal hath his friends, too﻿—these Lutherans. What man could have foreseen how insolent they be grown, for joy at welcoming a Queen of their faith,” he repeated hotly. “No man could have foreseen. My bands are curtailed.”

===Chinese===
他是个执拗寡言的人，是当时天下教皇派、一切复旧势力的首领。但在他同克伦威尔的长期角力中，他似乎命定要从一处壁垒被逼到另一处壁垒，始终不敢公然自命为他所惧所恨之人的仇敌。克兰默那副宽和心肠，他看不上：这样一位大主教，为了整饬纲纪本可以施拉肢架、下火刑，他却无所作为。……还有这些新学派的人，这些舌粲莲花的汉子，这些黑须、目光闪烁而精明的汉子，他一概深恶。他自己剃得精光，精瘦的一张黄脸，鹰钩鼻险些要戳进下巴。头一个说出这话的正是他：“新学进来之前，英格兰的日子何等快活。”

前一夜，国王曾发誓要取御玺大臣的脑袋，只因克利维斯的安妮长得像一只插遍丁香的猪。此刻，公爵浑身打战，寒气直透脏腑，额头一片黑沉沉的痛楚，黄疸的眼睛前火星乱舞，直恨自己昨晚没有力主即刻逮捕御玺大臣。因为克伦威尔此刻正倨傲地侍立在国王身旁——国王还和颜悦色地命他戴上帽子，说天气很冷，而谁都知道克伦威尔大人害耳痛。

“你是王室典礼官。”国王的声音淹没了诺福克的晨间问安。他猛地转向公爵，用力之猛，使他那腥红的庞大身躯几乎要整个儿倾倒在那高大佝偻的身影上。一股灼痛直蹿上体侧，他一把按住痛处，看去竟像在狂怒地拔他的匕首。他已经想象到两位大使查普伊斯（Chapuys）与马里拉克（Marillac）撞见被人打破头的卫兵，把书信发往巴黎，叫弗朗索瓦和他外甥对着信笺窃笑不止。

“你是王室典礼官。这些典礼全归你调度，你却容叛贼和恶棍在我的苑囿里打破人头，给普天下看！”

诺福克盛怒之下，脱口说道：

“御玺大臣也有他的朋友——便是这些路德派。谁能料到他们竟狂妄到这般地步，只因欢喜迎了一位他们教中的王后，”他忿忿地又说了一遍。“谁也料不到。我带的部众遭了削减。”


===Original===
Cromwell said:

“Aye, men are needed to keep down the Papists of your North parts.”

The two men faced each other. It had been part of the Duke’s plan﻿—and Cromwell knew it very well﻿—that the City men should meet with the Lutherans there in the King’s own park. It would show the insolence of the heretics upon whom the Privy Seal relied, and it might prove, too, the strength of the Old Faith in the stronghold of the City.

Henry rated violently. It put him to shame, he repeated many times. “Brawling beneath my face, cries in my ears, and the smell of bloodshed in my nose.”

Norfolk repeated dully that the Protestants were wondrous insolent. But Cromwell pointed out with a genial amusement: “My Lord Duke should have housed the City men within the palace. Cat will fight with dog the world over if you set them together.”

The Duke answered malignantly:

===Chinese===
克伦威尔道：

“确然，总得有人手，才压得住您北边各郡的教皇派。”

两人对面相峙。让伦敦城的人恰好在国王自己的苑囿里撞上路德派，这本是公爵计中的一着——克伦威尔对此心知肚明。这既能显出御玺大臣所倚仗的异端何其狂妄，也能验证旧教在伦敦城这座堡垒中的实力。

亨利厉声斥骂。这叫他蒙羞，他翻来覆去说了好几遍。“在我眼皮底下斗殴，在我耳边叫嚷，血腥气直灌我的鼻子。”

诺福克木然地重复道，那些路德派狂妄得出奇。克伦威尔却带着一团和气的谐趣指出：“公爵大人本该把城里人安顿在宫墙之内。猫儿和狗儿搁到一处，天下哪里都是要斗的。”

公爵阴毒地答道：


===Original===
“It was fitting the citizens should wait to enter. I would not cumber his Highness’ courtyards. We know not yet that this Lady cometh to be welcomed Queen.”

“Body of God,” the King said with a new violence: “do ye prate of these matters?” His heavy jaws threatened like a dog’s. “Hast thou set lousy knaves debating of these?”

Norfolk answered darkly that it had been treated of in the Council last night.

“My Council! My Council!” The King seemed to bay out the words. “There shall some mothers’ sons rue this!”

Norfolk muttered that he had spoken of it with no man not a Councillor. The King’s Highness’ self had moved first in this.

Henry suddenly waved both hands at the sky.

===Chinese===
“市民们原该候着入宫。我不愿搅扰陛下的庭院。这位夫人来日是否迎立为王后，眼下犹未可知哩。”

“上帝的身体，”国王说着，火气又添一层：“尔等竟拿这些事来嚼舌？”他沉重的双颌像狗一般威吓着。“汝竟叫一帮生虱子的恶棍去议论这些么？”

诺福克阴沉地答道，此事昨夜已在枢密院里议过了。

“我的枢密院！我的枢密院！”国王仿佛吠出这几个字。“总有人家的儿子要为这事懊悔！”

诺福克咕哝道，此事除枢密院诸勋之外，他未同任何人谈起；头一个动议的，正是国王陛下御躬自己。

亨利猛地朝天空挥动双手。


===Original===
“Take you good order,” he said heavily into the lean and yellow face of the Duke. “Marshal these ceremonies fitly from henceforth. Let nothing lack. Get you gone.” An end must be made of talk and gossip. The rumour of last night’s Council must appear an idle tale, a falsehood of despairing Papists. “The Queen cometh,” he said.

With the droop of the Duke’s long arms his hat seemed to brush the stones, his head fell on his chest. It was finished.

He had seen so many things go that he loved. And now this old woman with her Germans, her heresies﻿—her children doubtless﻿—meant the final downfall of the Old Order in his day. It would return, but he would never see it. And under Cromwell’s sardonic gaze his head hung limply, and his eyes filled with hot and blinding drops. His face trembled like that of a very old man.

The King had thrust his hand through Cromwell’s arm, and, with a heavy familiarity as if he would make him forget the Council of last night, he was drawing him away towards the water-gate. He turned his head over his shoulder and repeated balefully:

“The Queen cometh.”

As he did so his eye fell upon a man tugging at the bridle of a mule that had a woman on its back. He passed on with his minister.

===Chinese===
“尔好生整饬，”他冲着公爵那张精瘦蜡黄的脸沉声说道。“自今而后，把这等典礼调度妥帖，一应勿使有缺。去罢。”闲话与流言必须一概了结。昨夜枢密院的传闻，只许显作无聊的闲谈、绝望的教皇派造出的谣言。“王后来了，”他说。

公爵长长的手臂一垂，帽子仿佛扫着了石地，头也垂落在胸前。一切都完了。

他眼见多少心爱之物次第逝去。如今这个老女人，带着她的德意志人、她的异端——无疑还有她的儿女——意味着旧秩序在他这一代的最终倾覆。它终会回来，可他永远见不着了。在克伦威尔讥诮的注视下，他的头颓然垂着，眼里充满了滚烫而迷目的大滴泪水。他的脸抖动着，像极了垂暮老人的脸。

国王已把手臂挽进克伦威尔的臂弯，带着一种沉重亲昵——仿佛要叫他忘掉昨夜的枢密院——引他往水门方向走去。他扭头回望，阴狠地又说了一遍：

“王后来了。”

就在这时，他瞥见一个汉子正在拽一头骡子的辔头，骡背上骑着个女人。他同他的大臣径自走了过去。

### V / 五

===Original===
In turning, Norfolk came against them at the very end of the path. The man’s green coat was spotted with filth, one of his sleeves was torn off and dangled about his heel. The mule’s knees were cut, and the woman trembled with her hidden face and shrinking figure.

They made him choke with rage and fear. Some other procession might have come against these vagabonds, and the blame would have been his. It disgusted him that they were within a yard of himself.

“Are there no side paths?” he asked harshly.

Culpepper blazed round upon him:

“How might I know? Why sent you no guide?” His vivid red beard was matted into tails, his face pallid and as if blazing with rage. The porter had turned them loose into the empty garden.

“Kat is sore hurt,” he mumbled, half in tears. “Her arm is welly broken.” He glared at the Duke. “Care you no more for your own blood and kin?”
===Chinese===
转身之间，诺福克在小径尽头正好迎面撞上他们。那汉子的绿外衣溅满污秽，一只袖子整个撕落下来，在他脚跟边晃荡。骡子两膝磨破了皮，那女子藏起脸、缩着身子，兀自发抖。

他们叫他怒塞胸臆，又叫他心惊胆战。迎头撞上这些流浪汉的，本可能是别的仪仗，那罪名便要落在他头上。他们离他本人不出一码，这叫他满心作呕。

“就没有旁路么？”他厉声问。

卡尔佩珀勃然回身，冲着他嚷：

“我怎么会知道？你干吗不派个向导？”他那部鲜红的胡须纠结成一绺绺，脸色苍白，却又像烧着怒火。守门人已把他们撒手放进了这座空园子。

“凯特伤得厉害，”他半含着泪咕哝，“她的胳膊差不多断了。”他瞪视公爵。“你对自己的骨血至亲就一点不上心么？”


===Original===
Norfolk asked:

“Who is your Kat? Can I know all the Howards?”

Culpepper snarled:

“Aye, we may trust you not to succour your brother’s children.”

The Duke said:

“Why, she shall back to the palace. They shall comfort her.”
===Chinese===
诺福克问：

“你那个凯特是谁？霍华德家的人，我岂能个个都识得？”

卡尔佩珀狞声道：

“是了，你兄弟的儿女休想得你救助——这一点我们尽可信得过。”

公爵道：

“嗐，让她回宫里去就是。自会有人抚慰她。”


===Original===
“That shall she not,” Culpepper flustered. “Sh’ath her father’s commands to hasten to Dover.”

The Duke caught her eyes in the fur hood that hid her face like a Moorish woman’s veil. They were large, grey and arresting beneath the pallor of her forehead. They looked at him, questioning and judging.

“Wilt not come to my lodging?” he asked.

“Aye, will I,” came a little muffled by the fur.

“That shall she not,” Culpepper repeated.

The Duke looked at him with gloomy and inquisitive surprise.
===Chinese===
“那可不行，”卡尔佩珀慌了神，“她奉着她父亲的严命，要赶去多佛。”

公爵的目光，撞进皮兜帽下她那双眼睛——那兜帽遮着她的脸，像摩尔女子的面纱。在她苍白的额头底下，那双眼又大又灰，慑人心神。它们望着他，探问着，也裁断着。

“可愿到我的寓所去？”他问。

“愿意，”皮毛里传出一声，微微发闷。

“那可不行，”卡尔佩珀又说了一遍。

公爵望着他，又阴沉、又狐疑、又惊讶。


===Original===
“Aye, I am her mother’s cousin,” he said. “I fend for her, which you have never done. Her father’s house is burnt by rioters, and her men are joined in the pillaging. But I’ll warrant you knew it not.”

Katharine Howard with her sound hand was trying to unfasten her hood, hastily and eagerly.

“Wilt come?” the Duke asked hurriedly. “This must be determined.”

Culpepper hissed: “By the bones of St. Nairn she shall not.” She lifted her maimed hand involuntarily, and, at the sear of pain, her eyes closed. Immediately Culpepper was beside her knees, supporting her with his arms and muttering sounds of endearment and despair.

The Duke, hearing behind him the swish pad of heavy soft shoes, as if a bear were coming over the pavement, faced the King.
===Chinese===
“是了，我是她母家的表亲，”他说，“一直是我照拂她，你从来不曾。她父亲的宅子叫乱民烧了，家里的男丁还入了伙劫掠。不过我敢保你压根儿不知道。”

凯瑟琳·霍华德用她那只完好的手，急急地、热切地解着兜帽。

“你到底来不来？”公爵急急追问，“这事须有个决断。”

卡尔佩珀嘶声道：“凭圣奈恩的骨头起誓，她不能去。”她不由自主地抬起那只伤残的手，一阵灼痛袭来，双眼随即闭紧。卡尔佩珀立刻扑到她膝边，双臂扶住她，喃喃吐出一串又怜爱又绝望的话来。

公爵听得身后软底重鞋嚓嚓踏响，仿佛一头熊正走上石面，便回身面对国王。


===Original===
“This is my brother’s child,” he said. “She is sore hurt. I would not leave her like a dog,” and he asked the King’s pardon.

“Why, God forbid,” the King said. “Your Grace shall succour her.” Culpepper had his back to them, caring nothing for either in his passion. Henry said: “Aye, take good care for her,” and passed on with Privy Seal on his arm.

The Duke heaved a sigh of relief. But he remembered again that Anne of Cleves was coming, and his black anger that Cromwell should thus once again have the King thrown back to him came out in his haughty and forbidding tone to Culpepper:

“Take thou my niece to the water-gate. I shall send women to her.” He hastened frostily up the path to be gone before Henry should return again.

Culpepper resolved that he would take barge before ever the Duke could send. But the mule slewed right across the terrace; his cousin grasped the brute’s neck and her loosened hood began to fall back from her head.
===Chinese===
“这是我兄弟的孩子，”他说，“她伤得不轻。我总不能把她像条狗似的丢下。”说着，向国王请罪。

“嗐，上天不容，”国王说，“公爵自当抚慰她。”卡尔佩珀背对着他们两人，盛怒之下对哪一个也浑不在意。亨利道：“是了，好好照看她。”便挽着御玺大臣的手臂走过去了。

公爵宽慰地舒了一大口气。但他又想起克利维斯的安妮就要驾到，想起克伦威尔竟然又能这样再一次把国王攥回自己手里，一腔黑沉沉的怒火，便化作对卡尔佩珀倨傲冷峻的声口发泄出来：

“领着我的侄女到水门去。我会派妇人去照应她。”他冷若冰霜地疾步上径，要在亨利再折回来之前先走为妙。

卡尔佩珀打定主意，要抢在公爵派人之前先雇船走。可那骡子偏偏横甩过露台，他表妹一把抱住畜生的脖颈，松开的兜帽开始从她头上向后滑落。


===Original===
The King, standing twenty yards away, with his hand shaking Cromwell’s shoulder, was saying:

“See you how grey I grow.”

The words came hot into a long harangue. He had been urging that he must have more money for his works at Calais. He was agitated because a French chalk pit outside the English lines had been closed to his workmen. They must bring chalk from Dover at a heavy cost for barges and balingers. This was what it was to quarrel with France.

Cromwell had his mind upon widening the breach with France. He said that a poll tax might be levied on the subjects of Charles and Francis then in London. There were goldsmiths, woolstaplers, horse merchants, whoremasters, painters, musicians and vintners.﻿ ﻿…

The King’s eyes had wandered to the grey river, and then from a deep and moody abstraction he had blurted out those words.

Henry was very grey, and his face, inanimate and depressed, made him seem worn and old enough. Cromwell was not set to deny it. The King had his glass.﻿ ﻿…
===Chinese===
国王立在二十码开外，一只手摇着克伦威尔的肩膀，正说道：

“你瞧我都灰白成什么样了。”

这句话脱口便热辣辣地汇入一场长篇大论。他一直在力争：加来的工事还须添拨银钱。他躁动不安，因为英军防线外一座法兰西人的白垩矿场，已经不许他的工人们采掘。他们只得从多佛转运白垩，驳船与帆桨轻船的花费不赀。这便是与法兰西交恶的滋味。

克伦威尔心里盘算的，却是把与法兰西的裂口再扯大些。他说，不妨向其时客居伦敦的查理与弗朗索瓦两家君王的臣民，征收一笔人头税。他们中间有金匠、羊毛商、马贩子、妓馆主、画师、乐师，还有酒商。……

国王的目光游移到灰色的河面上，随后，从一种深沉郁结的出神里，他冲口说出了那句话。

亨利的确灰白了，那张脸死气沉沉、郁郁寡欢，衬得他够憔悴、够老的了。克伦威尔无意否认。国王自有他的镜子。……


===Original===
He sighed a little and began:

“The heavy years take their toll.”

Henry caught him up suddenly:

“Why, no. It is the heavy days, the endless nights. You can sleep, you.” But him, the King, incessant work was killing.

“You see, you see, how this world will never let me rest.” In the long, black nights he started from dozing. When he took time to dandle his little son a panic would come over him because he remembered that he lived among traitors and had no God he could pray to. He had no mind to work.﻿ ﻿…

Cromwell said that there was no man in England could outwork his King.
===Chinese===
他微微叹了口气，开口道：

“沉重的年岁，讨着它们的债。”

亨利猛地打断他：

“嗐，不。是沉重的白昼，是无尽的黑夜。你倒睡得着，你这个人。”而他，国王，正叫无休无止的操劳活活拖垮。

“你瞧啊，你瞧啊，这尘世怎么也不肯让我安歇。”在漫长漆黑的夜里，他常从假寐中惊起。偶得闲抱一抱他的小儿子，恐慌也会兜头罩下，因为他记起自己身处叛贼中间，又没有一个可以祷告的上帝。他无心劳碌。……

克伦威尔说，英格兰再没有一个人，比他的国王更能耐劳苦。


===Original===
“There is no man in England can love him.” His distracted eyes fell upon the woman on the mule. “Happy he whom a King never saw and who never saw King,” he muttered.

The beast, inspired with a blind hatred of Culpepper, was jibbing across the terrace, close at hand. Henry became abstractedly interested in the struggle. The woman swayed forward over her knees.

“Your lady faints,” he called to Culpepper.

In his muddled fury the man began once again trying to hold her on the animal. It was backing slowly towards a stone seat in the balustrade, and man and woman swayed and tottered together.

The King said:

“Let her descend and rest upon the seat.”
===Chinese===
“英格兰也没有一个人能爱他。”他涣散的目光落在骡背上的女子身上。“那人真有福啊——国王从不曾见他，他也从不曾见国王，”他低声道。

那畜生怀着对卡尔佩珀一股盲目的仇恨，正在近旁的露台上甩头尥蹶、赖着不走。亨利一时出神，竟对这场人兽缠斗有了兴致。那女子身子前倾，折伏在膝上。

“你的女伴要晕过去了，”他朝卡尔佩珀喊道。

那汉子一腔昏乱的怒火，又动手要把她扶稳在牲口背上。骡子正缓缓退向栏壁间的一张石凳，男女二人随着它一同摇晃趔趄。

国王道：

“叫她下来，在凳上歇一歇。”


===Original===
His mind was swinging back already to his own heavy sorrows. On the stone seat the woman’s head lay back upon the balustrade, her eyes were closed and her face livid to the sky. Culpepper, using his teeth to the finger ends, tore the gloves from his hands.

Henry drew Cromwell towards the gatehouse. He had it dimly in his mind to send one of his gentlemen to the assistance of that man and woman.

“Aye, teach me to sleep at night,” he said. “It is you who make me work.”

“It is for your Highness’ dear sake.”

“Aye, for my sake,” the King said angrily. He burst into a sudden invective: “Thou hast murdered a many men﻿ ﻿… for my sake. Thou hast found out plots that were no plots: old men hate me, old mothers, wives, maidens, harlots.﻿ ﻿… Why, if I be damned at the end thou shalt escape, for what thou didst thou didst for my sake? Shall it be that?” He breathed heavily. “My sins are thy glory.”

They reached the long wall of the gatehouse and turned mechanically. A barge at the river steps was disgorging musicians with lutes like half melons set on staves, horns that opened bell mouths to the sky, and cymbals that clanged in the rushing of the river. With his eyes upon them Henry said: “A common man may commonly choose his bedfellow.” They had reminded him of the Queen for whose welcome they had been commanded.
===Chinese===
他的心思早已荡回自己的重重忧愁里去了。石凳上，那女子头向后仰在栏壁上，双眼闭着，一张青灰的脸对着天空。卡尔佩珀用牙齿咬住手套的指尖，把它们从手上撕了下来。

亨利拉着克伦威尔往门楼走去。他心里朦朦胧胧想着，要派一名内廷侍从去扶那对男女一把。

“是了，那便教教我夜里怎么入睡，”他说，“是你逼着我操劳。”

“这全是为了陛下至重的缘故。”

“是了，为了我，”国王怒道。他陡然破口大骂起来：“你害了数不清的人命……全是为了我。你查出了那些根本不是阴谋的阴谋：老头子恨我，老母亲、妻子、少女、荡妇。……嗐，若我到头来下了地狱，你倒脱得了身，因为你所做的一切，全是为了我？天下可有这样的道理？”他喘着粗气。“我的罪孽，就是你的荣光。”

他们走到门楼的长墙下，机械地折转身子。河阶旁一条驳船正往下卸乐师：鲁特琴像安在琴杆上的半个甜瓜，号角敞着喇叭口朝天张开，铙钹在奔流的河水声里铿然作响。亨利望着他们说道：“寻常人寻常总能自己挑床伴。”他们让他想起了那位王后——这些乐师原是奉命迎她驾的。


===Original===
Cromwell swept his hand composedly round the half horizon that held the palace, the grey river and the inlands.

“Your Highness may choose among ten thousand,” he answered.

The sound of a horn blown faintly to test it within the gatehouse, the tinkle of a lutestring, brought to the King’s lips: “Aye. Bring me music that shall charm my thoughts. You cannot do it.”

“A Queen is in the nature of a defence, a pledge, a cement, the keystone of a bulwark,” Cromwell said. “We know now our friends and our foes. You may rest from this onwards.”

He spoke earnestly: This was the end of a long struggle. The King should have his rest.

They moved back along the terrace. The woman’s head still lay back, her chin showed pointed and her neck, long, thin and supple. Culpepper was bending over her, sprinkling water out of his cap upon her upturned face.
===Chinese===
克伦威尔从容地一挥手，扫过半幅天际，那里笼着宫阙、灰色的河流与内陆的乡野。

“陛下尽可在一万人里挑，”他答道。

门楼里一支号角轻轻试吹了一声，鲁特琴弦叮的一响，引得国王开了口：“是了。给我弄些能镇住我念头的音乐来吧。这你是办不到的。”

“一位王后，本质上便是一道防御、一宗抵押、一剂黏合，是一道壁垒的拱心石，”克伦威尔说，“如今谁是友、谁是敌，我们认清了。自此往后，陛下尽可安歇了。”

他说得恳切：一场旷日持久的角力，至此终局。国王合该得到他的安歇。

他们沿露台折返回来。那女子的头仍向后仰着，露出尖尖的下巴和修长、纤瘦而柔韧的脖颈。卡尔佩珀正俯在她身上，从帽子里掬水，洒向她仰起的脸。


===Original===
The King said to Cromwell: “Who is that wench?” and, in the same tone: “Aye, you are a great comforter. We shall see how the cat jumps,” and then, answering his own question, “Norfolk’s niece?”

His body automatically grew upright, the limp disappeared from his gait and he moved sturdily and gently towards them.

Culpepper faced round like a wild cat from a piece of meat, but seeing the great hulk, the intent and friendly eyes, the gold collar over the chest, the heavy hands, and the great feet that appeared to hold down the very stones of the terrace, he stood rigid in a pose of disturbance.

“Why do ye travel?” the King asked. “This shall be Katharine Howard?”

Culpepper’s hushed but harsh voice answered that they came out of Lincolnshire on the Norfolk border. This was the Lord Edmund’s daughter.
===Chinese===
国王对克伦威尔道：“那个丫头是谁？”接着又用同样的声调说：“是了，你可真会宽慰人。且看这猫往哪边跳。”然后自答自问道：“诺福克的侄女？”

他的身躯不由自主地挺直起来，步态里的跛行消失无踪，他稳健而轻柔地朝他们走去。

卡尔佩珀像护食的野猫般霍地回过身来，可一看见那庞大的身躯、专注而和善的眼睛、胸前金项圈、沉甸甸的大手，还有那双仿佛能把露台上的石面都踏住的大脚，他便僵在原地，摆出一副慌乱失措的架势。

“你们打远路来？”国王问，“这位想必就是凯瑟琳·霍华德了？”

卡尔佩珀压低却依旧粗厉的嗓音答道：他们从林肯郡诺福克边地来。这是埃德蒙·霍华德勋爵的女儿。


===Original===
“I have never seen her,” the King said.

“Sh’ath never been in this town.”

The King laughed: “Why, poor wench!”

“Sh’ath been well schooled,” Culpepper answered proudly, “hath had mastern, hath sung, hath danced, hath your Latin and your Greek.﻿ ﻿… Hath ten daughters, her father.”

The King laughed again: “Why, poor man!”

“Poorer than ever now,” Culpepper muttered. Katharine Howard stirred uneasily and his face shot round to her. “Rioters have brent his only house and wasted all his sheep.”
===Chinese===
“我从没见过她，”国王说。

“她从来没进过这座京城。”

国王笑了：“嗐，可怜的丫头！”

“她受过很好的调教，”卡尔佩珀骄傲地答道，“延请过先生，唱过歌，跳过舞，还学过你们的拉丁文、希腊文。……她父亲膝下有十个女儿呢。”

国王又笑了：“嗐，可怜的人！”

“如今比哪会儿都更可怜了，”卡尔佩珀咕哝道。凯瑟琳·霍华德不安地动了动，他的脸倏地转向她。“乱民烧了他仅有的一座宅子，毁尽了全部的羊。”


===Original===
The King frowned heavily: “Anan? Who rioted?”

“These knaves that love not our giving our ploughlands to sheep,” Culpepper said. “They say they starved through it. Yet ’tis the only way to wealth. I had all my wealth by it. By now ’tis well gone, but I go to the wars to get me more.”

“Rioters?” the King said again, heavily.

“ ’Twas a small tulzie﻿—a score of starved yeomen here and there. I killed seven. The others were they that were hanged at Norwich.﻿ ﻿… But the barns were brent, the sheep gone, and the house down and the servants fled. I am her cousin of the mother’s side. Of as good a strain as Howards be.”

Henry, with his eyes still upon them, beckoned behind his back for Cromwell to come. A score or so of poor yeomen, hinds and women, cast out of their tenancies that wool might be grown for the Netherlands weavers, starving, desperate, and seeing no trace of might and order in their hidden lands, had banded, broken a few hedges and burnt a few barns before the posse of the country could come together and take them.

The King had not heard of it or had forgotten it, because such risings were so frequent. His brows came down into portentous and bulging knots, his eyes were veiled and threatening towards the woman’s face. He had conceived that a great rebellion had been hidden from his knowledge.
===Chinese===
国王重重皱起眉头：“嗯？什么？是谁作的乱？”

“就是那起子恶徒，见不得咱们把耕地改作牧场，”卡尔佩珀说，“他们说因此挨了饿。可这是发财的唯一门路。我那份家私全是这样挣来的。如今耗得差不多了，我便去打仗，再挣一份来。”

“乱民？”国王又说了一遍，声音沉重。

“不过是一场小械斗——东一处西一处，二十来个挨饿的自耕农。我杀了七个。其余的，就是在诺里奇问绞的那一批。……可谷仓烧了，羊没了，宅子塌了，仆人逃散了。我是她母家的表亲。血统与霍华德家一样高贵。”

亨利眼睛仍望着他们，却在背后招手，唤克伦威尔过来。二十来个穷苦的自耕农、雇工和妇人，被从佃地上撵了出来，好让地里长出羊毛，供给尼德兰的织工；他们挨着饿，走投无路，又在自己藏身的乡野间看不见半点权势与秩序的踪影，于是啸聚起来，捣破了几道树篱，烧了几座谷仓——郡里的民团还没来得及集结把他们拿下，事已至此。

国王不曾听说过这事，或者早已忘了，因为这类暴动实在太过频繁。他的眉毛拧成骇人的疙瘩压了下来，目光蒙着威胁，逼向那女子的脸。他心里已经断定：一场大叛乱竟瞒着他发生了。


===Original===
She raised her head and shrieked at the sight of him, half started to her feet, and once more sank down on the bench, clasping at her cousin’s hand. He said:

“Peace, Kate, it is the King.”

She answered: “No, no,” and covered her face with her hands.

Henry bent a little towards her, indulgent, amused, and gentle as if to a child.

“I am Harry,” he said.
===Chinese===
她一看见他，便抬起头尖叫起来，半撑着要站起来，随即又跌坐回凳上，一把抓住她表兄的手。他道：

“别怕，凯特，这是国王。”

她答道：“不，不，”便用双手掩住了脸。

亨利朝她微微俯下身来，纵容着、觉着有趣，又温存得像对着一个孩子。

“我是哈里，”他说。


===Original===
She muttered:

“There was a great crowd, a great cry. One smote me on the arm. And then this quiet here.”

She uncovered her face and sat looking at the ground. Her furs were all grey, she had had none new for four years, and they were tight to her young body that had grown into them. The roses embroidered on her glove had come unstitched, and, against the steely grey of the river, her face in its whiteness had the tint of mother of pearl and an expression of engrossed and grievous absence.

“I have fared on foul ways this journey,” she said.

“Thy father’s barns we will build again,” the King answered. “You shall have twice the sheep to your dower. Show me your eyes.”

“I had not thought to have seen the King so stern,” she answered.
===Chinese===
她喃喃道：

“方才人好多，闹哄哄的。有人打了我的胳膊。再后来，就是这里的一片安静。”

她放开双手露出脸，坐着望向地面。她一身裘皮已经通体灰旧，四年不曾添置新的，紧紧裹着她那具已然长成的年轻身体。手套上绣的玫瑰绽了线，衬着钢灰色的河水，她那张苍白的脸泛着珍珠母般的光泽，神情全然出神，带着沉重的怅惘若失。

“这一路，我走的是污糟的路，”她说。

“你父亲的谷仓，我们定要重新盖起，”国王答道，“你的嫁妆里，羊群加倍。抬起眼睛给我瞧瞧。”

“我没想到会见到这么严峻的国王，”她答道。


===Original===
Culpepper caught at the mule’s bridle.

“Y’ are mad,” he muttered. “Let us begone.”

“Nay, in my day,” the King answered, “y’ad found me more than kind.”

She raised her eyes to his face, steadfast, enquiring and unconcerned. He bent his great bulk downwards and kissed her upon the temple.

“Be welcome to this place.” He smiled with a pleasure in his own affability and because, since his beard had pricked her, she rubbed her cheek. Culpepper said:

“Come away. We stay the King’s Highness.”
===Chinese===
卡尔佩珀一把抓住骡子的笼头。

“你疯了，”他低声道，“咱们走吧。”

“不，想当年，”国王答道，“你会发现我何止于仁厚。”

她抬眼望向他的脸，目光沉定、探询而毫不在意。他俯下庞大的身躯，吻在她的鬓角上。

“欢迎你到这地方来。”他笑了，既为着自己的和蔼可亲而得意，也因为她挨了胡须的扎，正揉着脸颊。卡尔佩珀道：

卡尔佩珀道：“走吧。咱们耽搁国王陛下了。”


===Original===
Henry said: “Bide ye here.” He wished to hear what Cromwell might say of these Howards, and he took him down the terrace.

Culpepper bent over her with his mouth opened to whisper.

“I am weary,” she said. “Set me a saddle cushion behind my shoulders.”

He whispered hurriedly:

“I do not like this place.”

“I like it well. Shall we not see brave shews?”
===Chinese===
亨利道：“你们且待在这儿。”他想听听克伦威尔会怎样评说这些霍华德家的人，便携着他沿露台走下去了。

卡尔佩珀朝她俯下身，张着嘴要耳语。

“我乏了，”她说，“在我背后肩胛后垫块鞍垫吧。”

他急急地低声道：

“我不喜欢这地方。”

“我倒喜欢得很。咱们不就能瞧上热闹的好戏了么？”


===Original===
“The mule did stumble on the threshold.”

“I marked it not. The King did bid us bide here.”

She had once more laid her head back on the stone balustrade.

“If thou lovest me.﻿ ﻿…” he whispered. It enraged and confused him to have to speak low. He could not think of any words.

She answered unconcernedly:

“If thou lovest my bones﻿ ﻿… they ache and they ache.”
===Chinese===
“骡子在门槛上绊了一下。”

“我没留意。是国王叫咱们待在这儿的。”

她又把头向后靠在石栏壁上。

“你若爱我的话。……”他低声道。不得不压低嗓音，这叫他又恼又乱。他想不出一句可说的话来。

她漫不经心地答道：

“你若爱我这把骨头……它们疼啊，一直地疼。”


===Original===
“I have sold farms to buy thee gowns,” he said desperately.

“I never asked it,” she answered coldly.

Henry was saying:

“Ah, Princes take as is brought them by others. Poor men be commonly at their own choice.” His voice had a sort of patient regret. “Why brought ye not such a wench?”

Cromwell answered that in Lincoln, they said, she had been a coin that would not bear ringing.

“You do not love her house,” the King said. “Y’ had better have brought me such a one.”
===Chinese===
“我卖掉了田庄给你添置衣裳，”他绝望地说。

“我从来不曾求过你，”她冷冷地答道。

亨利那边正说着：

“唉，君王们，只能领受旁人送到眼前的人。穷汉子倒通常能自己作主。”他的声音里带着一种隐忍的憾意。“你们怎么不给我送这样一个丫头？”

克伦威尔答道：人家说，在林肯的时候，她是一枚经不起敲响的钱。

“你不喜欢她那一族，”国王说，“你原该给我带来这么一个的。”


===Original===
Cromwell answered that his meaning was she had been won by others. The King’s Highness should have her for a wink.

Henry raised his shoulders with a haughty and angry shrug. Such a quarry was below his stooping. He craved no light loves.

“I do not miscall the wench,” Cromwell answered. She was as her kind. The King’s Highness should find them all of a make in England.

“Y’ are foul-mouthed,” Henry said negligently. “ ’Tis a well-spoken wench. You shall find her a place in the Lady Mary’s house.”

Cromwell smiled, and made a note upon a piece of paper that he pulled from his pocket.
===Chinese===
克伦威尔答道，他的意思是：她早叫旁人得去了。陛下只消一眨眼工夫，便能得着她。

亨利傲然而愠怒地耸了耸肩。这样的猎物，不值得他俯身。他不稀罕轻浮的爱恋。

“我并非辱骂那丫头，”克伦威尔答道。她跟她那一类一个样。陛下在英格兰会发现，她们全是同一副坯子。

“你满嘴污言，”亨利淡淡地说，“这是个谈吐斯文的丫头。你去给她在玛丽公主府里安排个位置。”

克伦威尔一笑，从口袋里抽出一张纸，记了一笔。


===Original===
Culpepper, his arms jerking angularly, was creaking out:

“Come away, a’ God’s name. By all our pacts. By all our secret vows.”

“Ay thou didst vow and didst vow,” she said with a bitter weariness. “What hast to shew? I have slept in filthy beds all this journey. Speak the King well. He shall make thee at a word.”

He spat out at her.

“Is thine eye cocked up to that level?﻿ ﻿… I am very hot, very choleric. Thou hast seen me. Thou shalt not live. I will slay thee. I shall do such things as make the moon turn bloody red.”

“Aye art thou there?” she answered coldly. “Ye have me no longer upon lone heaths and moors. Mend thy tongue. Here I have good friends.”
===Chinese===
卡尔佩珀双臂僵直地抽动着，嘎声嘎气地挤出话来：

“走吧，以上帝之名。凭咱们的一切盟约。凭咱们的一切密誓。”

“是了，你赌了又赌咒，”她说，带着刻毒的倦怠，“你可拿出什么来了？这一路我睡的全是脏床。去奉承奉承国王吧。他一句话就能成全你。”

他冲着她啐出话来。

“你的眼睛竟抬到那个高度去了？……我这人火性大，脾气暴。你是见识过的。你活不成了。我要杀了你。我要做出些事来，教月亮都变成血红。”

“是了，你又来这一套了？”她冷冷地答道，“你们再不能把我撂在孤零零的荒原野泽上了。收敛点你的舌头。在这里，我有好朋友。”


===Original===
Suddenly he began to entreat:

“Thy mule did stumble﻿—an evil omen. Come away, come away. I know well thou lovest me.”

“I know well I love thee too well,” she answered, as if in scorn of herself.

“Come away to thy father.”

“Why what a bother is this,” she said. “Thou wouldst to the wars to get thee gold? Thou wouldst trail a pike? Thou canst do little without the ear of some captain. Here is the great captain of them all.”
===Chinese===
忽然他开始哀求起来：

“你的骡子绊了跤——不祥之兆。走吧，走吧。我知道你是爱我的。”

“我也知道，我把你爱得太深了，”她答道，像是在鄙夷她自己。

“回你父亲那儿去吧。”

“嗐，这还有什么可烦的，”她说，“你想去打仗挣金子？想去扛长矛？得不到哪位队长的垂青，你就难有作为。这儿有的，正是他们众人之中最伟大的统帅。”


===Original===
“I dare not speak here,” he muttered huskily. “But this King.﻿ ﻿…” He paused and added swiftly: “He is of an ill omen to all Katharines.”

“Why, he shall give me his old gloves to darn,” she laughed. “Fond knave, this King standeth on a mountain a league high. A King shall take notice of one for the duration of a raindrop’s fall. Then it is done. One may make oneself ere it reach the ground, or never. Besides, ’tis a well-spoken elder. ’Tis the spit of our grandfather Culpepper.”

When Henry came hurrying back, engrossed, to send Culpepper and the mule to the gatehouse for a guide, she laughed gently for pleasure.

Culpepper said tremulously: “She hath her father’s commands to hasten to Dover.”

“Her father taketh and giveth commands from me,” Henry answered, and his glove flicked once more towards the gate. He had turned his face away before Culpepper’s hand grasped convulsively at his dagger and he had Katharine Howard at his side sweeping back towards Cromwell.

She asked, confidingly and curiously: “Who is that lord?” and, after his answer, she mused, “He is no friend to Howards.”
===Chinese===
“在这儿我不敢说，”他嗓音沙哑地咕哝道，“可这个国王。……”他顿了顿，又飞快补上一句：“他对所有叫凯瑟琳的人，都是个凶兆。”

“嗐，他顶多会把他旧手套赏给我缝补，”她笑了，“痴心的蠢货，这位国王站的地方，是一里格高的山头。国王留意一个人，只消一滴雨落下的工夫。过后便万事皆休。人要么赶在雨点落地之前成事，要么永无机会。再说，这是位谈吐斯文的长者。活脱脱是我们外祖父卡尔佩珀的模子里刻出来的。”

待到亨利心事重重地匆匆折回，打发卡尔佩珀和骡子往门楼去寻向导时，她欢喜得轻轻笑了。

卡尔佩珀声音发颤地说：“她奉有她父亲的严命，要赶紧去多佛。”

“她父亲受命、传命，都得凭我，”亨利答道，手套又朝大门一拂。趁卡尔佩珀的手还没痉挛地攥住匕首，他已转过脸去，携着凯瑟琳·霍华德在身侧，风也似的折回克伦威尔那里去了。

她信赖而好奇地问：“那位大人是谁？”听完他的回答，她沉吟道：“他可不是霍华德家的朋友。”


===Original===
“Nay, that man taketh his friends among mine,” he answered. He stopped to regard her, his face one heavy and indulgent smile. The garter on his knee, broad and golden, showed her the words: “*Y pense*”; the collars moved up and down on his immense chest, the needlework of roses was so fine that she wondered how many women had sat up how many nights to finish it: but the man was grey and homely.

“I know none of your ways here,” she said.

“Never let fear blanch thy cheeks till we are no more thy friend,” he reassured her. He composed one of his gallant speeches:

“Here lives for thee nothing but joy.” Pleasurable hopes should be her comrades while the jolly sun shone, and sweet content at night her bedfellow.﻿ ﻿…

He handed her to the care of the Lord Cromwell to take her to the Lady Mary’s lodgings. It was unfitting that she should walk with him, and, with his heavy and bearlike gait, swinging his immense shoulders, he preceded them up the broad path.
===Chinese===
“不然，那人择友，专从我的人里头择，”他答道。他停下脚步端详她，整张脸挂着一个沉甸甸、宠溺的笑。膝上那条宽宽的金色袜带，向她显出那句铭文：“*Y pense*”〔法语，意谓“心念于此”〕；项圈在他偌大的胸膛上起伏，玫瑰的刺绣细密精美，叫她暗自思量：多少女子熬了多少个夜晚才绣成它——可这男人灰白苍老、相貌平平。

“你们这里的规矩，我一概不知，”她说。

“只要我们还做你的朋友，就断不许恐惧漂白你的脸颊，”他宽慰她道。他缀成一篇自己惯常的雍容辞令：

“在这里，为你存着的唯有欢愉。”当快活的太阳照着的时候，愉悦的希望该做她的同伴；到了夜里，甜甜的知足该做她的床伴。……

他把她托付给克伦威尔勋爵照管，领她去玛丽公主的居所。她不宜与他同行，于是他迈着沉重如熊的步子，甩动偌大的双肩，在前头引路，走上宽阔的大道。

### VI / 六

===Original===
Cromwell watched the King’s great back with an attentive smile. He said, ironically, that he was her ladyship’s servant.

“I would ye were,” she answered. “They say you love not those that I love.”

“I would have you not heed what men say,” he answered, grimly. “I am douce to those that be of goodwill to his Highness. Those that hate me are his ill-wishers.”

“Then the times are evil,” she said, “for they are many.”

She added suddenly, as if she could not keep a prudent silence:

“I am for the Old Faith in the Old Way. You have hanged many dear friends of mine whose souls I pray for.”

===Chinese===
克伦威尔含着一副专注的微笑，望着国王那伟岸的背影。他语带讥诮地说，自己乃是夫人麾下效劳的仆役。

“我倒愿你是呢。”她答道，“人家说，凡我所爱的人，你一概不爱。”

“我劝您莫把旁人的话放在心上。”他冷然答道，“凡对陛下怀善意的人，我都温厚相待。恨我的人，便是存心跟陛下过不去。”

“那这年月可就险恶了，”她说，“因为这样的人多得很。”

她突然又添了一句，仿佛一场审慎的沉默再难守住：

“我信的是旧道里的旧教。你绞死过我许多亲爱的朋友，他们的灵魂，我至今还代为祈祷。”


===Original===
He looked at her attentively.

She had a supple, long body, a fair-tinted face, fair and reddish hair, and eyes that had a glint of almond green﻿—but her cheeks were flushed and her eyes sparkled. She was so intent upon speaking her mind that she had forgotten the pain of her arm. She thought that she must have said enough to anger this brewer’s son. But he answered only:

“I think you have never been in the King’s court”﻿—and, from his tranquil manner, she realised very suddenly that this man was not the dirt beneath her feet.

She had never been in the King’s court; she had never, indeed, been out of the North parts. Her father had always been a very poor man, with an ancient castle and a small estate that he had nearly always neglected because it had not paid for the farming. Living men she had never respected﻿—for they seemed to her like wild beasts when she compared them with such of the ancients as Brutus or as Seneca. She had been made love to and threatened by such men as her cousin; she had been made love to and taught Latin by her pedagogues. She was more learned than any man she had ever met﻿—and, thinking upon the heroes of Plutarch, she found the present times despicable. She hardly owed allegiance to the King. Now she had seen him and felt his consciousness of his own power, she was less certain. But the King’s writs had hardly run in the Northern parts. Her menfolk and her mother’s people had hanged their own peasants when they thought fit. She had seen bodies swinging from treetops when she rode hawking. All that she had ever known of the King’s power was when the convent by their castle gates had been thrown out of doors, and then her menfolk, cursing and raging, had sworn that it was the work of Crummock. “Knaves ruled about the King.”

===Chinese===
他专注地打量着她。

她身段柔长，面色白皙，头发金褐带红，眼睛里闪着一抹杏仁绿——只是此刻她双颊烧红，目光灼灼。她一心要吐尽胸中的话，连臂上的疼痛都忘了。她心想，这些话定足以激怒这个酿酒匠的儿子了。他却只答道：

“我想您从未进过宫廷吧。”——从他那份安详自若里，她蓦然醒悟：此人绝非她脚下的尘土。

她从未进过宫廷；说实话，她连北方诸郡都不曾出过。她父亲始终是个穷汉，守着一座古堡和一小片田产；那地力种庄稼入不敷出，他便索性常年荒疏。活人她从来不曾敬重过——与布鲁图斯（Brutus）、塞内加这样的古人相比，他们在她眼里无异于野兽。像她表兄那样的男人向她求过爱、也恫吓过她；她的塾师们向她求过爱、也教过她拉丁文。她的学问胜过她见过的任何一个男人——一想到普鲁塔克（Plutarch）笔下的英雄，她便觉得当今之世不值一哂。她对国王算不上负有什么效忠之谊。如今她见过了他，领教了他那份对自身权势的自觉，心里倒不那么笃定了。况且国王的令状在北方几乎行不通。她的族人和她母族的人，觉得合适时便自行绞死自家的佃农。她骑马放鹰时，见过尸身在树梢上晃荡。她对王权仅有的一点见识，便是她们城堡门前那座女修道院被扫地出门的那一遭；当时她的族人们咒骂着、狂怒着，赌咒说那是克鲁默克的手笔。“国王身边是无赖当道。”


===Original===
If knaves ruled about him, the King was not a man that one need trouble much over. Her own menfolk, she knew, had made and unmade Kings. So that, when she thought of the hosts of saints and of the blessed angels that hovered, wringing their hands and weeping above England, she had wondered a little at times why they had never unmade this King.

But to her all these things had seemed very far away. She had nothing to do but to read books in the learned tongues, to imagine herself holding disquisitions upon the spiritual republic of Plato, to ride, to shoot with the bow, to do needlework, or to chide the maids. Her cousin had loved her passionately; it was true that once, when she had had nothing to her back, he had sold a farm to buy her a gown. But he had menaced her with his knife till she was weary, and the ways of men were troublesome to her; nevertheless she submitted to them with a patient wisdom.

She submitted to the King; she submitted﻿—though she hated him by repute﻿—to Cromwell’s catechism as they followed the King at a decent interval.

===Chinese===
倘若国王身边真是无赖当道，那国王本人也犯不着谁为他多操心了。她知道，她自己族人就曾立王，也曾废王。因此，每逢想到那成群结队的圣徒与蒙福的天使盘旋于英格兰上空、绞着手哭泣，她有时会略感纳罕：他们怎么从来不曾废掉这一个国王。

但这一切在她看来都远得很。她无非读读雅言典籍，想象自己纵谈柏拉图（Plato）那灵魂的共和国，骑马，射箭，做针线，或是训斥侍女。她表兄爱她爱得炽烈；确有其事：有一回她身上竟没有一件像样的衣裳，他卖掉一座田庄，给她买了件袍裙。只是他也拿刀子威吓过她，威吓得她心生厌倦；男人的行径于她是桩烦难事；然而她以一份坚忍的明智对他们屈从。

她屈从于国王；也屈从于——尽管她久闻其名、心生憎恶——克伦威尔的要理问答，两人隔着一段得体的距离跟在国王身后。


===Original===
He walked beside her with his eyes on her face. He spoke of the King’s bounty in a voice that implied his own power. She was to be the Lady Mary’s woman. He had that lady especially in his good will, he saved for her household ladies of egregious gifts, presence and attainments. They received liberal honorariums, seven dresses by the year, vails, presents, perfumes from the King’s own still-rooms, and a parcel-gilt chain at the New Year. The Lady Rochford, who ruled over these ladies, was kind, courteous, free in her graces as in the liberties she allowed the ladies under her easy charge.

He enlarged upon this picture as if it were a bribe that he alone could offer or withhold. And something at once cautious and priestly in his tone let her quick intuition know that he was both warning her and sounding her, to see how far her mutinous spirit would carry her. Once he said, “There must be tranquillity in the kingdom. The times are very evil!”

She had felt very quickly that insults to this man would be a useless folly. He could not even feel them, and she kept her eyes on the ground and listened to him.

===Chinese===
他走在她身旁，目光盯着她的脸。他以一种暗含自身权势的声调，谈起国王的恩泽。她将去做玛丽公主的女官。那位公主尤其在他的眷顾之中；他为她的内廷延揽才艺、仪容、造诣俱出众的女官。她们领着丰厚的酬俸，一年七套衣裳，外加赏钱、礼品、御用蒸馏房所制的香露，新年还有一条局部鎏金的链子。统管这些女官的罗奇福德夫人（the Lady Rochford）和气、周到，施恩大方，对麾下女官们容许的自由也大方——她的管教本就宽松。

他把这幅图景愈描愈大，仿佛那是一注只有他一人能给能收的贿赂。他语调里那种既谨慎又带教士气的东西，让她敏捷的直觉明白：他既在警告她，又在试探她，要看看她那桀骜的心性能把她带出多远。有一回他说：“王国之内必得安宁。时世实在险恶！”

他继续试探她。凡与谁交谈，便要洞悉谁心底的秘密——这是他帝王术这一行的本分。


===Original===
He went on sounding her. It was part of his profession of kingcraft to know the secret hearts of every person with whom he spoke.

“And your goodly cousin?” He paused. The King had commanded that a place should be found for him. “Should he be best at Calais? There shall be blows struck there.”

She knew very well that he was trying to discover how much she loved her cousin, and she answered in a low voice, “I would have him stay here. He is the sole friend I have in this place.”

Cromwell said, with a hidden and encouraging meaning, her cousin was not her only friend there.

“Aye, but your lordship is not so old a friend as he.”

===Chinese===
“那么，您那位俊俏的表兄呢？”他顿了一顿。国王已下旨要给他安插一个位置。“他去了加来可好？那边要动刀兵。”

她心里明白得很，他是要探出她爱表兄爱到几分，便低声答道：“我要他留在此地。在这地方，他是我唯一的朋友。”

克伦威尔说，话里藏着一个暗中鼓舞的意味：她表兄并非她在那里唯一的朋友。

“是了，可大人您终不如他那般旧交。”

“不敢当。称我一声您忠心的仆人吧。”


===Original===
“Not me. Call me your good servant.”

“There is even then my uncle.”

“Little good of a friend you will have of Norfolk. ’Tis a bitter apple and a very rotten plank to lean upon.”

She could not any longer miss his meaning. The King’s scarlet and immense figure was already in the grey shadow of the arch under the tower. In walking, they had come near him, and while they waited he stood for a minute, gazing back down the path with boding and pathetic eyes; then he disappeared.

She looked at Cromwell and thanked him for the warning, “*quia spicula praevisa minus laedunt*.”

“I would have you read it: *gaudia plus laetificant*,” he answered gravely.

===Chinese===
“那便还有我叔祖呢。”

“从诺福克那里您得不着什么朋友的益处。那是一只苦苹果，一根烂透了的、倚不得的木头。”

她再不会错解他的意思了。国王那腥红而庞大的身影，已经没入塔下楼拱的灰色阴影里。一路走来，他们已离他近了；他们等候之际，他伫立了片刻，以不祥而哀楚的目光回望来路；随后他便不见了。

她望着克伦威尔，为这一番告诫道谢：“*quia spicula praevisa minus laedunt*”〔预见的箭，伤人较轻〕。

“我劝您把它续读下去：*gaudia plus laetificant*〔喜悦之事，更增欢愉〕。”他郑重地答道。

一个手擎螺形号角的汉子突然在拱道下吹起号来，七声空洞而嗡嗡回荡的浊响，盖过了他的话音。水门那边应来一声清亮的呼哨。


===Original===
A man with a conch-shaped horn upturned was suddenly blowing beneath the archway seven hollow and reverberating grunts of sound that drowned his voice. A clear answering whistle came from the water-gate. Cromwell stayed, listening attentively; another stood forward to blow four blasts, another six, another three. Each time the whistle answered. They were the great officers’ signals for their barges that the men blew, and the whistle signified that these lay at readiness in the tideway. A bustle of men running, calling, and making pennons ready, began beyond the archway in the quadrangle.

Cromwell’s face grew calm and contented; the King was sending to meet Anne of Cleves.

“Y’ are well read?” he asked her slowly.

“I was brought up in the Latin tongue or ever I had the English,” she answered. “I had a good master, one that spoke the learned language always.”

“Aye, Nicholas Udal,” Cromwell said.

“You know all men in the land,” she said, with fear and surprise.

===Chinese===
克伦威尔驻足谛听；又一人上前吹了四声，再一人六声，再一人三声。每一回那呼哨都有应答。这些人吹的是大员们召唤自家游舫的信号，呼哨则表示船只整装待发、正泊在潮水道上。拱道之外的四方院里，顿时起了一片奔走、呼喝、备旗的忙乱。

克伦威尔的脸色变得安详而满足；国王正遣人去迎克利维斯的安妮。

“您读的书多吧？”他缓缓问她。

“我学拉丁文在先，会英文还在后头。”她答道，“我有过一位好业师，一位永远说着雅言的人。”

“是了，尼古拉斯·尤达尔。”克伦威尔说。

“全国的人您都识得。”她说，又惧又讶。


===Original===
“I had him to master for the Lady Mary, since he is well disposed.”

“ ’Tis an arrant knave tho’ the best of pedagogues,” she answered. “He was cast out of his mastership at Eton for being a rogue.”

“For that, the worshipful your father had him to master,” he said ironically.

“No, for that he was a ruined man, and taught for his victuals. We welly starved at home, my sisters and I.”

He said slowly:

“The better need that you should grow beloved here.”

===Chinese===
“我延请他去给玛丽公主当业师，因为他心思向善。”

“他是个十足的恶棍，虽说做塾师是头一流的。”她答道，“他当年在伊顿就因为无赖行径被撵掉了教职。”

“正因如此，可敬的令尊才请他去当家塾先生。”他语带讥诮地说。

“不，那是因为他破了产，教书只为糊口。我们在家里差点饿死，我和我妹妹们。”

他缓缓说道：

“如此，您就愈发需要在此地博得人爱了。”


===Original===
Standing there, before the bushes where no ears could overhear, he put to her more questions. She had some Greek, more than a little French, she could judge a good song, she could turn a verse in Latin or the vulgar tongue. She professed to be able to ride well, to be conversant with the terms of venery, to shoot with the bow, and to have studied the Fathers of the Church.

“These things are well liked in high places,” he said. “His Highness’ self speaks five tongues, loveth a nimble answer, and is a noble huntsman.” He surveyed her as if she were a horse he were pricing. “But I doubt not you have appraised yourself passing well,” he uttered.

“I have had some to make me pleasant speeches,” she answered, “but too many cannot be had.”

“See you,” he said slowly, “these tuckets that they blow from the gate signify that the new Queen cometh with a great state.” He bit his under lip and looked at her meaningly. “But a great state ensueth a great heaviness to the head of the State. *Principis hymen, principium gravitatis.*﻿ ﻿… ’Tis a small matter to me; you may make it a great one to your ladyship’s light fortunes.”

She knew that he awaited her saying:

===Chinese===
他站在那里，在无从偷听的树丛前，又向她发问。她懂些希腊文，法文也不止一点，能鉴赏好歌，能用拉丁文或本国语（the vulgar tongue）赋诗。她自陈骑术颇佳，熟谙狩猎的行话，会射箭，还研读过教父们的著作。

“这些本事在上头甚得青眼。”他说，“陛下本人便说五种语言，喜欢机敏的答对，还是位高明的猎手。”他打量她，仿佛在估一匹马的价钱。“不过您给自己的估价，笃定也极高。”他吐出这句。

“也曾有人对我说过些中听的话，”她答道，“不过这种话再多也不嫌多。”

“您瞧，”他缓缓地说，“这些从门楼吹起的号声，表示新王后正以盛大仪仗驾临。”他咬着下唇，意味深长地看她。“然而仪仗愈盛，压在国家之首上的分量也愈重。*Principis hymen, principium gravitatis.*〔君王之婚，乃庄重之始——亦是沉重之始。〕……这于我不过小事一桩；到了夫人您这份轻飘飘的福分上，它却可能成一件大事。”

她知道他在等着她说：


===Original===
“I do not take your lordship,” and she pulled the hood further over her face because it was cold, and uttered the words with her eyes on the ground.

“Why,” he said readily, “you are a lady having gifts that are much in favour in these days. Be careful to use those gifts and no others. Meddle in nothing that does not concern you. So you may make a great marriage with some lord in favour. But meddle in naught else!”

She would find many to set her an evil example. The other ladies amongst whom she was going were a mutinous knot. Let her be careful! If by her good behaviour she earned it, he would put the King in mind to advance her. If by good speeches and good example﻿—since she had great store of learning﻿—she could turn the hearts of these wicked ladies; if she could report to him evil designs or plots, he would speak to the King in such wise that His Highness should give her a great dower and any lord would marry her. Or he would advance her cousin so that he should become marriageable.

She said submissively:

“Your lordship would have me become a spy upon the ladies who shall be my fellows?”

===Chinese===
“我不明白大人的意思。”因为天冷，她把兜帽又往下拉了拉，眼睛望着地面，吐出了这句话。

“咳，”他应对如流，“您是一位身怀才艺的淑媛，而这些才艺如今正蒙恩宠。留心只用这些才艺，别的莫用。凡与您无干的事，一概莫插手。这样，您或许能同某位得宠的勋贵结一门好亲。别的可一概莫管！”

她将发现，许多人会给她作出恶榜样。她将去相与的那些女官，是一伙不安分的人。她可得当心！倘若她的好品行挣得这份回报，他便会提醒国王提拔她。倘若她凭着好言语、好榜样——既然她满腹学问——能扭转那些恶毒女官的心；倘若她能向他密报恶谋或奸计，他自会向国王进言，让陛下赐她一份厚厚的妆奁，任何勋贵都会娶她。再不然，他就提拔她的表兄，让他够得上论婚娶。

她恭顺地说：

“大人是要我做奸细，窥伺那些日后与我同列的女官么？”


===Original===
He waved his hand with a large and calming gesture.

“I would have you work for the good of the State as you find it,” he said gravely. “That, too, is a doctrine of the Ancients.” He cited the case of Seneca, who supported the government of Nero, and she noted that he twisted to suit his purpose Tacitus’ account of the soldiers of that same Prince.

Nevertheless, she made no comment. For she knew that it is the nature of men calmly to ask hateful sacrifices of women. But her throat ached with rage. And when she followed him along the corridors of the palace she seemed to feel that each man, each woman that they passed hated that lord with a hatred born of fear.

He walked in front of her arrogantly, as if she were a straw to be drawn along in the wind of his progress. Doors flew open at a flick of his finger.

===Chinese===
他挥一挥手，姿态宽弘而使人安定。

“我要您就着眼下的国情，为国家的福祉效力。”他郑重地说，“这也正是古人的教义。”他引了塞内加辅弼尼禄（Nero）政局的先例为证；她又察觉，他把塔西佗（Tacitus）记那位皇帝麾下军士的文字，也随心剪裁、为己所用了。

然而她未置一词。因为她知道，从容不迫地向女人索取可憎的牺牲，本是男人的天性。只是她喉咙里气得发疼。当她跟随他穿过宫殿的长廊，她仿佛觉得，他们经过的每一个男人、每一个女人，都以一种生于恐惧的仇恨，恨着那位大人。

他傲然走在她前头，仿佛她只是他行进所带起的风里卷着的一根草。他手指一弹，门扇便纷纷洞开。


===Original===
Suddenly they were in a tall room, long, and dim because it faced the north. It seemed an empty cavern, but there were in it many books upon a long table and at the far end, so that they looked quite small, two figures stood before a reading-pulpit. The voice of the serving man who had thrown open the door made the words “The Lord Privy Seal of England” echo mournfully along the gilded and dim rafters of the ceiling.

Cromwell hastened over the smooth, cold floor. The woman’s figure in black, the long tail of her hood falling almost to her feet like a widow’s veil, turned from the pulpit; a man remained bent down at his reading.

“*Annuntio vobis gaudium magnum*,” Cromwell’s voice uttered. The lady stood, rigid and straight, her hands clasped before her. Her face, pale so that not even a touch of red showed above the cheekbones and hardly any in the tightly-pursed lips, was as if framed in her black hood that fastened beneath the chin. The high, narrow forehead had the hair tightly drawn back so that none was visible, and the coif that showed beneath the hood was white, like a nun’s; the temples were hollowed so that she looked careworn inexpressibly, and her lips had hard lines around them. Above her head all sounds in that dim room seemed to whisper for a long time among the rafters as if here dwelt something mysterious, sepulchral, a great grief or a great passion.

===Chinese===
倏忽之间，他们已置身一间高敞的屋子里，长而幽暗，因为它朝北。屋子像一座空洞的石窟，但里面一张长桌上堆着许多书；在屋子最尽头——望去已颇渺小——两个人影立在一座书台前。那个推开门的仆役一声唱名，让“英格兰御玺大臣”几个字悲哀地回荡在天花板镀金而昏暗的椽木之间。

克伦威尔急急掠过平滑而冰冷的地面。那着黑衣的女人身影——兜帽的长尾几乎垂到脚边，像寡妇的面纱——从书台前转过身来；一个男人仍旧伏着他的书。

“*Annuntio vobis gaudium magnum*”〔我向诸位宣告一个大喜讯〕，克伦威尔的嗓音诵道。那位夫人站得僵直，双手交握在身前。她的脸苍白至极——颧骨之上不见一丝血色，紧抿的双唇里也几乎没有——衬着在颏下系拢的黑兜帽，宛如嵌进画框里。高而窄的额头把头发紧紧勒回，一根也不外露；兜帽底下露出的那顶布帽是白色的，像修女的一般；两鬓凹陷，使她显得忧劳不可言喻，唇边刻着硬冷的细纹。在她头顶之上，那幽暗屋子里的一切声响，仿佛都在椽木间久久低语，仿佛这里栖居着某种神秘的、坟墓般的东西——一场大悲恸，或一段大激情。


===Original===
“I announce to you a master-joy,” Cromwell was saying. “I bring your La’ship a damsel of great erudition and knowledge of good letters.”

His voice was playful and full; his back was bent supply. His face lit up with a debonnaire and pleasant smile. The lady’s eyes turned upon the girl, forbidding and suspicious; she remained motionless, even her lips did not move. Cromwell said that this was a Katharine of the Howards, and one fit to aid her Ladyship and Magister Udal with their erudite commentary of Plautus his works.

The man at the reading desk looked round and then back at his book. His pen scratched upon the margin of a great volume. Katharine Howard was upon her knees grasping at the lady’s hand to kiss it. But it was snatched roughly away.

===Chinese===
“我向您宣告一桩头等的喜事，”克伦威尔正说着，“我给夫人带来一位姑娘，饱有学问，通晓文墨。”

他的嗓音轻快而洪亮；背脊柔顺地弓着。他脸上焕出一副潇洒可亲的微笑。夫人的目光转向这姑娘，冷峻而多疑；她一动不动，连嘴唇也不曾动一下。克伦威尔说，这是霍华德家的一位凯瑟琳，足以襄助夫人您与尤达尔先生，共成那部普劳图斯剧作的博注。

书案前的那男人回望了一眼，又回到他的书上。他的笔在一巨册的页边沙沙刮着。凯瑟琳·霍华德已双膝跪倒，伸手去捧夫人的手行吻礼。那只手却被粗暴地抽走了。


===Original===
“This is a folly,” the voice came harshly from the pursed lips. “Get up, wench.” Katharine remained kneeling. For this was the Lady Mary of England﻿—a martyr for whom she had prayed nightly since she could pray.

“Get up, fool,” the voice said above her head. “It is proclaimed treason to kneel to me. This is to risk your neck to act thus before Privy Seal.”

The hard words were aimed straight at the face of Cromwell.

===Chinese===
“这是犯傻。”那嗓音从紧抿的唇间生硬地迸出。“起来，丫头。”凯瑟琳仍旧跪着。因为这就是英格兰的玛丽公主——一位殉道者，她自打会祈祷起，夜夜为她祈祷。

“起来，蠢货。”那嗓音在她头顶上说。“朝廷已有明令：向我下跪即是叛国。在御玺大臣面前这等作为，是拿你的脖颈去冒险。”

这些硬邦邦的话，直冲着克伦威尔的脸掷去。


===Original===
“Your ladyship knows well I would fain have it otherwise,” he answered softly.

“I do not ask it,” she answered.

He maintained a gentle smile of deprecation, beckoning a little with his head and with his eyes, begging her for private conversation. She lifted Katharine roughly to her feet and followed him to a distant window. She seemed as if she were an automaton without will or independent motions of her own, so small were her steps and her feet so hidden beneath her stiff black skirts. He began talking to her in a voice of which only the persuasive higher notes came into the room.

===Chinese===
“夫人深知，我巴不得事情另有个样子。”他轻声答道。

“我并未乞求。”她答道。

他脸上维持着一副歉然的温和微笑，头和眼睛微微示意，恳请她私下相谈。她把凯瑟琳一把拽起，随他走到远处一扇窗前。她看上去像一具没有意志、没有自主动作的自动人偶——她的步子那么小，双脚又全然掩在僵硬的黑裙底下。他开始对她说话，那嗓音里只有劝诱性的高音，飘进屋内。


===Original===
At that time she was still proclaimed bastard, and her name was erased from the list of those it was lawful to pray for in the churches. At times she endured great hardships, even to going short of food, for she suffered from a wasting complaint that made her a great eater. But starvation could not make her submit to the King, her father, or to the Lord Cromwell who was ruler in the land. Sometimes they gave her a great train, strove to make her dress herself richly, and dragged her to such festivals as this of the marriage with Anne of Cleves. This was done when the Lord Privy Seal dangled her before the eyes of the Emperor of France as a match; then it was necessary to increase the appearance of her worth in England. But sometimes the King, out of a warm and generous feeling of satisfaction with his young son, was moved to behave bountifully to his daughter, and, seeking to dazzle her with his munificence, gave her golden crosses and learned books annotated with his own hand, richly jewelled and with embroidered covers. Or when the Emperor, her cousin, interceded that she should be treated more kindly, she was threatened with the block. Of late Cromwell had set himself to gain her heart with his intrigue that he could make so smooth and with his air that could be so gentle﻿—that the King found so lovable. But nothing moved her to set her hand to a deed countenancing her dead mother’s disgrace; to smile upon her father and his minister, who had devised the means for casting down her mother; or to consent to relinquish her right to the throne. So that at times, when the cloud of the Church abroad, and of the rebellions all over the extremities of the kingdoms, threatened very greatly, the King was driven to agonies of fear and rage lest his enemies or his subjects should displace him who was excommunicated and set her, whom all Catholics regarded as undergoing a martyrdom, on his throne. He feared her sometimes so much that it was only Cromwell that saved her from death. Cromwell would spend hours of his busy days in the long window of her work room, urging her to submission, dilating upon the powers that might be hers, studying her tastes to devise bribes for her. It was with that aim, because her whole days in her solitude were given to the learned writers, that he had sought out for her Magister Udal as a companion and preceptor who might both please her with his erudition and induce her to look kindly upon the New Learning and a more lax habit of mind. But she never thanked Cromwell. Whilst he talked she remained frozen and silent. At times, under the spur of a cold rage, she said harsh things of himself and her father, calling upon the memory of her mother and the wrongs her Church had suffered﻿—and, on his departing, before he had even left the room she would return, frigidly and without change of face, to the book upon her desk.

===Chinese===
那时候，她仍被明令斥为私生女，她的名字也已从教堂里合法代祷的名录上勾去。她时时忍受极大的困苦，甚至到了短少口粮的地步——她患着一种消耗之症，偏偏使她食量极大。然而饥饿也不能使她屈从国王——她的父亲——或屈从执掌这片国土的克伦威尔大人。有时他们给她大批扈从，极力劝她穿得富丽，把她拖到诸如这回克利维斯的安妮大婚一类的庆典上去。这是御玺大臣拿她在法兰西皇帝（the Emperor of France）眼前招摇、充作议亲筹码时的手笔；那时节，便有必要抬高她在英格兰的身价。但有时国王出于对幼子的温馨得意、心怀慷慨，也会动念厚待女儿，想以自己的豪赐使她目眩，便给她金十字架和亲笔批注的学术书籍——宝饰累累，封面刺绣。或是当她的表亲皇帝出为说项、请朝廷厚待她时，她便受到断头台的威胁。近来克伦威尔打定主意要赢得她的心：凭他那一手做得极圆熟的权术，凭他那一副装得极温存的神气——正是国王觉得可爱的那副神气。但无论什么都不能使她动笔签署任何认可亡母之辱的文书；不能使她对父亲和他的大臣展颜——正是这两人设下倾覆她母亲的手段；也不能使她同意放弃她对王位的权利。以致有时，当海外教会的阴云与王国四陲的叛乱威胁得万分凶险，国王便被驱入恐惧与狂怒交织的极苦，唯恐他的仇敌或臣民推翻他这个遭绝罚的人，把她——所有旧教徒都视为正在受难的殉道者——扶上他的王座。他有时怕她怕得厉害，全靠克伦威尔才保得她一命。克伦威尔肯在他繁忙的日程里耗上几个时辰，坐在她工作间的长窗畔，劝她归顺，铺陈她有朝一日或可执掌的权势，揣摩她的癖好来为她度身设饵。也正是抱定这个宗旨——因为她在幽独中的整日整日都交给古典作家们——他才为她物色了尤达尔先生来作伴侣兼业师：既可拿他的博学娱她，又可诱使她对“新学”和一种更宽纵的心习生出好感。但她从不曾谢过克伦威尔。他说话时，她始终冻着一般，缄默不语。有时，受着一腔冷怒的鞭策，她会说出些关于他本人和她父亲的狠话，呼唤亡母的记忆、她的教会所受的种种冤屈——到他告退时，人还没走出屋门，她已冷淡地、面不改色地回到书桌上那册书前。


===Original===
So the Privy Seal talked to her by the window for the fiftieth time. Katharine Howard saw, before the high reading pulpit, the back of a man in the long robes of a Master of Arts. He held a pen in his hand and turned over his shoulder at her a face thin, brown, humorous and deprecatory, as if he were used to bearing chiding with philosophy.

“Magister Udal!” she uttered.

He motioned with his mouth for her to be silent, but pointed with the feather of his quill to a line of a little book that lay upon the pulpit near his elbow. She came closer to read:

“*Circumspectatrix cum oculis emisitiis!*” and written above it in a minute hand: “A spie with eyes that peer about and stick out.”

===Chinese===
御玺大臣就这样在窗边同她谈着——已是第五十次了。凯瑟琳·霍华德看见，在高高的书台前，有一个穿硕士（Master of Arts）长袍的男人的背影。他手中执笔，回头向她转过一张清瘦、黧黑、诙谐而带歉意的脸，仿佛他素来惯于以哲人之道领受呵责。

“尤达尔先生！”她失声唤道。

他努嘴示意她噤声，却拿鹅毛笔的翎毛，指着书台上一本小书里、他手肘近旁的一行字。她凑近去念：

“*Circumspectatrix cum oculis emisitiis!*”〔好一位目光四巡、眼珠凸出的窥伺妇人！〕上方另有一行蝇头小字：“一个贼眼四顾、眼球凸出的探子。”


===Original===
He pointed over his shoulder at the Lord Privy Seal.

“How poor this room is, for a King’s daughter!” she said, without much dropping her voice.

He hissed: “Hush! hush!” with an appearance of terror, and whispered, forming the words with his lips rather than uttering them: “How fared you and your house in the nonce?”

“I have read in many texts,” she answered, “to pass the heavy hours.”

He spoke then, aloud and with an admonitory air:

===Chinese===
他朝身后的御玺大臣指了指。

“这屋子何等寒酸，哪里配国王的女儿！”她说，嗓音没压低多少。

他倒抽冷气似的“嘘！嘘！”，一脸惊惶，嘴唇翕动而不出声地悄问：“这一向，您和您一家可还安好？”

“我读了不少书卷，”她答道，“好挨过这些沉重的时日。”

于是他开了腔，声音朗朗，带着训诫的口吻：


===Original===
“Never say the heavy hours﻿—for what hours are heavy that can be spent with the ancient writers for companions?”

She avoided his reproachful eyes with:

“My father’s house was burnt last month; my cousin Culpepper is in the courts below. Dear Nick Ardham, with his lute, is dead an outlaw beyond sea, and Sir Ferris was hanged at Doncaster﻿—both after last year’s rising, pray all good men that God assail them!”

Udal muttered:

“Hush, for God’s dear sake. That is treason here. There is a listener behind the hangings.”

===Chinese===
“莫说什么沉重的时日——既有古昔作家相伴的时光，哪一小时算得上沉重？”

她避开他责备的目光，道：

“我父亲的家宅上月烧掉了；表兄卡尔佩珀就在下面的院子里。亲爱的尼克·阿德姆（Nick Ardham），带着他的琉特琴，已作为亡命之徒死在海外；费里斯爵士（Sir Ferris）在唐卡斯特（Doncaster）上了绞架——都是去年叛乱之后的事。愿一切善人祈求上帝降罚于他们！”

尤达尔喃喃道：

“嘘，千万看在亲爱的上帝份上。这话在此地就是叛国。挂毯后头有耳朵。”


===Original===
He began to scrawl hastily with a dry pen that he had not time to dip in the well of ink. The shadow of the Lord Cromwell’s silent return was cast upon them both, and Katharine shivered.

He said harshly to the magister:

“I will that you write me an interlude in the vulgar tongue in three days’ time. Such a piece as being spoken by skilful players may make a sad man laugh.”

Udal said: “Well-a-day!”

===Chinese===
他慌忙拿一支来不及蘸墨的干笔涂写起来。克伦威尔大人悄然折返的影子罩上两人，凯瑟琳打了个寒噤。

他向这位先生疾言道：

“我要你三日之内，用本国语给我写一出插剧（interlude）。要写成这样一个本子：出自娴熟伶人之口，能教一个伤心人发笑。”

尤达尔道：“苦也！”


===Original===
“It shall get you advancement. I am minded the piece shall be given at my house before his Highness and the new Queen in a week.”

Udal remained silent, dejected, his head resting upon his breast.

“For,” Cromwell spoke with a raised voice, “it is well that the King be distracted of his griefs.” He went on as if he were uttering an admonition that he meant should be heeded and repeated. The times were very evil with risings, mutinies in close fortresses, schism, and the bad hearts of men. Here, therefore, he would that the King should find distraction. Such of them as had gifts should display those talents for his beguiling; such of them as had beauty should make valuable that beauty; others whose wealth could provide them with rich garments and pleasant displays should work, each man and each woman, after his sort or hers. “And I will that you report my words where either of you have resort. Who loves me shall hear it; who fears me shall take warning.”

He surveyed both Katharine and the master with a heavy and encouraging glance, having the air of offering great things if they aided him and avoided dealing with his enemies.

===Chinese===
“它会给你带来升迁。我意欲此剧于一周之内，在我府上为陛下与新王后演出。”

尤达尔垂头丧气，头抵在胸前，默然无语。

“因为，”克伦威尔提高了嗓音，“叫国王从忧烦里解脱出来，是一桩好事。”他继续说下去，仿佛在颁一道要人谨记并转述的训诫。时世险恶已极：叛乱四起，要塞哗变，教门分裂，人心浇漓。因此，他愿国王在此间寻得排遣。他们中间有才艺的，该献出才艺为他解闷；有姿色的，该令其姿色生值；其余财力足以置办华服盛饰的，也该各效其力——每个男人、每个女人，各按其分。“我还要你们两人把我的话传到你们所到之处。爱我的，自会听到它；怕我的，自会引以为戒。”

他以沉重而鼓舞的一瞥，扫过凯瑟琳与先生两人，一副只要他们相助、不与其仇敌往还，便许以大利的神气。


===Original===
The Lady Mary was gliding towards them like a cold shadow casting itself upon his warm words; she would have ignored him altogether, knowing that contempt is harder to bear than bitter speeches. But the fascination of hatred made it hard to keep aloof from her father’s instrument. He looked negligently over his shoulder and was gone before she could speak. He did not care to hear more bitter words that could make the breach between them only wider, since words once spoken are so hard to wash away, and the bringing of this bitter woman back to obedience to her father was so great a part of his religion of kingcraft. In that, when it came, there should be nothing but concord and oblivion of bitter speeches, silent loyalty, and a throne upheld, revered, and unassailable.

Udal groaned lamentably when the door closed upon him:

“I shall write to make men laugh! In the vulgar tongue! I! To gain advancement!”

The Lady Mary’s face hardly relaxed:

===Chinese===
玛丽公主向他们滑行而来，像一道冷影，罩在他那些温热的言语上；她本想全然当他不存在——她深知轻蔑比恶言更难消受。然而仇恨自有其魅惑，使她难以远离她父亲的这件工具。他漫不经心地回头一瞥，未待她开口便去了。他不愿再听更多的恶言，那只会使两人间的裂隙愈深——话一出口便再难洗刷，而把这一个刻毒的女人带回对她父亲的顺服，在他那套帝王术的教义里，又是何等要紧的一宗。功成之日，便只应有协和，有对恶言的遗忘，有无言的忠忱，和一座被撑持着、受敬畏着、无可撼动的王座。

克伦威尔出门、门扉合拢之后，尤达尔凄然长叹：

“我竟要写逗人发笑的东西！用本国语！我！为了博取升迁！”

玛丽公主的脸色丝毫不见缓和：


===Original===
“Others of us take harder usage from my lord,” she said. She addressed Katharine: “You are named after my mother. I wish you a better fate than your namesake had.” Her harsh voice dismayed Katharine, who had been prepared to worship her. She had eaten nothing since dawn, she had travelled very far and with this discouragement the pain in her arm came back. She could find no words to say, and the Lady Mary continued bitterly: “But if you love that dear name and would sojourn near me I would have you hide it. For﻿—though I care little﻿—I would yet have women about me that believe my mother to have been foully murdered.”

“I cannot easily dissemble.” Katharine found her tongue. “Where I hate I speak things disparaging.”

“That I attest to of old,” Udal commented. “But I shall be shamed before all learned doctors, if I write in the vulgar tongue.”

“Silence is ever best for me!” the Lady Mary answered her deadly. “I live in the shadows that I love.”

===Chinese===
“我们旁人受这位大人的苛待还要更重些呢。”她说。她向凯瑟琳发话：“你用的是家母的名字。愿你的命数，比你那位同名人来得要好。”她严厉的嗓音叫凯瑟琳心头发慌——她本是存着敬拜之心来的。她自破晓起粒米未进，又经长途跋涉，再受这一番挫折，臂上的疼痛又发作起来。她找不出话来回答，玛丽公主又刻毒地继续道：“但倘若你珍爱那个亲爱的名字、又愿栖身在我近旁，我劝你把它掩藏起来。因为——我虽不甚在意——我仍愿围在我身边的女人们相信：家母是惨遭毒手的。”

“我不惯作伪。”凯瑟琳这才找回了舌头，“我恨谁，便要说出贬损谁的话。”

“这一点我久已可以作证。”尤达尔插言，“可若要用本国语写作，我便要在天下博学博士面前蒙羞了。”

“沉默于我永远是上策！”玛丽公主答她的话冷得像死一般，“我活在我所爱的暗影之中。”


===Original===
“That, full surely, shall be reversed,” Katharine said loyally.

“I do not ask it,” Mary said.

“Wherefore must I write in the vulgar tongue?” Udal asked again, “Oh, Mistress of my actions and my heart, what whim is this? The King is an excellent good Latinist!”

“Too good!” the Lady Mary said bitterly. “With his learning he hath overset the Church of Christ.”

She spoke harshly to Katharine: “What reversal should give my mother her life again? Wench! Wench!﻿ ﻿…” Then she turned upon Udal indifferently:

===Chinese===
“那光景，千真万确会翻转过来的。”凯瑟琳忠贞地说。

“我并未乞求。”玛丽说。

“可我究竟为什么非用本国语写作不可？”尤达尔又问，“唉，执掌我行动、执掌我心的女君啊，这是哪一门的兴致？国王的拉丁文可是好到了家！”

“好过了头！”玛丽公主苦涩地说，“他仗着那点学问，把基督的教会都掀翻了。”

她厉声对凯瑟琳道：“怎样的翻转，能再还我母亲一条性命？丫头！丫头！……”说罢她淡然转向尤达尔：


===Original===
“God knows why this man would have you write in the vulgar tongue. But so he wills it.”

Udal groaned.

“My dinner hour is here,” the Lady Mary said. “I am very hungry. Get you to your writing and take this lady to my women.”

===Chinese===
“上帝才知道，这人为何偏要你用本国语写作。但他既这样定了。”

尤达尔呻吟了一声。

“我的用餐时辰到了，”玛丽公主说，“我很饿了。你去写你的东西吧，领这位姑娘到我的女官那里去。”

### VII / 七

===Original===
The Lady Mary’s rooms were seventeen in number; they ran the one into the other, but they could each be reached by the public corridor alongside. It was Magister Udal’s privilege, his condition being above that of serving man, to make his way through the rooms if he knew that the Lady Mary was not in one of them. These chambers were tall and gloomy; the light fell into them bluish and dismal; in one a pane was lacking in a window; in another a stool was upset before a fire that had gone out.
To traverse this cold wilderness Udal had set on his cap. He stood in front of Katharine Howard in the third room and asked:
“You are ever of the same mind towards your magister?”
“I was never of any mind towards you,” she answered. Her eyes went round the room to see how Princes were housed. The arras pictured the story of the nymph Galatea; the windows bore intertwined in red glass the cyphers H and K that stood for Katharine of Aragon. “Your broken fortunes are mended?” she asked indifferently.
He pulled a small book out of his pocket, ferreted among the leaves and then setting his eye near the page pointed out his beloved line:
“*Pauper sum, pateor, fateor, quod Di dant fero.*” Which had been translated: “I am poor, I confess; I bear it, and what the gods vouchsafe that I take”﻿—and on the broad margin of the book had written: “Cicero sayeth: That one cannot sufficiently praise them that be patient having little: And Seneca: The first measure of riches is to have things necessary﻿—and, as ensueth therefrom, to be therewith content!”
===Chinese===
玛丽公主的居室共有十七间，一间套着一间，但每一间也都能从旁边的公共走廊进去。尤达尔先生的身份高于仆役，故而有此特权：只要探知玛丽公主不在屋里，便可穿行这些房间。这些厅室高旷而幽暗，落进来的天光发蓝、惨淡；有一间窗上缺了一块玻璃；又有一间，熄了的炉火前翻倒着一只凳子。

要穿越这片寒冷的荒野，尤达尔戴上了帽子。他在第三间屋里，站到凯瑟琳·霍华德面前问道：

“汝对汝的业师，心意始终如一么？”

“我对汝从不曾有过什么心意，”她答道。她的眼睛把屋子环视一周，看看帝王家的儿女住得如何。壁毯上织着宁芙伽拉忒亚（Galatea）的故事；窗上用红玻璃交缠出 H 与 K 两个花押，那是阿拉贡的凯瑟琳（Katharine of Aragon）的名号。“汝那破碎的家运，补缀好了么？”她淡淡地问。

他从衣袋里掏出一本小书，在书页间翻寻一阵，然后把眼睛凑近纸页，指出他心爱的那一行：

“*Pauper sum, pateor, fateor, quod Di dant fero.*”〔拉丁文，意谓“我贫穷，我显露，我承认；神之所赐，我皆承受”〕这句话曾有译文：“我贫穷，我承认；我承受着，而神所垂允的，我便领受”——并且书页的宽边上还写着：“西塞罗有言：家无余财而能恬然安处者，颂之不能尽。塞内加亦有言：财富的第一重分寸，是拥有必需之物——由此而下，便是与之偕安！”


===Original===
“I will give you a text from Juvenal,” she said, “to add to these: Who writes that no man is poor unless he be worthy of ridicule.”
He winced a little.
“Nay, you are hard! The text should be read: Nothing else maketh poverty so hard to bear as that it forceth men to ridiculous shifts.﻿ ﻿… *Quam quod ridiculos esse*.﻿ ﻿…”
“Aye, magister, you are more learned even yet than I,” she said indifferently. She made a step towards the next door but he stood in front of her holding up his thin hands.
“You were my best pupil,” he said, with a hungry humility as if he mocked himself. “Poor I am, but mated to me you should live as do the Hyperboreans, in a calm and voluptuous air.”
“Aye, to hang myself of weariness, as they do,” she answered.
===Chinese===
“我且赠汝一条尤维纳利斯（Juvenal）的文句，好添在这几条后面，”她说，“他写道：人除非可笑到该受讥诮，便算不得穷。”

他微微瑟缩了一下。

“罢了，汝真严苛！这句经文该这么读：再没有别的，比贫穷逼人作可笑的窘态，更叫贫穷难挨的了。……*Quam quod ridiculos esse.*〔拉丁文残句：……莫过于令人显得可笑〕……

“是了，先生，汝到底比我更有学问，”她淡淡地说。她朝邻门挪了一步，他却拦在她面前，举起那双瘦手。

“汝曾是我最好的学生，”他说，带着一种饥渴的谦卑，仿佛在自嘲。“我固然穷，可若配了我，汝便能像希珀玻瑞亚人（Hyperboreans）那样过活，在一派安宁而耽乐的空气里。”

“是了，好教我像他们似的，闷得把自己吊死，”她答道。


===Original===
He corrected her with the version of Pliny, but she answered only: “I have a great thirst upon me.”
His eyes were humorous, despairing and excited.
“Why should a lady not love her master?” he asked. “There are examples. Know you not the old rhyme:

> “ ‘It was a lording’s daughter, the fairest one of three,
 

 Why lovèd of her master.﻿ ﻿…’ ”

“Ah, unspeakable!” she said. “You bring me examples in the vulgar tongue!”
===Chinese===
他拿普林尼（Pliny）书里的说法纠正她，她却只答一句：“我渴得很。”

他的眼色又诙谐，又绝望，又兴奋。

“贵妇何以就不该爱她的先生？”他问。“先例有的是。汝难道不知那支古谣：

> “‘那是一位勋爵的女儿，三姊妹里最美的那一个，
 
 深得她先生的欢心。……’”

“啊，不堪言状！”她说。“汝竟拿俗语的例证来给我引证！”


===Original===
“I babble for joy at seeing you and for grief at your harsh words,” he answered.
She stood waiting with a sort of haughty submissiveness.
“I would you would delay your wooing. I have been on the road since dawn with neither bit nor sup.”
He protested that he had starved more hideously than Tantalus since he had seen her last.
She gave him indifferently her cheek to kiss.
“For pity’s sake take me where I may rest,” she said, “I have a maimed arm.”
===Chinese===
“我一见着汝，欢喜得语无伦次；听汝的狠话，又悲得语无伦次，”他答道。

她站着等候，神情里一半高傲，一半驯顺。

“但愿汝把求婚的事暂且缓一缓。我自破晓便在路上，水米未沾牙。”

他申辩说，自上次见她一面以来，他饿得比坦塔罗斯（Tantalus）还要凄惨。

她淡漠地递过脸颊，由他一吻。

“发发慈悲，带我到个能歇脚的地方去吧，”她说，“我一条胳膊带着伤。”


---

===Original===
He uttered her panegyric, after a model of Tibullus, to the Lady Rochford and the seven maids of honour under that lady’s charge. He was set upon Katharine’s enjoyment, and he invented a lie that the King had commanded a dress to be found for her to attend at the revels that night. The maids were already dressing themselves. Two of them were fairheaded, and four neither fair nor dark; but one was dark as night, and dressed all in black with a white coif, so that she resembled a magpie. Some were curling each other’s hair and others tightening stay-laces with little wheels set in their companions’ backs. Their bare shoulders were blue with the cold of the great room, and their dresses lay in heaps upon sheets that were spread about the clean floor﻿—brocades sewn with pearls, velvets that were inlaid with filagree work, indoor furs and coifs of fine lawn that were delicately edged with black thread.
The high sounds of their laughter had reached through the door, but a dead silence fell. The dark girl with a very long bust that raked back like a pigeon’s, and with dark and sparkling eyes, tittered derisively at the magister and went on slowly rubbing a perfumed ointment into the skin of her throat and shoulders.
“Shall he bring his ragged doxies here too?” she laughed. “What a taradiddle is this of Cophetua and a beggar wench.” The other maids all tittered derisively at Udal.
The Lady Rochford, warming her back close before the fire, said helplessly, “I have no dresses beyond what you see.” She was already attired in a bountiful wine-coloured velvet that was embroidered with silver wire into entwined monograms of the initials of her name. Her hood of purple made, above her ample brows, a castellated pattern resembling the gate of a drawbridge. She, being the mistress of that household, and compassionately loved by the ladies because she was so helpless, timorous, and unable to control them, they had combined to comb and perfume her and to lace her stomacher before setting about their own clothing. White-haired and with a wrinkled face, she appeared, under her rich clothes, like some will-less and pallid captive that had been gorgeously bedizened to grace a conqueror’s triumph. She was cousin to the late Queen Anne Boleyn, and the terror of her own escape, when the Queen and so many of her house had been swept away, seemed still to remain in the drawing-in of her eyes. In the mien of the youngest girls there, there could be seen a strained tenseness of lids and lips as though, in the midst of laughter, they were hearkening for distant sounds or the rustle of listeners behind the tapestry. And where a small door came into one wall they had pulled down the arras from in front of it, so that no one should enter unobserved. Lady Rochford addressed herself to Katharine with limp gestures of protest:
“God knows I would help you to a gown, but we have no more than we are granted; here are seven ladies and seven dresses. Where can another be got? The King’s Highness knoweth little of ladies’ gowns or he had never ordered one against tonight. Each of those hath taken the women seven weeks to sew.”
Udal said with a touch of anger, since it enraged him to have to invent further, as if the one lie about the King were not enough: “The Lord Privy Seal commanded very strictly this thing to be done. He is this lady’s very diligent protector. Have a care how you disoblige her.”
===Chinese===
他仿着提布卢斯（Tibullus）的一篇范式，向罗奇福德夫人（Lady Rochford）和夫人管带的七名宫廷侍女，诵了一篇称扬她的赞辞。他一门心思要叫凯瑟琳欢愉尽兴，便编下一个谎，说国王传了旨意，要为她觅一件袍子，好出席当晚的欢宴。侍女们已在梳妆。她们两个是金发，四个不金不黑；还有一个黑得像夜，通身上下黑衣，罩一顶白色束发帽，望去活像一只喜鹊。有的互相卷着头发，有的替同伴勒紧束胸的系带，小小的绞轮就嵌在她们背上。她们裸露的肩膀，在这大屋子的寒气里冻得发青；衣裙一堆堆摊在铺满洁净地板的床单上——缀珠的锦缎，錾着金银细丝的天鹅绒，屋内穿的裘皮，还有细麻纱的束发帽，帽缘用黑线锁出精致的花边。

她们尖高的笑声原本穿门而出，此刻却死一般静了下来。那黑衣少女上身颀长，像鸽子的胸脯一般向后收斜，一双黑眼莹亮有光，冲着业师嗤嗤讪笑，一面只管缓缓往脖颈和肩膀的皮肤里揉着香膏。

“他还要把他那些破衣烂衫的姘头也带到这儿来么？”她笑道。“好一出科菲图阿（Cophetua）国王配讨饭丫头的浑话。”其余侍女一齐冲着尤达尔嗤嗤讪笑。

罗奇福德夫人正贴着炉火烘背，无可奈何地说：“除了你们眼前这些，我再没有袍子了。”她已穿上一袭丰赡的酒红色天鹅绒，银线绣出她姓名起首字母交缠而成的连体花押。紫色兜帽在她丰润的眉额上方，堆出雉堞般的样式，宛如一道吊桥的闸楼。她是这一家的主妇，又因无能、胆怯、管束不住她们，反蒙众贵妇怜爱；她们先合力替她梳头熏香、束好胸衣，然后才着手打扮自己。她一头白发，满脸皱纹，在华服底下，活像一个没有意志的苍白囚俘，被盛装丽饰打扮起来，去点缀征服者的凯旋。她是故安妮·博林王后的族亲，当年王后和她族中那么多人被一网打尽，她自家脱身的那场惊惶，似乎还留在她双目紧缩的神情里。在座最年幼的几名少女，眉眼间也绷着一股紧劲儿，仿佛正在笑语之中，侧耳听着远处的声响，或壁毯背后窃听者的窸窣。有一道小门开在一面墙上，她们把门前的壁毯都掀了下来，免得有谁进来而不被察觉。罗奇福德夫人软塌塌地打着推拒的手势，向凯瑟琳进言：

“上帝知道，我何尝不想帮你弄件袍子，可我们领到的就只有这些；这儿是七位贵妇、七件袍子。再往哪儿弄一件去？国王陛下对贵妇的袍子知道得太少，否则断不会传旨在今晚之前赶备一件。那样的袍子，一件要费女裁缝们七个星期的工夫。”

尤达尔带着几分怒意说——要他接着编下去，他实在光火，仿佛扯了国王那一个谎还不够：“御玺大臣严令办妥此事。他是这位小姐异常勤谨的保护人。你们仔细着，别开罪了她。”


===Original===
The ladies rustled their slight clothing at that name, turned their backs, and looked at Katharine above their shoulders. The Lady Rochford recoiled so far that her skirts were in danger from the fire in the great hearth; her woebegone, flaccid face was suddenly drawn at the mention of Cromwell, and she appeared about to kneel at Katharine’s feet. She looked round at the figures of the girls.
“One of these can stay if your ladyship will wear her dress,” she flustered. “But who is tall enow? Cicely is too long in the shank. Bess’s shoulders are too broad. Alack! God help me! I will do what I can”﻿—and she waved her hands disconsolately.
Cold, fatigue, and her maimed arm made Katharine waver on her feet. This white-haired woman’s panic seemed to her grotesque and disgusting.
“Why, the magister lies,” she said. “I am no such friend of Privy Seal’s.”
Swift and wicked glances passed among the girls; the dark one threw back her head and laughed discordantly, like a magpie. She came with a deft and hopping step and gazed at Katharine with her head on one side.
“Old Crummock will want our teeth next to make him a new set. He may have my head, tell him. I have no need for it, it aches so since he killed my menfolk.”
===Chinese===
一听这个名号，贵妇们单薄的衣衫窸窣作响，一齐转过背去，又从肩头上头打量凯瑟琳。罗奇福德夫人直往后缩，裙裾险些扫进大壁炉的火里；一提到克伦威尔，她那张愁苦松垂的脸蓦地一抽，眼看就要跪到凯瑟琳脚下去。她环视姑娘们的身影。

“您若肯穿谁的裙子，谁就留下不走，”她慌慌张张地说。“可谁个身量够高呢？西西莉的小腿太长，贝丝（Bess）的肩膀太宽。哎呀！上帝帮帮我罢！我总要想想办法”——她灰心丧气地挥着手。

寒冷、疲乏，加上那条伤臂，使得凯瑟琳脚下发晃。在她看来，这白发妇人的惶恐，既怪诞，又可憎。

“嗐，业师扯谎，”她说。“我跟御玺大臣才没有这样的交情。”

姑娘们交换着迅疾而歹毒的眼色；那黑衣少女把头一仰，发出一声不谐调的笑，像喜鹊叫。她迈着轻巧蹦跳的步子走上前来，偏着头端详凯瑟琳。

“老克鲁默克下回该要我们的牙齿，好给他镶一口新的。告诉他，我的脑袋尽管拿去。我留着也没用，自从他杀了我家的男人，它就疼个不停。”


===Original===
Lady Rochford shuddered as if she had been struck.
“Beseech you,” she said weakly to Katharine. “Cicely Elliott is sometimes distraught. Believe not that we speak like this among ourselves.” Her eyes wandered in a flustered and piteous way over her girls and she whimpered, “Jane Gaskell, stand back to back with this lady.”
Katharine Howard cried out, “Keep your gowns for your backs and your tongues still. Woe betide the girl who calls me a gossip of Privy Seal.”
Cicely Elliott cast her dark head back and uttered one of her discordant laughs at the ceiling, and a girl, hiding behind the others, called out, “What a fine —﻿—﻿—!”
Katharine cried, “It is all lies that this fool magister utters. I will go to no masques nor revels.” She turned upon Lady Rochford, her face pallid, her lips open: “Give me water,” she said harshly. “I will get me back to my pigsties.”
Lady Rochford wrung her hands and protested that her ladyship should not repeat that they were always thus. Privy Seal should not visit it upon them.
===Chinese===
罗奇福德夫人猛一哆嗦，像挨了一击。

“求求您，”她有气无力地对凯瑟琳说。“西西莉·埃利奥特有时候神志昏乱。别以为我们私下里也说这样的话。”她的眼睛慌乱而可怜地在她的姑娘们身上逡巡，呜咽似的说：“简·加斯克尔（Jane Gaskell），跟这位小姐背贴背站直了。”

凯瑟琳·霍华德扬声道：“袍子留给你们自己的背，舌头也收进嘴里去。哪个丫头再嚼舌，说我是御玺大臣的密友，叫灾殃降到她头上。”

西西莉·埃利奥特把黑脑袋往后一甩，冲着天花板又发出一声她那不谐调的笑；一个躲在众人背后的姑娘嚷道：“好一个——的——！”

凯瑟琳嚷道：“这蠢业师说的全是谎话。假面舞会也好，欢宴也好，我一概不去。”她猛地转向罗奇福德夫人，脸色惨白，嘴唇张着：“给我水，”她声音刺耳地说。“我要回我自己的猪圈去。”

罗奇福德夫人绞着双手，只求小姐千万别声张她们平日总是这般光景；御玺大臣也断乎不可为此降罪到她们头上。


===Original===
The magister blinked upon the riot that his muddling had raised. He called out, “Be quiet. Be quiet. This lady is sick!” and stretched out his hands to hold Katharine on her feet.
Cicely Elliott cried, “God send all Crummock’s informers always sick.”
“Thou dastard!” Katharine screamed aloud. She tried to speak but she choked; she grasped Udal’s hand as if to wring from him the denial of his foolish lies, but a sharp and numbing pain shot up her maimed wrist to her shoulders and leaped across her forehead.
“Thou filthy spy,” the dark girl laughed wildly into her agonised face. “If there had never been any like thee all the dear men of my house had still breathed.”
Katharine sprang wildly towards her tormentor, but a black sheet seemed to drop across her eyes. She fell right down and screamed as her elbow struck the floor.
===Chinese===
业师直眨眼，看着自己胡搅出来的一场大乱。他喊道：“静一静。静一静。这位小姐病了！”一面伸出双手，想扶住凯瑟琳站稳。

西西莉·埃利奥特喊道：“上帝叫克鲁默克的密探们个个都病，才遂了愿。”

“你这懦夫！”凯瑟琳放声尖叫。她想说话，却噎住了；她攥住尤达尔的手，仿佛要硬逼他吐出一句翻悔，收回那些蠢谎；可一阵尖利发麻的疼痛，从伤腕直窜上肩头，又跃过她的前额。

“你这下流探子，”黑衣少女冲她那张痛楚的脸狂笑。“世上若从来不曾有你这号人，我满门亲爱的男丁便都还喘着气。”

凯瑟琳向这个折磨她的人狂扑过去，可一方黑幕仿佛当眼罩落了下来。她直挺挺栽倒，手肘磕上地板时，发出一声尖叫。

## Part II The House of Eyes / 第二部 眼睛之屋

### I / 一

===Original===
A grave and bearded man was found to cup her. He gave her a potion composed of the juice of nightshade and an infusion of churchyard moss. Her eyes grew dilated and she had evil dreams. She lay in a small chamber that was quite bare and had a broken window, and the magister ran from room to room begging for quilts to cover her.

It was nobody’s affair. The Lord Privy Seal, her uncle, the Catholics, and the King were still perturbed about Anne of Cleves, and there were no warrants signed for Katharine’s housing or food. All the palace was trembling with confusion, for, when the Queen had been upon the point of setting out from Rochester, the King was said to have been overcome by a new spasm of disgust: she was put by again.

The young Earl of Surrey, a cousin of Katharine’s, gave Udal contemptuously a couple of crowns towards her nourishment. Udal applied them to bribing Throckmorton, the spy who had been with Privy Seal upon the barge, to inscribe on his lord’s tablets the words: “Katharine Howard to be provided for.” Udal made up his courage sufficiently to speak to the Duke, whom he met in a corridor. The Duke was jaundiced against his niece, because her cousin Culpepper had fallen upon Sir Christopher Aske, the Duke’s captain who had kept the postern. It had needed seven men to master him, and this great tumult had arisen in the King’s own courtyard. Nevertheless, the Duke sent his astrologer to cast Katharine’s horoscope. He signed, too, an order that some girl be found to attend on her.

Udal filled in the girl’s name as Margot Poins, the granddaughter of old Badge, of Austin Friars. Even among these clamours his tooth watered for her, and he gave the order to young Poins to execute. The young man rode off into Bedfordshire, where his sister had been sent out of the way to the house of their aunt. He presented the order as in the nature of a writ from the Duke, and amongst Lutherans in London a heavy growl of rage went up﻿—against Norfolk, against the Papists of the Privy Council, and, above all, against Katharine Howard, whom they called the New Harlot.

Katharine, having taken much nightshade juice, was raving upon her bed. The leech became convinced that she was possessed by a demon, because the pupils of her eyes were as large as silver groats, and her hands picked at the coverlets. He ordered that thirteen priests should say an exorcism at the door of her room, and that the potion of nightshade﻿—since it might inconvenience without dislodging the fiend inhabiting her slender body﻿—should be discontinued.

Udal sought for priests, but having no money, he was disregarded by them. He ran to the chaplain of the Bishop of Winchester. For the clergy upheld or ordained by Archbishop Cranmer were held to be less efficacious in matters of witchcraft and possession. Just then Cromwell had triumphed, and Anne of Cleves was upon the water coming to the palace.

===Chinese===
人们寻来一位神情庄重、蓄着胡须的男子，给她行杯吸之术。他给她服下一剂药，用颠茄汁合着教堂墓地苔藓的浸液调制而成。她的瞳孔渐渐张大，做起凶梦来。她躺在一间四壁萧然的小屋里，窗子破了一扇；业师挨着一间间屋子奔走，讨要被子来给她盖。

这是一桩无主的事。御玺大臣、她的叔祖、教皇派诸公和国王，都还在为克利维斯的安妮心神不宁；凯瑟琳的居所饮食，没有任何手令签发。整座王宫乱得发抖：原来王后已到了要从罗切斯特动身的当口，据说国王又被一阵新的嫌恶攫住：她再度被搁下了。

年轻的萨里伯爵——凯瑟琳的一位表亲——轻蔑地丢给尤达尔两三枚克朗，权充她的调养之资。尤达尔却拿这钱去贿赂斯罗克莫顿——御舟上随侍御玺大臣的密探——求他在他家大人的记事板上写下这几个字：“凯瑟琳·霍华德，须予安置。”尤达尔又鼓足了胆量，在一条走廊里拦住公爵进言。公爵对自己的侄孙女正怀着一腔怨气，因为她的表兄卡尔佩珀袭击了克里斯托弗·阿斯克爵士——公爵手下把守后门的队长。动用了七条汉子才把他制服，这场大乱就闹在国王自家的庭院里。尽管如此，公爵还是派了他的星士去为凯瑟琳推算星命，又签署一道手令，着人寻一个女孩子来侍候她。

尤达尔在女孩一栏里填上了玛戈·普因斯的名字——奥古斯丁修士区老巴杰的孙女。纵然身处这些喧嚣之中，他对她仍垂涎不已，便把那道手令交给小普因斯去执行。年轻人纵马驰往贝德福德郡——他姐姐先前为免事端，已被打发到他们姨母的宅子里去了。他把那道手令说成是公爵签发的令状一般的东西；伦敦的路德派中间，随之响起一片沉甸甸的怒吼——冲着诺福克，冲着枢密院里的教皇派，而首当其冲的，是凯瑟琳·霍华德：他们称她作新荡妇。

凯瑟琳服了大量颠茄汁，在床上谵语连连。医师确信她被恶魔附了体，因为她的瞳孔胀得像两枚银格罗特，双手直抓挠被褥。他吩咐请十三个神父在她房门口诵驱魔经，又吩咐颠茄汁的药剂即刻停用——它既驱不出寓居她纤瘦身躯里的恶鬼，徒然折磨她罢了。

尤达尔四处寻访神父，无奈身无分文，无人理会。他便跑去求温彻斯特主教的司铎——因为克兰默大主教所扶持或授圣职的教士，人们在巫蛊附魔之事上素来认为法力不济。恰在此时，克伦威尔大功告成，克利维斯的安妮已经上船，正沿水路朝王宫而来。

===Original===
Bishop Gardiner’s chaplain, a fat man, with beady and guileless eyes sunk in under an immense forehead, imagined that Udal’s visit was a pretext for overhearing the words of rage and discomfiture that in that Papist centre might be let drop about the new Queen. For Udal, because Privy Seal had set him with the Lady Mary, passed amongst the Papists for one of Cromwell’s informants, and it amused his sardonic and fantastic nature to affect mysterious denials, which made the fiction the more firmly believed and gave to Udal himself a certain hated prestige. The chaplain answered that in the present turmoil no such body as thirteen clergymen could be found.

“But the lady shall be torn in pieces,” Udal shrieked. Panic had overcome him. Who knew that the fiend, having torn his Katharine asunder, might not enter into the body of his Margot, who was already at her bedside? His lips quivered with terror, his eyes smiled furiously, he wrung his hands. He swore he would penetrate to the King’s Highness’ self. Udal was a man who stuck at nothing to gain a point. He had heard from Katharine that the King had spoken graciously to her, and he swore once more that she was the apple of the King’s eye, as well as a beloved disciple of Privy Seal’s.

“Be sure,” he foamed, “they shall be avenged on a Gardiner and his crew if you let her die.”

The chaplain said impassively: “God forbid that we, who are loyal to his Highness, should listen to these tales you bring us of his lechery!” They had there a new Queen, their duty was to her, and to no Katharine Howard. The bishop’s clergy were all joyfully setting to welcome the lady from Cleves, they had no time to waste over a leman’s demons. It overjoyed him to refuse Privy Seal’s man a boon on the plea of loyalty to the new Queen. Nevertheless, he went straight to the presence of the bishop, and told him the marvels that Udal had reported.

“The man is incontinent and a babbler,” the chaplain said. “We may believe one tenth.”

“Well, you shall find for once how this wench is housed and where,” his master answered moodily. “God knows what we may believe in these days. Doubtless the Nuntio of Satan hath a new plot in the hatching.” Making these enquiries, the chaplain came upon the backwash of Udal’s reports that the King loved some leman. Some lady, somewhere﻿—some said a Howard, some a Rochford, some would have it a Spanish woman﻿—was being hidden up, either by the King, by the Duke of Norfolk, or by Privy Seal. God knew the truth of these things: but similar had happened before; and it was certain that the Cleves woman had been for long kept dangling at Rochester. Perhaps that was the reason. His Highness had his own ways in these matters: but where there was smoke, generally fire was to be found. The chaplain brought this budget back to Bishop Gardiner. Gardiner swore a wild oath that, by the bones of the Confessor, they had unmasked a new plot of Satan’s Legate, the Privy Seal. But, by the grace of God, he would counterplot him.

===Chinese===
加德纳主教的司铎是个胖子，一双小圆而毫不设防的眼睛，深陷在巨大的额头底下。他认定尤达尔此来是个由头，意在偷听——在那个教皇派的巢穴里，人们兴许会随口漏出些关于新王后的怨愤败兴之言。原来尤达尔既由御玺大臣安插在玛丽公主身边，教皇派便把他认作克伦威尔的密探之一；而他生性讥诮荒唐，偏乐于故作神秘地矢口否认，倒叫这番虚构越传越真，也教尤达尔自己得了个招人恨的名头。司铎回答说：眼下乱成这般，一时哪里凑得出十三个教士的班底。

“可那位小姐要被撕碎了！”尤达尔尖声叫道。惊惶已制服了他。谁知道那恶鬼撕碎了他的凯瑟琳之后，会不会钻进他的玛戈的身体里去？她此刻就在她床边侍候。他双唇吓得发颤，眼里放出狂乱的笑，双手直绞。他赌咒发誓，要一路闯到国王陛下面前去。尤达尔这个人，为达到目的什么事都做得出来。他从凯瑟琳口中听说国王曾和颜悦色地同她说过话，便又一再赌咒，说她是国王的掌上明珠，还是御玺大臣钟爱的门徒。

“记住，”他口沫横飞地喊道，“你们若让她死了，自有找加德纳和他那伙人算账的一天。”
